# Appendix: workflow digests for the feat/pin-table-cn carve-up (generated 2026-09-02)

Source: read-only 26-agent workflow wf_beb451a6-80d. Sections: per-slice maps + skeptic verdicts, CI/conflict scout, translator PR reviews, both planner proposals.

## 1. Per-slice maps and skeptic verdicts


######## SLICE ffi-fragment-rust — Item 4 — Fragment FFI Rust surface (rust/crates/sirius-sys + rust/crates/sirius bindings for sirius::ffi::Fragment/Context, plus the one generic C++ FFI fix they need)

SUMMARY: DEV already ships the whole C++ `sirius::ffi::Fragment`/`Context` surface this slice needs (#1481 merged): `make_fragment`, `stream_view_name`, `declare_input_column/sender`, `declare_output`, `declare_output_broadcast/hash_key`, `build`, `relay_from`, `close_input`, `run`, `result_to_arrow`, `output_batch_count`, `output_types` are all in `src/include/sirius_ffi.hpp` on `origin/dev`. What is missing is the Rust half: DEV's `rust/crates/sirius-sys/src/lib.rs` binds only `Context` + `execute_substrait` (61-line bridge), and `rust/crates/sirius/src/lib.rs` is 257 lines with no `Fragment` at all. This slice adds the cxx bridge for `Fragment` and the safe `sirius::Fragment<'ctx>` wrapper, moves `SiriusContext`'s `UniquePtr` behind a `RefCell` so `fragment(&self)` can hand out several concurrently-live fragments of one query, and adds `stream_view_name` so the front end emitting a stream read and the engine creating the view share one definition. It also carries the single generic C++ fix the Rust tests cannot run without: `lower_substrait` must own a transaction, because DuckDB 1.5.5 (already the DEV submodule pin, landed in #1328) throws `TransactionContext::ActiveTransaction called without active transaction` and `Fragment::build()` commits its view-creation transaction before calling it — a latent break on DEV that CI never caught because the only C++ Fragment tests exercise failure paths. Every other CN slice sits on this one: the arena slice adds `staging_*`/`StagingArena`/`PackedBatch`/`export_packed`/`push_packed` to these same two files, the cardinality slice adds `declare_input_cardinality`/`output_row_count`, and the pin-table slice adds `PinTableSpec`/`pin_table`/`unpin_table`. Without it, `experimental/starrocks/src/{engine,compute_node_service,fragment_executor,wire_type_parity}.rs` do not compile at all.

RISKS:
- Every new Rust test needs a real GPU. check.yml line 198 runs only `cargo test --no-run --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml --package sirius --package sirius-sys`, so upstream CI compiles and links them but never executes them. The PR description must say explicitly which GPU box the suite was run on and paste the pass output, or reviewers have no evidence at all.
- fragment_over_an_empty_input_stream_terminates is a hang-class test and under_watchdog calls `std::process::exit(101)` on a 120s timeout — a wedge takes the whole `cargo test` binary down, not just that case. Verify it passes on DEV+layer-1 before including it; if it needs the partition/hash-join empty-side work in src/op/sirius_physical_partition.cpp, move it to the empty-build-join slice.
- Relaxing `SiriusContext::execute_substrait` from `&mut self` to `&self` is source-compatible for callers but moves the UniquePtr behind a RefCell: a re-entrant call (execute_substrait from inside something holding the borrow) now panics with BorrowMutError at runtime instead of failing to compile. No such caller exists today, in-tree or in experimental/starrocks.
- The `lower_substrait` fix changes an already-merged path. `Context::execute_substrait` opens its own transaction, so `owned_transaction` is false and its behavior is unchanged — but that must be stated and tested, not assumed, since it is the one path with existing users.
- cxx binds `stream_view_name` as a free function returning `UniquePtr<CxxString>`, which pins the C++ signature to `std::unique_ptr<std::string>` exactly. A later 'cleanup' to return `std::string` by value compiles on the C++ side and breaks the bridge at link time with no source-level warning. Worth a comment on the C++ declaration (that is exactly the 3-line hpp doc this slice carries).
- `Fragment::into_arrow` takes `&mut self` and does not consume the fragment, so the `into_` prefix is misleading; #1598 called it `result_to_arrow`, matching the C++. Every CN slice calls `.into_arrow()` (experimental/starrocks/src/engine.rs), so renaming after this lands churns downstream branches. Decide the name in this PR.
- SOT regressed the doc comments #1598 shipped on the bridge (Fragment usage order, broadcast/hash_key exclusivity, build's two rejection rules, relay_from's preconditions). If the new PR copies SOT verbatim it silently ships worse docs than the draft it replaces.

DESCRIPTION OUTLINE:
**Motivation.** #1481 merged the C++ `sirius::ffi::Fragment`: a Sirius process can already run one fragment of a multi-fragment query, park its output as native GPU batches, and hand them to the next fragment with no Arrow and no file in between. Nothing outside C++ can reach it. The StarRocks compute node — one CN per GPU, which is how TPC-H Q1 and Q6 get to run across many GPUs — is Rust, and `rust/crates/sirius` today binds exactly one call: `Context::execute_substrait`. This PR binds the fragment surface so a Rust front end can declare streams, build a plan against them, relay between fragments, and drain the result. It is the foundation the compute-node PRs stack on; without it none of them compile.

**What changed.**
1. `rust/crates/sirius-sys`: the cxx bridge grows `type Fragment`, `make_fragment`, `stream_view_name`, and the fragment methods `declare_input_column`, `declare_input_sender`, `declare_output`, `declare_output_broadcast`, `declare_output_hash_key`, `build`, `relay_from`, `close_input`, `run`, `result_to_arrow` (unsafe: the caller supplies the `ArrowArrayStream` address), `output_batch_count`, `output_types`. Every symbol already exists in `src/include/sirius_ffi.hpp` on dev — this adds no C++ API.
2. `rust/crates/sirius`: safe `Fragment<'ctx>`, whose lifetime parameter enforces the one rule the C++ side cannot (a fragment must not outlive its engine), and `SiriusContext::fragment(&self)`. The context's `UniquePtr` moves behind a `RefCell` because several fragments of one query are alive simultaneously — senders parked, waiting for their receiver — and a `&mut self` factory would allow exactly one. `stream_view_name` is re-exported so a front end emitting the stream read and the engine creating the view read the convention from one definition.
3. `src/sirius_ffi.cpp`: `lower_substrait` now owns a transaction if the caller has not opened one. DuckDB 1.5.5 (our submodule pin since #1328) throws `TransactionContext::ActiveTransaction called without active transaction` from every catalog lookup, and `Fragment::build()` commits its view-creation transaction before lowering — so today *every* result-fragment build on dev throws. It is latent because the two existing C++ fragment cases only cover build failures. `Context::execute_substrait` already begins its own transaction, so that path is untouched.

**How verified.** New Rust tests, all GPU: a broadcast sender's two outputs each deliver the full result to their own receiver; two independently-built hash senders over the same 20k keys with different row-group boundaries assign every key to the same stream (INT64 and DECIMAL(15,2) — the q10 shuffle-key shape) and their partitions union to the whole input; a fragment over an input stream that ends without ever carrying a batch terminates instead of waiting forever, under a watchdog; and a receiver that declares `id DOUBLE` against a sender producing `BIGINT` is refused at the hop, before any data moves, with the error naming both types. Plus a C++ regression case for the transaction fix. Note that CI runs `cargo test --no-run` only — these were executed on <GPU box>; output attached.

**Not handled here, on purpose.** The exchange staging arena and packed cross-node transport (`staging_*`, `StagingArena`, `PackedBatch`, `export_packed`/`push_packed`), declared stream cardinality for the optimizer (`declare_input_cardinality`/`output_row_count`), `pin_table`/`unpin_table` over the FFI, and byte-ranged parquet splits each land in their own PR on top of this one. The empty-build-side join fix (`sirius_physical_hash_join`/`sirius_physical_partition`) is separate too, which is why the inner-join-over-an-empty-build-stream test is not in this PR.

OPEN QUESTIONS:
- Does the `SIRIUS_LOG_BACKEND`/`SIRIUS_LOG_DIR`/`SIRIUS_LOG_LEVEL` block in `Context::Impl::bring_up` (SOT src/sirius_ffi.cpp, ~16 lines + `#include "config.hpp"`) belong here? It is genuinely generic — the extension path installs the log sink from SiriusContextExtensionCallback's ctor, which the FFI path never constructs, so SIRIUS_LOG_* is dead for every embedder and engine-side stalls are invisible on a compute node. But it mutates DuckDB's `Config::LOG_*` statics, which is a different concern from binding Fragment. Fold into layer 1, or make it a fourth ~20-line PR?

- Should `fragment_over_an_empty_input_stream_terminates` ship here or in the empty-build-join slice? Needs one GPU run against dev + the transaction fix to answer. It is a pure `Read stream_0` with no join, so it should not touch the partition/hash-join code — but if it wedges, `under_watchdog` exits the whole test process, so it is not a cheap thing to get wrong.

The crate is `publish = false`, and the only in-tree consumers are experimental/starrocks (which wants `&self`) and the crate's own tests, so this looks free — worth one sentence in the PR body rather than an assumption.

######## SLICE byte-range-splits — Parquet byte-range splits (engine rule → ingestible → Substrait/FFI/planner → StarRocks CN emission)

SUMMARY: A StarRocks FE never hands a worker "read lineitem.parquet" — it stats each file and chops it into TBrokerRangeDesc splits (bytes 0..512MB to instance 0, 512MB..1GB to instance 1). Until this slice, `ScanFilePaths::check_range` refused any range that was not provably the whole file ("byte-range split scan ranges are not supported"), so a multi-CN FILES() query over one large parquet simply failed to translate — the dominant blocker in the TPC-H survey (18/22 queries). This slice carries a split end to end: a deterministic byte-range→row-group ownership rule (`row_group_start_offset` / `row_groups_in_byte_range`) matching the StarRocks BE reader convention, threaded into `parquet_gpu_ingestible::build_file_scan_info` before stats pruning; ranges riding `FileOrFiles.start/length` through a per-plan `ClientContextState` because DuckDB's Substrait consumer is a submodule and `parquet_scan` has no byte-range parameter; and the translator's `resolve_ranges` replacing the whole-file-only gate. The single correctness property everything serves: N splits of one file read every row exactly once, deterministic under any placement. That is precisely what makes Q1/Q6 fan out to one CN per GPU without pinning — with N GPUs and one lineitem file, no splits means no parallel scan at all. It is also the alternative to (and complement of) the FE `files_query_whole_file_ranges` patch on the pin path: the pin path deliberately turns splits off, this path makes them work.

SIZE~1450  PR TITLE: feat(scan): byte-range parquet splits, engine to StarRocks CN

RISKS:
- L1/L2 merged without L3/L4 are dead code in production (nothing sets `resolved_file_ranges`). That is deliberate and is what makes them safely reviewable, but a reviewer may object to merging unreachable code — the PR description must say the stack is landing bottom-up in the same session and name the follow-up PR.
- L4 without L1-L3 is the one genuinely dangerous ordering: the CN would emit `FileOrFiles.start/length` into an engine that discards them, and every one of N splits would read the whole file — N-times row duplication with no error. Enforce merge order in the gh stack; do not let L4 be cherry-picked.
- `has_byte_ranges` (the pin-cache guard in L2) has NO C++ test on SOT — grep confirms it is referenced only from `cache_entry_info::from` and `can_serve_with_columns`. A reviewer will ask for one; a 15-line case in test/cpp/scan/test_can_serve_with_columns.cpp (ranged scan vs whole-file pin, both directions) closes it. Note that file is otherwise entirely pin-slice, so adding a case there creates a light textual overlap with the pin PR.
- `extract_scan_byte_ranges` re-parses the plan bytes a second time (SubstraitToDuckDB already parsed them). Cost is per-plan, not per-row, and only when the plan carries ranges — but call it out explicitly, because `lower_substrait` is on the Fragment::build hot path in multi-CN runs where a fragment is built per query per CN.
- `collect_from_rel`'s throw-on-unknown-`Rel` type is a fail-closed choice that will turn any future Substrait rel type Sirius starts accepting into a hard error at lowering time until someone adds a case. That is the intended trade (a skipped rel could hide a ranged read), but it is a maintenance tripwire worth naming in the PR body.
- `scan_byte_ranges_state::claim` is single-shot per file, so two scans in one plan reading ranges of the same file throw. Legitimate today (the CN never emits that shape), but it is a hard refusal, not a degradation — say so.
- The Rust test `byte_range_splits_read_every_row_exactly_once` requires a GPU, so upstream CI will not run it; only the Catch2 `[parquet_byte_range][scan]` case (L2) exercises the selection path in CI, and even that needs a GPU build. The pure-rule L1 cases and all of L4's translate.rs tests are the CI-visible coverage.
- Cross-slice coupling to flag: the staging-arena slice's Rust test `zero_row_export_is_metadata_only_and_holds_no_lease` uses `local_files_plan_ranged` to manufacture a zero-row batch (a range strictly inside one row group). If the arena PR lands before this one it must inline its own helper or the two PRs must be ordered.

DESCRIPTION OUTLINE:
MOTIVATION (state it in the first paragraph of every layer). The StarRocks FE does not hand a worker "read lineitem.parquet". It stats each file and chops it into TBrokerRangeDesc splits so several workers scan in parallel — bytes 0..512MB to one instance, 512MB..1GB to the next. Its greedy least-loaded assignment cuts a split whenever a file would overflow bytesPerInstance, so a distributed FILES() query essentially always carries byte ranges. Until this stack, `ScanFilePaths::check_range` refused any range not provably the whole file ("byte-range split scan ranges are not supported") because DuckDB's `local_files` reader could not carry an offset. That refusal is the dominant blocker in the TPC-H survey (18/22 queries) and it is what stops TPC-H Q1 and Q6 from fanning out to one CN per GPU: with N GPUs and one lineitem file, no splits means no parallel scan at all.

THE SINGLE CORRECTNESS PROPERTY everything below serves: N splits of one file read every row EXACTLY ONCE, deterministic under any placement. Repeat it at the top of each layer.

WHAT CHANGED, per layer.
L1 — the ownership rule, wired to nothing. `row_group_start_offset` = min of the first column chunk's data/index/dictionary page offsets and the row group's `file_offset`, each counting only when present. This is the StarRocks BE reader convention (be/src/formats/parquet/utils.cpp) that the FE load-balances against — correctness of a distributed scan reduces to every reader deriving the SAME owner for every row group. cudf's thrift structs carry no `__isset`, so a zero page offset is treated as absent (real offsets start after the 4-byte magic); a row group with no candidate offset at all is a loud throw, never a guess. A range owns a row group iff it contains its start: a straddling row group belongs to the range holding its start (the byte range bounds ownership, not I/O), and a range inside one row group owns nothing — a valid empty split.
L2 — the ingestible honors a per-file range. `resolved_file_ranges` parallel to `resolved_file_paths`; the split provider hands each file's range to `build_file_scan_info`; the filter applies right after `all_row_groups` and BEFORE `filter_row_groups_with_stats`, so a range and a predicate compose. `(0,0)` is the whole file, keeping every existing plan byte-identical. An empty selection rides the existing all-pruned fallback (`set_num_rows(0)`) — never an error, never a whole-file read. A range/path count mismatch is refused at construction. The pinned-table cache learns one fact: a ranged scan holds a fraction of each file's rows, so it neither serves nor is served by the cache.
L3 — ranges ride the plan. `FileOrFiles` carries native start/length, but DuckDB's Substrait consumer is a submodule that discards them and `parquet_scan` has no byte-range parameter. So `lower_substrait` re-parses the plan bytes it already holds, collects every ranged LocalFiles item into a per-plan `ClientContextState`, and `build_parquet_table_info` claims each file's ranges when it lifts the bind data into the ingestible — expanding a file with N ranges into N entries whether or not the bind deduplicated the repeated path, since disjoint ranges make only the per-file union matter. Paths are canonicalized at insert AND lookup (`file:/p`, `file:///p`, `file://localhost/p`, plain) — not hypothetical: the first live two-CN run tripped `assert_all_consumed` on exactly that spelling mismatch.
L4 — the CN stops refusing. `resolve_ranges` normalizes each file's ranges into the ScanFiles this instance reads: `size == -1` resolves to EOF, empty intervals drop, intervals sort (the FE promises no order), OVERLAPS ARE REFUSED rather than merged (under start-offset ownership two overlapping ranges read the same row groups twice and count(*) disagrees with no error), adjacent ranges coalesce, and an exact whole-file tiling collapses to a whole-file read so every pre-split plan translates byte-identically. Two delivery shapes are now handled rather than mis-read: `has_more` is refused (more ranges follow through `deliver_scan_ranges`, which this CN does not implement; accepting the prefix would silently read a subset) and an explicitly empty placeholder range carries no file. Folds in the outer-`compression_type` refusal — a fail-closed net on the same predicate this change rewrites.

HOW VERIFIED. L1: `sirius_unittest "[parquet_byte_range]"` — exact-tiling sweep for k in 1..16 (pairwise disjoint, union complete), straddle/empty/boundary edge cases, the convention corners, and a real-footer check on the test lineitem that cross-checks cudf's `filter_row_groups_with_byte_range` (informational — our rule stays authoritative, a divergence warns). L2: the two-way split scan over a generated 10-row-group parquet drives the real provider/coalescer path; row counts sum to the file total, `(0,0)` equals no range, an inside-one-row-group range is a valid empty scan, and mismatched pairing throws. L3: the Rust FFI test executes splits on a real GPU — two half-file ranges partition 30k rows exactly, both splits in ONE plan (duplicate path, two LocalFiles items) equal the whole-file plan, and an empty split is an empty result. L4: `cargo test -p starrocks-plan-translator` with the live 2-CN FE capture shape (162140518-byte lineitem, split at 81070259). Also record the end-to-end multi-CN Q1/Q6 run if it is available at PR time.

WHAT IS INTENTIONALLY NOT HANDLED. The `sirius_read_parquet` (S3) path refuses ranges outright — its footer/prefetch lifecycle was never tested against them, and refusing beats a quiet whole-file read. Byte-ranged scans are excluded from pinned-table serving in both directions (that is the file-subset-serving PR's problem, and the FE-side `files_query_whole_file_ranges` switch is how the pin path opts out of splits entirely). Incremental scan-range delivery (`has_more`) is refused, not implemented. Overlapping ranges are refused, not merged. Two scan nodes over the same ranged file in one plan are refused (the registry is path-keyed; the ranges cannot be attributed). No docs/super-sirius/scan.md update in this stack — see open questions.

SUPERSEDES. Draft PRs #1636, #1637, #1638, #1639 (branches origin/feat/scan-parquet-byte-range, feat/scan-byte-range-ingestible, feat/ffi-substrait-byte-ranges, feat/starrocks-partial-splits) — same four cuts, 21 dev commits stale. Close them and link. Also supersedes the approach in #1232, which accepts splits only when the ranges assigned to one fragment tile the whole file and then collapses them; that is the special case where a fragment owns everything, this handles the general one.

######## SLICE staging-arena — Exchange staging arena + packed export/push FFI (Item 1)

SUMMARY: This slice adds `sirius::exec::exchange_staging_arena` — one device region, allocated outside every RMM/cudf pool, that a cross-node exchange packs into on the send side and lands into on the receive side, so a transport registers exactly one region — plus the FFI pair (`Fragment::export_packed` / `Fragment::push_packed`) and the thread-safe `StagingArena` handle that let a Rust embedder drive that exchange. It is the enabling primitive for one StarRocks CN per GPU: without it a fragment boundary can only be crossed in-process via `relay_from`, so TPC-H Q1/Q6 cannot fan out across GPUs at all. The arena is deliberately plain `cudaMalloc` (pool/stream-ordered memory silently loses UCX's `cuda_ipc` fast path, a measured ~220x degradation to host bounce) with an optional VMM `CU_MEM_HANDLE_TYPE_FABRIC` mode for multi-host MNNVL, and it hands out leases from an address-ordered, fully coalescing free list so out-of-order releases cannot drift the arena toward exhaustion. Everything here is engine-side and self-contained: nothing in the slice references the CN, NIXL, StarRocks, or `experimental/`; the CN-side consumers (`engine.rs`, `fragment_executor.rs`, `nixl_transport.rs`) are a later slice. Verified by 13 Catch2 cases (ARENA-1..13, which need a GPU and run on the existing `gpu-2xt4` test runner) plus three Rust integration tests that drive a full export→transmit-from-lease→push hop in one process and assert it delivers exactly what the proven `relay_from` hop delivers.

SIZE~1720  PR TITLE: feat(exec): add the exchange staging arena for cross-node batch transfer

DEPENDS_ON: rust-fragment-bindings (the Rust half only: Fragment<'ctx>, SiriusContext::fragment(), the RefCell interior-mutability reshape of SiriusContext, GPU_CONTEXT_LOCK and the write_users_parquet / local_files_plan / stream_read_plan test helpers all come from the ex-#1598 branch origin/stream/16-rust-fragment, which carries NO staging or packed code — so the two slices are disjoint but this one sits on top); scan-byte-ranges (test-only: zero_row_export_is_metadata_only_and_holds_no_lease builds its zero-row batch with local_files_plan_ranged, i.e. the substrait byte-range extraction from the ex-#1636/#1637 stack; if this slice must land first, rewrite that test to produce an empty batch another way or move it into the byte-range slice)

Reviewable in one sitting as a pure allocator with 13 tests that fully pin its invariants. - feat(ffi): expose the staging arena to embedders (Context::staging_* + StagingArena): files=['src/include/sirius_ffi.hpp', 'src/sirius_ffi.cpp', 'rust/crates/sirius-sys/src/lib.rs', 'rust/crates/sirius/src/lib.rs'] | ~250 lines. RISKS:
- No in-tree consumer after this lands: export_packed/push_packed have zero callers in the repo once experimental/starrocks is excluded, so a reviewer will reasonably ask 'who uses this'. Mitigation: the three Rust tests drive the whole send->transmit-from-lease->receive loop in one process and assert byte-for-byte equivalence with the already-merged relay_from hop, and the description must state the Q1/Q6-across-many-GPUs motivation up front.
- A second device allocator outside the RMM/cudf pool is the single most contestable design decision here. The justification (UCX cuda_ipc cannot export cudaMallocAsync allocations and silently degrades ~220x to staged host copies — correct bytes, no error) lives only in a class comment today; it must be in the PR description too, with the measurement.
- The fabric/VMM path (cuMemCreate with CU_MEM_HANDLE_TYPE_FABRIC, ~65 lines of the .cpp) is never exercised by any test and cannot be, since it needs a live IMEX domain and MNNVL — the CI runner is gpu-2xt4. Consider carving it into a follow-up PR so the first arena PR is 100% covered cudaMalloc code; if kept, the description must say it is opt-in via SIRIUS_EXCHANGE_STAGING_ARENA=fabric and untested in CI.
- Two comments cite paths that do not exist upstream: `bench/a100x8/TUNING.md` (in the destructor's peak-live-bytes rationale) and `experimental/starrocks/src/nixl_transport/two_node_harness.rs` (in the fabric ctor). Both must be reworded before pushing or the PR ships dangling references to a private branch.
- Stale 'bump head' language contradicts the shipped free-list allocator in FOUR places: sirius_ffi.hpp Context::staging_release, sirius-sys staging_release doc, rust/crates/sirius staging_release doc, and the zero-row Rust test's closing comment. A reviewer reading the header will believe the allocator is a bump allocator; must be corrected in this PR.
- ARENA-6 calls setenv/unsetenv on SIRIUS_EXCHANGE_STAGING_BYTES inside the shared sirius_unittest binary. It restores the unset state at the end and Catch2 runs cases serially, but it is process-global mutation in a suite that also brings up engines.
- export_packed leases `total + kPackChunkBytes` (8 MiB of slack) per batch because every chunked_pack::next() span must be a full chunk. That is a real arena-sizing consequence (a fragment with many small output batches burns 8 MiB per concurrent in-flight export) and should be called out rather than discovered.
- export_packed and push_packed both use cudf::get_default_stream() and synchronize it, while the rest of the engine runs per-task streams. The STREAM-LINEAGE cudaStreamWaitEvent on the batch's writer event covers correctness, but the choice deserves a sentence in the description.
- The three Rust tests all require a GPU and are only compile-checked in CI (`cargo test --no-run` in .github/workflows/check.yml, line 198) — they never execute there. Say so, and say where they were run.
- Layer ordering nuance: SiriusContext::staging_lease/staging_release take `&self` on SOT, which only works because the Rust Fragment slice moved the inner UniquePtr behind a RefCell. If the staging FFI layer lands before that slice, those two methods have to be `&mut self` and then churn back.

UPSTREAM: Rebasing DEV (de719006) -> upstream-pr/dev (84ea4ab5, 12 commits) is low-risk for this slice. Checked file by file: (1) CMakeLists.txt — upstream adds src/op/aggregate/group_key_labels.cpp, src/op/sirius_physical_dense_count_join.cpp, src/vss/cuvs_index_cache.cpp, two .cu files, and 8 test files, all in the op/, cuda/, vss/, integration/, scan_manager/, planner/ and operator/ blocks; our two lines go into the `src/exec/` block (after batch_stream.cpp) and the `test/cpp/exec/` block (after test_bounded_thread_pool.cpp), neither of which upstream touches. No textual conflict. (2) src/sirius_ffi.cpp and src/include/sirius_ffi.hpp — untouched upstream (not in the DEV..UP stat at all). (3) rust/ — untouched upstream. (4) src/exec/ — untouched upstream. (5) The two helper headers this slice pulls in DID change upstream but compatibly: data/data_batch_utils.hpp only reworked `peak_materialization_bytes`; both `make_data_batch` overloads keep their exact signatures, and push_packed uses the `unique_ptr<cudf::table>` one. cudf/cudf_utils.hpp only gained a `make_table` helper; `get_cudf_type` and `get_cudf_table_view` are unchanged. (6) docs/super-sirius/configuration.md gained 9 lines upstream — a small chance of a hunk collision depending on where the new env-var rows go; place them near the existing SIRIUS_LOG_* paragraph (DEV line 553) rather than at the file tail. (7) Upstream #1547 (pinned-entry lookup) and #1555 (per-chunk MVCC fused scan-filter) touch scan_manager and scan paths only — no overlap. Note separately that the SOT arena source differs from the ex-#1644 branch (origin/stream-fragment-execution) by clang-format only, so no re-review of the algorithm is needed relative to that draft.

DESCRIPTION OUTLINE:
## Motivation

Sirius can already split a query into fragments and hand batches from one to the next — but only *in process*, via `Fragment::relay_from` (#1481). To run TPC-H Q1/Q6 with one StarRocks compute node per GPU, a fragment boundary has to cross a process and a device, which means a transport has to be handed a stable device address to read from and write into. It cannot be handed pool memory: UCX's `cuda_ipc` path cannot export a `cudaMallocAsync` allocation and silently falls back to staged host copies — correct bytes, no error, ~220x slower. That failure mode is invisible, which is why this is a dedicated allocator rather than a pool tag.

This PR adds that region and the FFI to drive it. It ships no transport and no consumer; it is the primitive the cross-node exchange (a follow-up) is built on.

## What changed

- **`sirius::exec::exchange_staging_arena`** (`src/exec/exchange_staging_arena.{hpp,cpp}`): one device region, sized from `SIRIUS_EXCHANGE_STAGING_BYTES` at context bring-up (unset = no arena, and every staging call fails loudly rather than taking a slow path). Leases come from an address-ordered, always-coalesced free list under a mutex; `release` merges forward and backward, so capacity bounds *concurrent live bytes*, not lifetime totals. Every lease is 256-byte aligned and contiguous, so it is a valid RDMA target and a valid `cudf::unpack` source. `SIRIUS_EXCHANGE_STAGING_ARENA=fabric` swaps `cudaMalloc` for a VMM allocation with `CU_MEM_HANDLE_TYPE_FABRIC`, the only allocation a peer on another host can map (opt-in; needs a live IMEX domain; not exercised in CI).
- **FFI**: `Context::staging_lease/release/base/capacity`, a thread-safe `StagingArena` handle sharing ownership of the one allocator (so an RPC thread can serve a peer's lease request while the context thread is inside `Fragment::run`), and the batch pair `Fragment::export_packed` (`cudf::chunked_pack` gathers *directly into* the lease — the staging copy is the pack's own gather) / `Fragment::push_packed` (unpack, schema-check against the declared stream, deep-copy out of the lease into pool memory, push). Copy-out-on-arrival, so a lease is reusable the moment `push_packed` returns.
- **Rust**: `SiriusContext::staging_*`, `StagingArena` (`Send + Sync`, with the safety argument spelled out), `PackedBatch`, `Fragment::export_packed`/`push_packed`.
- **Docs**: the two env vars in `configuration.md`; the export/push contract beside `relay_from` in `streaming-fragments.md`.

## How it was verified

- 13 Catch2 cases, `[staging_arena]`. ARENA-8 asserts `free + live == capacity` after every operation; ARENA-10 runs 10 000 out-of-order lease/release cycles with 8 leases live at a time and asserts no drift (the bump allocator this replaces exhausted a 1 MiB arena within a few hundred); ARENA-7 pins that a never-released lease blocks only its own block; ARENA-11 stamps and reads back device bytes to prove live leases are disjoint; ARENA-12 runs 8 threads x 2000 iterations; ARENA-13 pins the `align_up` overflow guard on wire-supplied lengths.
- Three Rust integration tests: a packed hop delivers exactly what the `relay_from` hop delivers for the same plan pair; a zero-row batch comes back metadata-only and holds no lease (the regression that used to orphan >= 8 MiB per empty batch and exhaust the arena after ~20 queries); a schema mismatch is refused by name.
- Rust tests need a GPU and are compile-checked only in CI (`cargo test --no-run`); run manually on <box>.

## Intentionally not handled

- No transport, no compute-node wiring — the NIXL/StarRocks consumers are a follow-up.
- Exporting a spilled (non-GPU-resident) batch throws rather than rehydrating.
- The fabric path is opt-in and untested in CI (no MNNVL runner).
- `export_packed` over-leases by one 8 MiB pack chunk, because every `chunked_pack::next()` span must be a full chunk; sizing guidance is in `configuration.md`.

OPEN QUESTIONS:
- Ship the fabric/VMM path in this PR or hold it back? It is ~65 of the 338 .cpp lines, is untestable on the gpu-2xt4 CI runner, and is only needed for the multi-HOST case; the single-host multi-GPU case that Q1/Q6 needs first is served entirely by the cudaMalloc path. Splitting it out would make the first PR fully covered by its own tests.
- Does the arena PR land before or after the Rust Fragment bindings slice (ex-#1598, origin/stream/16-rust-fragment)? That branch has no staging code at all, so the two are disjoint — but SiriusContext::staging_lease/release take `&self` on SOT only because that slice moved the inner UniquePtr behind a RefCell. Landing staging first means shipping `&mut self` and churning it.
- zero_row_export_is_metadata_only_and_holds_no_lease depends on `local_files_plan_ranged` (byte-range splits) to produce its zero-row batch. Keep the test here and reorder behind the byte-range slice, rewrite it to produce an empty batch without byte ranges (e.g. an always-false filter), or move it into the byte-range PR?
- Should `Fragment::export_packed`/`push_packed` go in this PR at all, or in a third stacked PR? They are the only part with a dependency on the Fragment slice, and they roughly double the review surface (~600 of ~1720 lines).
- Is 8 MiB the right `kPackChunkBytes`? The comment notes 1 MiB is cudf's minimum, and the chunk size is the per-export over-lease. Nobody has swept it.
- Should the arena's device bytes be visible to the RMM pool accounting / config dump in some read-only way? Today the only feedback an operator gets is the two INFO log lines (size at construction, peak live at teardown) — deliberate, but a reviewer may want a `SET`-visible statistic.
- Both env vars are process-environment knobs rather than `sirius.yaml` config. Is that acceptable upstream, or should `SIRIUS_EXCHANGE_STAGING_BYTES` become a `sirius.exchange.staging_bytes` YAML key with the env var as an override?
- The C++ FFI additions are ABI-visible on `sirius::ffi::Context`. Is there an FFI stability expectation upstream (the header is described as 'the seed of the public API libsirius will expose') that should gate adding five methods and a new exported class?

######## SLICE nixl-transport — Item 1.1 — NIXL integration (CN transport tier + StarRocks exchange-proto patch)

SUMMARY: This slice is the cross-node data plane of the multi-CN campaign: it moves a sender fragment's packed GPU output from one StarRocks CN to another over nixl/UCX `cuda_ipc` (WRITE into a peer-granted staging lease), with only control frames (agent metadata, lease grant, per-batch signal, EOS) on brpc. It is on the critical path for the stated Q1/Q6-on-many-GPUs goal: with one CN per GPU the FE byte-splits the scan and plans a two-phase aggregate, so every Q1/Q6 run crosses exactly one exchange boundary, and without this tier `execute_fragment_with_inputs` refuses every remote destination with a loud error. The slice carries three Sirius-only RPCs added to StarRocks' `internal_service.proto` by a checked-in patch (`exchange_nixl_md`, `request_staging_lease`, `transmit_packed`), a dedicated single-threaded nixl agent owner (`nixl_transport.rs`), a blocking PRPC client for CN→CN calls, a validated transport-tunables registry, a bring-up session warmup that fixes a 100%-reproducible cold-cluster deadlock, and the six service handlers/helpers in `compute_node_service.rs` that terminate the wire. It strictly depends on the staging-arena slice (Rust `StagingArena` / `export_packed` / `push_packed`) and on the exchange-runtime slice (`local_exchange.rs`, `FragmentRun`, `ExchangeIdentity`/`route_destination`, dispatch worker) — but its first three layers (proto patch, tunables, PRPC client) are independent of both and should land in parallel with them to shorten the critical path. One hard CI finding: SOT never updated `.github/workflows/experimental.yml`, so the moment `build.rs`'s patch guard lands, CI's `--no-default-features` job fails on an unpatched submodule; the workflow step is a required part of layer 1.

SIZE~6420  PR TITLE: feat(cn): move exchange batches GPU-to-GPU over nixl

DEPENDS_ON: staging-arena (HARD: `FragmentExecutor::{staging_info, staging_lease, staging_release, export_packed_next, drop_parked}` and `StagedBatch` must exist before `TransportState::bring_up` can register the arena or `send_fragment` can lease/pack; the C++ `exchange_staging_arena` + Rust `sirius::StagingArena` is what `executor.staging_info()` returns); exchange-runtime (HARD: `local_exchange.rs::{ExchangeKey, LocalExchange::push_remote_frame, ReadyFragment, SenderSource::Remote}` is the only consumer of `transmit_packed`; `SenderSlot` and `FragmentRun{inputs, remote_inputs, outputs, broadcast, hash_keys}` are the executor contract; `ExchangeIdentity`/`DestinationRoute`/`route_destination` must already classify a destination, with the Remote arm returning the loud 'needs the nixl transport tier' error that THIS slice replaces with a real drain; the `dispatch_worker` + `ServiceCore` refactor of `SiriusComputeNodeService` is the shape the handlers plug into); exchange-input-cardinality / 55e63371 (SOFT, other direction: the `rows = 10` proto field and `StagedBatch.rows` are defined here and consumed there; if cardinality lands first, this slice only reads/writes an existing field); engine-settings + gpu-affinity (SOFT, packaging only: `scripts/cn-env.sh` and the `pixi cn-build/cn-test/cn-run` rewrite are shared with those slices; whichever lands first carries the file)

FILES:

LAYERS:
- stacked/cn-exchange-proto-patch — build(cn): carry the Sirius exchange RPCs as a checked-in StarRocks patch: files=['experimental/starrocks/patches/nixl-exchange-proto.patch', 'experimental/starrocks/scripts/apply-starrocks-patches.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/build.rs', '.gitmodules', 'experimental/starrocks/pixi.toml', '.github/workflows/experimental.yml'] | ~170 lines, zero code dependencies, lands FIRST and in parallel with the exchange runtime. The three added RPCs generate trait methods that default to 'method not implemented', so the CN's behavior is unchanged until layer 4. The workflow step is mandatory and is the one thing SOT forgot: build.rs's guard hard-fails on a checked-out-but-unpatched submodule, which is exactly CI's state. Reviewer question this PR must answer up front: why patch a submodule instead of vendoring a Sirius-owned proto (see open_questions).
- stacked/cn-transport-tunables — feat(cn): a validated registry for the CN's transport knobs: files=['experimental/starrocks/src/tunable.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/docs/TUNABLES.md', 'experimental/starrocks/src/lib.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/main.rs'] | ~500 lines, self-contained, 10 tests that all run in CI, no dependency on layers 1/3/4 or on either prerequisite slice. Its motivation stands alone and is a real defect: `.parse().ok()` turned a typo (SIRIUS_CN_NIXL_WARMUP_TIMEOUT_SECS=6O with a letter O) into the silent default, so the knob looked like it had no effect. Resolve-at-bring-up + reject-not-clamp + log-what-you-got. Land before layer 3 (PrpcClient reads rpc_timeout through it) — or land 3 first with a const and switch here.
- stacked/cn-prpc-client — feat(cn): a blocking PRPC client for CN-to-CN calls: files=['experimental/starrocks/src/prpc.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/prpc_client.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/lib.rs'] | ~415 lines. Promotes the already-existing test-only client half of the PRPC framing to production (Frame::for_request/read/read_payload_sync/Error::remote) and adds Frame::into_response + the cached-connection client with one transparent reconnect. Its 3 tests run in CI against the CN's own BrpcServer dispatch, so this is genuinely verified without a GPU. Depends only on layer 2 (rpc_timeout). Its `serve_stub_cn` helper calls BrpcServer::with_executor — match whatever signature the exchange-runtime slice has landed by then.
- stacked/cn-nixl-transport — feat(cn): move exchange batches GPU-to-GPU over nixl: files=['experimental/starrocks/Cargo.toml', 'experimental/starrocks/Cargo.lock', 'experimental/starrocks/src/nixl_transport.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/lib.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/brpc.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/main.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/compute_node_service.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/scripts/cn-env.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/DEMO.md'] | The core, ~2100 production+test lines (plus the 359-line generated Cargo.lock reviewers will skip). One dedicated thread owns the nixl Agent (the binding documents a multithreading deadlock caveat), the staging arena is registered as VRAM, and the wire is lease→WRITE→transmit_packed→release. Includes the F1 bandwidth canary, which is the load-bearing safety property: cudaMallocAsync pool memory over cuda_ipc still transfers CORRECT bytes at ~220x slower, so nothing but a measurement can catch it. Land WITHOUT the warmup and WITHOUT the drain-ticket overlap (layers 5/6) — i.e. `ensure_session` lazy only, and a plain blocking `transport.send_fragment(spec)?` loop in execute_fragment_with_inputs — so the diff stays one sitting. HARD dependency on staging-arena and exchange-runtime.
- stacked/cn-nixl-session-warmup — fix(cn): pre-establish nixl peer sessions so a cold cluster cannot deadlock: files=['experimental/starrocks/src/nixl_transport/warmup.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/nixl_transport.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/lib.rs'] | ~560 lines with the best standalone motivation in the whole slice, and it is a BUG FIX, not an optimization: CN A's transport thread blocks in an outbound exchange_nixl_md to B while B's thread is blocked in its own outbound call to A. An all-to-all shuffle first-contacts every peer at once, so this is the normal case. Reproduced 2026-08-08 on a fresh 4-CN cluster: q14 run 1 (cold) FAILED after 121 s (errorCode=62, method request time out 60000ms, channel R:/127.0.0.1:9102); run 2 (warm, same cluster, same SQL) 751 ms. The TPC-H harness discards run 0, so every sweep paid the hang invisibly. Carries: mod warmup, SessionWarmup, TransportRequest::WarmSession, the ensure_session/install_session split, NixlTransport::start's warmup spawn, Drop's stop-warmup-first ordering, and lib.rs::list_alive_compute_nodes.
- stacked/cn-nixl-drain-overlap — perf(cn): dispatch a local receiver before joining the remote drains: files=['experimental/starrocks/src/nixl_transport.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/compute_node_service.rs'] | ~350 lines. Replaces the blocking send loop with DrainTicket/SenderDrains/FragmentOutcome + dispatch_then_join, so a local receiver whose sender set just completed starts while the transport thread is still draining this CN's other destinations. Measured: 3 x 30 ms per CN on q14 SF500's 948 MB/destination stage. The PR must state (as the code comments do) that wire ORDER IS UNCHANGED — one FIFO channel into one thread still handling SendFragment inline, seq/eos/drop_parked all inside the drain loop — because the receiver's per-(exchange, sender) seq-gap check is what would catch a regression. Carries 5 of the 13 tests: local_receiver_is_dispatched_before_the_remote_drains_complete, every_remote_destination_is_drained_even_after_one_fails, remote_drains_are_posted_once_per_destination_in_the_fes_order, the_sender_rpc_reports_ok_only_after_every_drain_has_finished, joining_drains_waits_for_every_ticket_and_reports_the_first_failure.
- stacked/cn-nixl-bench-harnesses — test(cn): two-process NVLink bench and two-host GPU echo over the production primitives (OPTIONAL): files=['experimental/starrocks/src/nixl_transport/nixl_bench.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/nixl_transport/nixl_echo.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/nixl_transport/two_node_harness.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/scripts/nixl-echo-2node.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/src/nixl_transport.rs'] | 2291 lines that are #[cfg(all(test, feature = "nixl-transport"))] and therefore NEVER compile in CI — an unreviewable, unverifiable block in an upstream PR. They are real engineering (they substitute only the brpc control plane and the CUDA memcpy shim; every nixl call is the production one), but they belong behind the production tier, or in the bench campaign, or not upstream at all. My recommendation: hold this layer until layers 1-6 are merged, then propose it separately with the hostnames genericized and a note on how a reviewer would run it.

RISKS:
- CI GOES RED WITHOUT THE WORKFLOW STEP. SOT never touched .github/workflows/experimental.yml. Its 'Init starrocks submodules' step checks out experimental/starrocks/starrocks, and from layer 1 onward build.rs's require_exchange_proto_patch fails on an unpatched, present submodule — so both `cargo clippy --all-targets --no-default-features` and `cargo test --workspace --no-default-features` die. Add `bash scripts/apply-starrocks-patches.sh` after the submodule init. This is not optional and is not deferrable to a later layer.
- A dirty-by-design submodule is an unusual thing to ask an upstream reviewer to accept. Mitigations already in the code: `ignore = dirty` in .gitmodules, the idempotent apply script, and build.rs's guard that names the exact remedy. Alternative to offer in the PR: vendor a Sirius-owned `sirius_exchange.proto` next to build.rs that imports StarRocks' types, extending the service via a separate file — no submodule mutation at all.
- `default = ["sirius-engine", "nixl-transport"]` means a plain `cargo build` in experimental/starrocks now needs libnixl (NIXL_PREFIX) on top of the Sirius build tree it already needed. NIXL_NO_STUBS_FALLBACK=1 is mandatory or nixl-sys silently links a dlopen stub that fails at agent creation. cn-env.sh and the pixi tasks encode this; the PR body must too.
- prpc.rs promotes four previously `#[cfg(test)]` items to production code paths (Frame::for_request, Frame::read, read_payload_sync, and the new into_response). Under --no-default-features they are dead; the `#[cfg_attr(not(feature = "nixl-transport"), allow(dead_code))]` attributes are load-bearing for `clippy -D warnings` and must not be dropped in review.
- warmup.rs is behind `#[cfg(feature = "nixl-transport")]`, so its three tests (peer-list parsing, backoff doubling) never run in CI. The pure-string helpers `parse_peer_list` and the backoff computation could move into the always-compiled part of nixl_transport.rs so CI covers them; as shipped, a regression in SIRIUS_CN_NIXL_WARMUP_PEERS parsing is invisible until a GPU box runs it.
- `SIRIUS_CN_NIXL_WARMUP` (kill switch) and `SIRIUS_CN_NIXL_WARMUP_PEERS` (explicit peer list) are read with raw `std::env::var` inside warmup.rs, bypassing the validated registry that layer 2 exists to enforce. TUNABLES.md calls this out honestly ('Related, but not in the registry'), but it is exactly the silent-typo class the registry was built to kill.
- The warmup thread adds a new outbound bring-up dependency: the CN opens a MySQL connection to the FE and runs `SHOW PROC '/compute_nodes'` on a 2 s poll to discover peers. On a cluster where the CN cannot reach the FE's query_port, or the FE credentials differ, warmup degrades to loud-but-non-fatal and the cold-start deadlock returns.
- `ArenaRegion::device_id()` is hardcoded to 0 and write_and_wait passes dev_id 0 to add_desc. That is only correct when the process sees exactly one device — the one-CN-per-GPU invariant. A reviewer running two CNs on one visible multi-GPU process would get silent wrong-device behavior. The invariant is documented in nixl_bench.rs's module doc but should be asserted (or at least stated) in nixl_transport.rs itself.
- `Tunables::resolve()` publishes to a process-global; `Tunables::get()` before resolve returns DEFAULTS. Correct for unit tests and for no-feature builds, and main resolves first — but it is process-global mutable config, which a reviewer will ask about.
- The GPU smoke test `nixl_cross_agent_write_between_arena_leases` mutates the process environment (`set_var("SIRIUS_EXCHANGE_STAGING_BYTES")`) under GPU_ENGINE_TEST_LOCK. It is #[ignore]d, so it never runs in CI, but the unsafe env mutation + cross-crate lock is a review point.
- nixl-sys 1.3 is a third-party crate that links a non-redistributable NVIDIA library at build time; confirm its license and crates.io provenance are acceptable to the upstream repo before layer 4.

UPSTREAM: Near-zero conflict surface. UP (84ea4ab5, 12 commits ahead of DEV) touches src/ C++ (#1547 pinned-entry lookup in the scan manager, #1555 per-chunk MVCC fused scan-filter, vss/cuvs, dense-count-join), test/cpp, test/tpch_performance, and CONTRIBUTING.md (#1664 reviewability docs). None of that overlaps this slice.

Two files to watch:
  1. `experimental/starrocks/Cargo.lock` — upstream bumped exactly one dependency (3.0.3 → 3.0.4); this slice adds 33 entries (the whole nixl-sys tree: bindgen, cexpr, cfg_aliases, clang-sys, glob, itertools, jobserver, libloading, minimal-lexical, nix, nixl-sys, nom, objc2-* x12, os_info, pkg-config, rustc-hash, shlex, bincode, block2, dispatch2). Regenerate with `cargo check` on top of upstream rather than resolving the hunks by hand. Note this file is NOT in the inventory you were handed but changes +355/-4 on SOT; it belongs entirely to this slice.
  2. `.github/workflows/experimental.yml` — untouched by upstream and by SOT; this slice is the only writer, so the added patch-apply step lands clean.

`.gitmodules` is untouched by upstream. `patches/apply-starrocks-patches.sh` globs `patches/*.patch`, so the FILES() whole-file-ranges patch (slice a4608cf0) will be picked up automatically when it lands — no coordination needed between the two slices beyond ordering the script first.

Rebase note: the old byte-range draft stack (#1636-#1639, based on a5cfa8fd, 21 dev commits stale) does not touch experimental/starrocks/src, so it will not conflict with this slice even if it is refreshed in parallel. No prior draft PR covers NIXL specifically — #1686 "Feat/pin table cn" is the umbrella that contains it, and #1672/#1674 "Demo multi cn" carry it as part of the whole campaign; #1398 "feat(starrocks): cross the fragment boundary as native batches" (closed) is the nearest ancestor for the exchange work and its description is reusable raw material for the exchange-runtime slice, not this one. All four are drafts to be closed.

DESCRIPTION OUTLINE:
MOTIVATION. With one StarRocks CN per GPU, the FE byte-splits a scan across N nodes and plans a two-phase aggregate, so TPC-H Q1 and Q6 cross exactly one exchange boundary per run. Today `execute_fragment_with_inputs` classifies that destination as Remote and refuses it: "cross-node exchange to host:port needs the nixl transport tier, which is not active." Multi-GPU Q1/Q6 does not run at all without this. The naive alternative — Arrow-encode the partial state, ship it over brpc, decode it — pulls every exchange batch off the device and back, on the one hop the whole design exists to make cheap.

WHAT CHANGED. A cross-node data plane where the payload never leaves GPU memory:
  - Three Sirius-only RPCs added to StarRocks' `internal_service.proto` by a checked-in patch (`exchange_nixl_md`, `request_staging_lease`, `transmit_packed`). `build.rs` fails the build with the exact `git apply` remedy when the submodule is unpatched, instead of letting `prost` emit a service missing three methods and failing 200 lines later on an unresolved import.
  - `nixl_transport.rs`: one dedicated thread owns the nixl `Agent` (the binding documents a multithreading deadlock caveat, so every agent touch funnels through a request channel, mirroring the engine-thread pattern). Bring-up creates a UCX backend and registers the engine's exchange staging arena as VRAM. The wire is WRITE-based and every lease lifetime stays process-local: sender `export_packed` → `request_staging_lease` at the peer → nixl WRITE lease→lease → `transmit_packed` with the cudf pack metadata in the brpc attachment → release the local lease; a final `transmit_packed{eos}` closes the sender on the peer's rendezvous. EOS and sender-set completion stay on brpc — one source of truth.
  - A mandatory first-contact bandwidth canary. This is the load-bearing safety property, not a nicety: `cudaMallocAsync` pool memory over `cuda_ipc` does not error — it silently degrades ~220x while still transferring correct bytes. Nothing in nixl or UCX reports it. So first contact WRITEs a 16 MiB probe and refuses the tier below a floor (default well above the ~0.4 GB/s degraded path and well below the measured healthy ones: ~85-90 GB/s same-host cuda_ipc on A100, 322-399 GB/s on GB200 NV18).
  - `prpc_client.rs`, a small blocking PRPC client (cached connection, correlation ids, one transparent reconnect), promoting the client half of the framing that already existed under `#[cfg(test)]`.
  - `tunable.rs`, a validated registry for the transport knobs, resolved once at bring-up: a typo or out-of-range value fails CN startup and the resolved set is logged. The previous `.parse().ok()` turned `SIRIUS_CN_NIXL_WARMUP_TIMEOUT_SECS=6O` (letter O) into the 180 s default with no diagnostic.
  - Six handlers/helpers in `compute_node_service.rs` terminating the wire, plus the `Option<NixlTransport>` seam threaded through `brpc.rs` and `main.rs`.

HOW VERIFIED. 13 Rust unit tests run in CI's `--no-default-features` job through a `NixlTransport::for_test` seam — the transport handle and its request types compile in every build precisely so the service seam is testable without libnixl (frames feed a dispatched receiver; a seq gap is a loud error; a canary frame releases its lease without touching the rendezvous; every missing-transport / missing-arena path is an error, not a silent no-op). `prpc_client`'s three tests round-trip against the CN's own BRPC dispatch over loopback. `tunable`'s ten tests pin reject-not-clamp. On hardware: an #[ignore]d two-agent GPU smoke test, and `pixi run cluster2` (two CNs, one GPU) running the DEMO.md Q6-shaped query to `61567694.9502` with `count(*) = 6001215` as the exactly-once check, with `nixl bandwidth canary ... gbps="145.3"` and `transmitted batches via nixl ... batches=1 bytes=64` in the log — 64 bytes because two-phase aggregation reduces each CN's scan to one partial-state row before the hop, against `bytes=457856` for the one-phase plan.

WHAT IS INTENTIONALLY NOT HANDLED. Bring-up peer-session warmup (the cold-cluster first-contact deadlock) is the next PR in this stack; until it lands, sessions are established lazily on first use and a fresh cluster's first cross-node query pays first contact. The drain-overlap optimization (dispatch a ready local receiver before joining remote drains) is the PR after that; this one drains synchronously. No per-query GC: a failed transmit drops the parked output best-effort, but there is no cancel path. No streaming — a sender fully materializes and parks before any byte moves. The two-process NVLink bench and two-host GPU echo harnesses are held back for a separate proposal because they never compile in CI. Concurrency across fragments still needs per-query lifecycle isolation in the engine.

OPEN QUESTIONS:
- Submodule patching vs. vendoring: will upstream accept `ignore = dirty` on the starrocks submodule plus a manual `apply-starrocks-patches.sh` step, or should the three RPCs live in a Sirius-owned `sirius_exchange.proto` next to build.rs that imports StarRocks' types? The vendored form removes the dirty submodule and the build.rs guard entirely, at the cost of a second service definition. Decide BEFORE layer 1, because everything stacks on it.
- Does the exchange-runtime slice own `ExchangeIdentity`, `DestinationRoute` and `route_destination` (with the Remote arm returning the 'needs the nixl transport tier' error, which this slice then replaces), or do they come with NIXL? I recommend the former — it is the minimal dependency direction, keeps a single-CN build able to refuse a remote destination coherently, and lets `data_stream_sink_to_remote_destination_is_a_loud_error` land with the runtime instead of here.
- Should `PTransmitPackedParams.rows` (field 10) and the `StagedBatch.rows` plumbing ship in this slice or with the exchange-input-cardinality slice (55e63371)? The proto patch is a single file that ships whole, so the field lands here regardless; the question is only whether the two `rows:` lines in `send_fragment` and `handle_transmit_packed` come with it (my recommendation: yes, so the field is not dead on arrival).
- Do the bench/echo harnesses (nixl_bench.rs, nixl_echo.rs, two_node_harness.rs, nixl-echo-2node.sh — 2291 lines that never compile in CI) belong upstream at all? If yes, they need the presto-gb200-gcn-17/18 hostnames genericized and a documented way for a reviewer to run them.
- Should `warmup::parse_peer_list` and the retry-backoff computation move out from behind `#[cfg(feature = "nixl-transport")]` so their three tests run in CI? Small change, real coverage gain.
- Should `SIRIUS_CN_NIXL_WARMUP` and `SIRIUS_CN_NIXL_WARMUP_PEERS` be folded into the validated registry rather than read with raw `env::var`? They are the two warmup knobs an operator is most likely to typo.
- Is `nixl-sys 1.3` acceptable as a crates.io dependency for this repo (license, provenance, the fact that it links a non-redistributable NVIDIA library at build time)? If not, the transport tier needs a vendored or dlopen-based binding.
- Should the CI job additionally build `--features nixl-transport --no-default-features`-style to type-check the agent tier without a GPU? It cannot link libnixl on a CPU runner, so probably not — but the consequence is that `agent_tier`, `warmup`, and the three bench modules are compiled by nothing in CI, which is worth stating explicitly in the PR.
- Where does `scripts/cn-env.sh` land — here, or with the engine-settings/multi-CN-launcher slice? It is ~70% nixl/UCX/bindgen and ~30% libsirius link paths; splitting it produces a worse file than letting one slice own it.
- `SHUTDOWN_GRACE` in main.rs: is the 15 s force-exit bound this slice's (the transport Drop joins the warmup and transport threads) or the engine-teardown slice's? Both have a claim; pick one so it does not land twice.

######## SLICE arrow-in-out — Item 1.2 — Arrow in/out on the Fragment FFI (Doris compatibility)

SUMMARY: Adds `sirius::ffi::Fragment::push_arrow(stream_id, schema_addr, array_addr)`: import one host-memory Arrow record batch (Arrow C Data Interface) into a declared fragment input stream, so an embedder that already holds Arrow — Apache Doris (issue #1590, morningman, 2026-09-01) — can feed a Sirius fragment without going through parquet files or the cudf packed-bytes wire format. This is NEW code, not a DEV..SOT carve-out: nothing on SOT touches Arrow import, and `cudf::from_arrow_host`/`to_arrow_host` are entirely unused in the tree today (grep over src/ and test/ finds zero hits; nanoarrow 0.7.0 is already in the dependency closure via `vcpkg_ports/cudf/vcpkg.json` → `nanoarrow`). The implementation mirrors SOT's `Fragment::push_packed` one-for-one — validate against the stream's declared schema before allocating, deep-copy into pool memory owned by the engine's GPU memory space, `stream.synchronize()`, `sirius::make_data_batch`, `session().push()`, throw when push returns false — so the reviewer reads a shape they have already reviewed. For Q1/Q6-on-many-GPUs it is not on the critical path (the CN uses relay/packed hops), but it is the cheapest way to add a second front end without touching the exchange: it needs only DEV's #1481 `Fragment`, not the staging arena, and it gives Doris (and any Arrow-native embedder) a first-class ingest path onto the same multi-fragment engine the CN drives.

SIZE~1100  PR TITLE: feat(ffi): import a host Arrow record batch into a fragment input stream

DEPENDS_ON: DEV @ de719006 / upstream #1481 — already merged; `sirius::ffi::Fragment`, `Fragment::Impl::resolved_inputs`, `Impl::session()`, `stream_session::push` and `sirius::make_data_batch` all exist on DEV, so layers 1-2 have NO slice dependency and can be the first PR of the whole campaign; rust-fragment (draft PR #1598 = origin/stream/16-rust-fragment) — required for LAYER 3 ONLY (the Rust binding + Rust integration tests): DEV's rust/crates/sirius-sys/src/lib.rs declares only `type Context`, so there is no `type Fragment` for `push_arrow` to hang off and no `Fragment<'ctx>` safe wrapper. If #1598's replacement has not landed when this is ready, ship layers 1-2 alone and follow with layer 3.; NONE on the arena slice (src/exec/exchange_staging_arena.*) — push_arrow copies from caller-owned host memory, so it never leases; NONE on the packed-exchange slice (export_packed/push_packed), the byte-range stack (#1636-#1639), the cardinality slice (declare_input_cardinality/output_row_count), the pin-table-FFI slice, or any experimental/starrocks CN slice

FILES:
- src/include/helper/arrow_host_import.hpp [whole-file] NEW (~75 lines). Declares `sirius::helper::import_host_arrow_batch(ArrowSchema const*, ArrowDeviceArray const*, std::vector<logical_type> const& declared, std::vector<std::string> const& declared_names, rmm::cuda_stream_view, rmm::device_async_resource_ref) -> std::unique_ptr<cudf::table>`. Forward-declares ArrowSchema/ArrowDeviceArray exactly like cudf/interop.hpp does, so the header stays free of any Arrow/nanoarrow include. Kept out of sirius_ffi.cpp on purpose: it is the piece worth unit-testing without a Fragment, and the follow-up result path (to_arrow_host) will grow its mirror here.
- src/helper/arrow_host_import.cpp [whole-file] NEW (~140 lines). Calls cudf::from_arrow_host(schema, input, stream, mr), then validates+normalizes column-by-column against the declared stream schema: exact `sirius::get_cudf_type(declared[i])` match passes through; arrow decimal128 -> declared DECIMAL64/DECIMAL32 (and any scale delta) goes through cudf::cast — mandatory, because get_cudf_type maps DuckDB DECIMAL(15,2) to DECIMAL64(-2) (src/include/cudf/cudf_utils.hpp:195-209) while from_arrow_host always yields DECIMAL128, so every TPC-H money column would otherwise be refused; anything else mismatched throws sirius::invalid_input_exception naming stream id, column index, column name, declared type and carried type — the same message shape as push_packed (src/sirius_ffi.cpp on SOT, lines 875-899). Also rejects a column-count mismatch and null pointers.
- src/include/helper/arrow_c_device_abi.hpp [whole-file] NEW (~50 lines). Minimal `struct ArrowDeviceArray` + ARROW_DEVICE_CPU, under the standard `#ifndef ARROW_C_DEVICE_DATA_INTERFACE` guard, so it no-ops if nanoarrow_device.h or arrow/c/abi.h is already in the TU. Needed because DuckDB's duckdb/common/arrow/arrow.hpp defines ArrowSchema/ArrowArray/ArrowArrayStream (ARROW_C_DATA_INTERFACE) but NOT ArrowDeviceArray, the pixi conda env ships no nanoarrow headers (only cudf_test/nanoarrow_utils.hpp, which itself includes them), and the vcpkg tree ships nanoarrow but no Arrow C++ — so neither include is portable across both build modes. See open_questions for the alternative (bind cudf::from_arrow(ArrowSchema*, ArrowArray*) and drop this file entirely).

No public FFI surface changes, so a reviewer can judge the type-validation/normalization policy (especially the mandatory decimal128 -> DECIMAL64 cast that every TPC-H money column needs) in isolation, before deciding anything about the FFI contract. Also the natural home for the follow-up result-path mirror, which is the argument for it being its own file rather than a lambda inside sirius_ffi.cpp.
- ffi-push-arrow (Fragment::push_arrow + docs): files=['src/include/sirius_ffi.hpp', 'src/sirius_ffi.cpp', 'test/cpp/exec/test_sirius_ffi_fragment.cpp', 'docs/super-sirius/streaming-fragments.md'] | ~190 lines. Layers 1+2 together (~760 lines) are shippable as a single PR if the Rust Fragment binding is not landed — Doris embeds the C++ surface, not the Rust crate, so this is a complete deliverable on its own.
- rust-push-arrow (bindings + integration tests): files=['rust/crates/sirius-sys/src/lib.rs', 'rust/crates/sirius/src/lib.rs'] | ~340 lines. Split out because it is the only part that depends on another slice (the Rust Fragment binding, draft #1598) and because it carries the end-to-end coverage that the C++ tests structurally cannot: building a Substrait stream-read plan is easy from Rust (the `substrait` dev-dependency is already there and SOT's stream_read_plan_f64 / local_files_plan helpers exist) and impossible from test/cpp/exec/test_sirius_ffi_fragment.cpp, whose own header comment says so. CI runs `cargo test --no-run` for these packages, so they are compile-checked upstream even without a GPU runner.

RISKS:
- No GPU CI: the Catch2 [gpu] cases and all three Rust tests need a real device. Upstream check.yml only does fmt/clippy/`cargo test --no-run` for the rust packages, so the positive path is compile-checked but not executed upstream — verification has to be stated as "run locally on <device>" in the PR description, same as every other GPU-touching PR.
- ArrowDeviceArray has no portable header in this build: DuckDB's duckdb/common/arrow/arrow.hpp defines ArrowSchema/ArrowArray/ArrowArrayStream but not ArrowDeviceArray; the pixi conda env (.pixi/envs/default/include) ships arrow/c/abi.h but no nanoarrow headers, while the vcpkg tree ships nanoarrow 0.7.0 (vcpkg_ports/nanoarrow, NANOARROW_DEVICE=ON, pulled in as a cudf dependency) but no Arrow C++. Either include breaks one of the two build modes. Mitigated by the ~50-line guarded shim in src/include/helper/arrow_c_device_abi.hpp — but a reviewer may reasonably prefer binding cudf::from_arrow(ArrowSchema const*, ArrowArray const*) instead, which needs no ArrowDeviceArray at all (see open_questions).
- Type normalization is a policy decision, not just plumbing. cudf::from_arrow_host always produces DECIMAL128 for arrow decimal128, while sirius::get_cudf_type maps DuckDB DECIMAL(15,2) to DECIMAL64(-2) (src/include/cudf/cudf_utils.hpp:195-209). Without the cast, importing any TPC-H money column fails the schema guard outright. The cast itself can lose data (DECIMAL128 values outside the DECIMAL64 range) — the PR must say whether that throws or is documented as the caller's problem. Same question for arrow large_string / string_view, which cudf may not import at all.
- Threading contract narrower than Doris asked for. Doris wants push during run() from other threads. The C++ substrate supports it (batch_stream's producer side is documented "any thread", the on_data hook re-nominates via task_creator::schedule which is "pure enqueue, safe from any thread" — docs/super-sirius/streaming-sessions.md:215,221), and stream_session's maps are frozen after build(). But cxx binds push_arrow as Pin<&mut Fragment>, so Rust cannot call it while run(&mut self) is in flight, and a mid-run push allocates GPU memory outside the running query's reservation. This PR ships the same "legal between build() and run()" contract as push_packed and defers the concurrent path.
- Sequencing vs the multi-CN campaign: this slice does not move TPC-H Q1/Q6 across GPUs. If reviewer bandwidth is the constraint, it should queue behind the arena / packed-exchange / cardinality slices even though it has the fewest dependencies of any of them.
- CMakeLists.txt is the only file with real upstream conflict exposure (see upstream_conflict_notes).

My 2 lines land at src/helper/type_conversions.cpp (~line 263) and test/cpp/helper/test_cudf_utils.cpp; upstream's nearest hunk is at @@ -716,6 +721,7 @@ in the test/cpp/integration block — adjacent enough for git to flag it, trivial to resolve either way. Within the campaign, the only in-family conflict is src/include/sirius_ffi.hpp and src/sirius_ffi.cpp, which the arena, packed-exchange, cardinality and pin-table-FFI slices all edit. push_arrow's declaration sits between relay_from and close_input in the header and its definition next to relay_from in the .cpp; push_packed lands in the same neighborhood, so whichever of the two goes second takes a small textual conflict. Landing this one FIRST is the cheaper order — it depends on nothing, so it can go in before the arena stack starts moving, and every later slice then rebases over a header that already has an extra method in that region.

The Rust layer (layer 3) conflicts with the rust-fragment slice by construction: it adds one method inside the `type Fragment` block that slice introduces. Stack it directly on top with `gh stack`.

No prior draft PR covers this ground — #1636-#1639 are the byte-range stack, #1644/#1598 are stream fragments and their Rust bindings, #1674/#1672/#1686 are the CN work. There is no Arrow-import PR to harvest a description from; the raw material here is issue #1590 itself.

DESCRIPTION OUTLINE:
## Motivation

Refs #1590. Apache Doris (comment by @morningman, 2026-09-01) wants to drive a Sirius fragment from a front end that already holds Arrow. Today the only ways into a fragment input stream are `relay_from` (another Sirius fragment in the same process) and, on the exchange branch, `push_packed` (cudf packed bytes staged in the exchange arena). Neither helps an Arrow-native embedder: Doris would have to write parquet to disk or reimplement cudf's pack format. `Fragment::push_arrow` closes that gap with the interchange format both sides already speak.

Worth stating: `cudf::from_arrow_host` / `cudf::to_arrow_host` are currently unused anywhere in Sirius, even though nanoarrow 0.7.0 has been in the dependency closure since cudf 26.06 (`vcpkg_ports/cudf/vcpkg.json`). This is the first use.

## What changed

1. **`sirius::helper::import_host_arrow_batch` (new, `src/helper/arrow_host_import.cpp`)** — `cudf::from_arrow_host(ArrowSchema const*, ArrowDeviceArray const*, stream, mr)`, then validate and normalize the resulting columns against the stream's declared DuckDB schema. Exact `get_cudf_type` matches pass through; arrow `decimal128` is cast down to the declared `DECIMAL64`/`DECIMAL32` and rescaled (**required**, not a nicety — `get_cudf_type` maps `DECIMAL(15,2)` to `DECIMAL64(-2)` while Arrow only has decimal128, so without this every TPC-H money column is refused); anything else mismatched throws naming stream id, column index, column name, declared type and carried type.
2. **`Fragment::push_arrow(stream_id, schema_addr, array_addr)` (`src/sirius_ffi.hpp`/`.cpp`)** — guards (`built`, non-null addresses, declared stream), import, deep copy into the engine's GPU memory space, `stream.synchronize()`, `sirius::make_data_batch(...)`, `session().push(...)`, throw when push returns `false` (a push after EOS never disappears silently). Structurally identical to `push_packed`; H2D copy is mandatory and the caller's Arrow buffers are free the moment it returns (cudf does not call `release` on the input, and neither do we).
3. **`src/include/helper/arrow_c_device_abi.hpp` (new)** — 50-line `ArrowDeviceArray` under the standard `ARROW_C_DEVICE_DATA_INTERFACE` guard. Explain why: DuckDB defines the data-interface structs but not the device ones, the conda env has `arrow/c/abi.h` but no nanoarrow, the vcpkg tree has nanoarrow but no Arrow C++ — a guarded local definition is the only include that works in both.
4. **Rust** — `unsafe fn push_arrow(...)` on the `sirius-sys` bridge, and a safe `Fragment::push_arrow(&mut self, stream_id, &RecordBatch)` that goes through `arrow_array::ffi::to_ffi`. No new crate dependency (`arrow-array 59` already has `ffi` on); `Cargo.lock` untouched.
5. **Docs** — a `### push_arrow()` subsection in `docs/super-sirius/streaming-fragments.md`, next to `relay_from`.

## How it was verified

- New Catch2 file `test/cpp/helper/test_arrow_host_import.cpp` (8 cases): round-trip fixed-width and strings tables through `cudf::to_arrow_host` -> `import_host_arrow_batch`, null-mask preservation, the decimal128 -> DECIMAL64 normalization, zero-row batch, and the three rejection paths.
- Two negative cases appended to `test/cpp/exec/test_sirius_ffi_fragment.cpp` (before-`build()`, null addresses) — that file cannot build a valid Fragment (its own header comment explains why), so the positive path lives in Rust.
- Three Rust `#[test]`s: `push_arrow_hop_matches_relay_hop` asserts an Arrow hop produces byte-identical rows to a `relay_from` hop; `push_arrow_rejects_a_mismatched_schema`; `push_arrow_of_a_zero_row_record_batch_is_accepted`.
- Run locally on <device>; CI compiles the Rust tests (`cargo test --no-run`) but does not run GPU tests.

## Intentionally not handled

- **The result direction.** `Fragment::result_to_arrow` still goes D2H -> `host_data_representation` -> `duckdb::DataChunk` -> `ColumnDataCollection` -> Arrow (`src/op/sirius_physical_result_collector.cpp:172-241`, then `ResultArrowArrayStreamWrapper`) — four copies for data that is already columnar on the GPU. `cudf::to_arrow_host` collapses that to one, and `import_host_arrow_batch`'s file is where its mirror will live. Separate PR: it changes the result-collector path that every existing consumer depends on, and mixing it in here would double the review.
- **Pushing during `run()` from another thread.** Doris asked for it; the C++ substrate already supports it (`batch_stream`'s producer side is any-thread, `on_data` -> `task_creator::schedule` is documented safe from any thread), but the cxx binding's `Pin<&mut Fragment>` forbids it from Rust and a mid-run push allocates outside the running query's reservation. Shipping the same between-`build()`-and-`run()` contract as `push_packed`; the concurrent path wants a shared `FragmentSender` handle (the `StagingArena` handle is the precedent) plus a real concurrency test, tracked in #NNNN.
- Arrow `large_string`, `string_view`, nested list/struct, and dictionary-encoded inputs: rejected with a clear error rather than silently coerced.

OPEN QUESTIONS:
- from_arrow_host vs from_arrow: `cudf::from_arrow(ArrowSchema const*, ArrowArray const*, stream, mr)` exists and is not deprecated in this cudf (interop.hpp:684), does the same H2D import, and needs NO ArrowDeviceArray — which would delete src/include/helper/arrow_c_device_abi.hpp and the whole portable-header problem, and matches what Doris naturally hands out (arrow::ExportRecordBatch yields an ArrowArray). The brief asks for from_arrow_host; is the device-array form actually wanted, or is it just the name that was reached for? Recommend asking @morningman which struct Doris will produce before writing the shim.
- Decimal overflow policy: when an Arrow decimal128 value does not fit the declared DECIMAL64, does push_arrow throw (safe, but a whole batch dies on one row) or does the cast silently wrap (matching the existing HUGEINT->INT64 narrowing FIXME in get_cudf_type)? Needs a decision before the normalization test is written.

Cheaper for the caller, but it hides per-batch backpressure and does not compose with declare_input_sender/close_input. Recommend single-batch now, note the stream variant as possible future work.

There is no src/data/ today, only src/include/data/, so src/helper/ is the option that adds no new directory.
