VERDICT NOTES: VERDICT: refuted. The primary refutation is categorical — this is not a slice of DEV..SOT. `git diff origin/dev origin/feat/pin-table-cn --numstat | grep -i arrow` is empty and a grep for from_arrow/to_arrow_host/ArrowDeviceArray/push_arrow across origin/feat/pin-table-cn's src, test and rust trees returns zero hits, so "apply the mapped hunks from SOT on DEV" produces nothing. Everything below the first finding is therefore a review of a design sketch, not of a carve.

WHAT THE MAP GOT RIGHT (verified, so these are not findings):
- DEV substrate for layers 1-2 genuinely exists: origin/dev:src/sirius_ffi.cpp has Fragment::Impl with `Context::Impl& ctx` (:227), `resolved_inputs` (:252), `session()` (:274); `sirius::make_data_batch` is at origin/dev:src/include/data/data_batch_utils.hpp:132; `sirius::invalid_input_exception` has the std::format_string ctor push_packed relies on (origin/dev:src/include/sirius/exception.hpp:47-57). So layers 1-2 have no slice dependency, as claimed.
- The decimal argument is correct and load-bearing: origin/dev:src/include/cudf/cudf_utils.hpp maps DuckDB DECIMAL precision<=18 to `cudf::data_type(cudf::type_id::DECIMAL64, neg_scale)`, so a DECIMAL(15,2) TPC-H money column is DECIMAL64(-2) while from_arrow_host yields DECIMAL128 — the normalization cast really is mandatory, not a nicety.
- cudf availability checks out: `nm -D --defined-only .pixi/envs/default/lib/libcudf.so | c++filt` exports cudf::from_arrow_host, cudf::to_arrow_host (table and column overloads) and cudf::from_arrow; vcpkg_ports/cudf/vcpkg.json (26.06.00) does list `nanoarrow`, and vcpkg_ports/nanoarrow/vcpkg.json is version 0.7.0.
- The build-mode observation is accurate: the pixi env has arrow/c/abi.h but no include/nanoarrow directory, while cudf_test/nanoarrow_utils.hpp includes <nanoarrow/nanoarrow_device.h>. (The conclusion drawn from it — that a shim is needed — is still wrong, see should_move_out.)
- No new Rust dependency needed: origin/dev:rust/crates/sirius/Cargo.toml already has `arrow-array = { version = "59", features = ["ffi"] }`, and it is byte-identical on SOT.
- CMake anchors exist: origin/dev:CMakeLists.txt:263 is src/helper/type_conversions.cpp and :710 is test/cpp/helper/test_cudf_utils.cpp. Orphan tests are caught by scripts/check_orphan_tests.py (CMakeLists.txt:907-910), and the mapped test IS registered, so that hook is satisfied.
- The upstream-conflict analysis holds: `git diff --stat origin/dev upstream-pr/dev -- src/include/sirius_ffi.hpp src/sirius_ffi.cpp rust docs/super-sirius test/cpp/exec test/cpp/helper src/helper CMakeLists.txt` touches only CMakeLists.txt (+14/-1) among the slice's files; docs/super-sirius/streaming-fragments.md is untouched upstream.
- test/cpp/exec/test_sirius_ffi_fragment.cpp's tag string really is "[isolated_context][sirius_ffi]" and its header comment (DEV :19-26) really does say the public FFI links no substrait-from-SQL helper, so the "no valid Fragment from C++" argument is sound.

MINIMUM TO MAKE IT TRUE: (1) reframe as new work with its own verification plan rather than a DEV..SOT carve; (2) settle from_arrow vs from_arrow_host BEFORE writing anything — picking from_arrow deletes both the shim file and the Rust/C++ struct mismatch in one move; (3) drop layer 3 to a follow-up, or map in write_users_parquet, stream_read_plan_f64 and into_arrow and re-declare the dependency against the landed replacement for #1598 rather than the draft branch.

SEQUENCING: the map itself concedes this does not move TPC-H Q1/Q6 across GPUs. Given the stated campaign priority, and given that layers 1-2 touch the same sirius_ffi.hpp/.cpp neighbourhood that the arena, packed-exchange, cardinality and pin-table slices all edit, the "land this FIRST because it depends on nothing" recommendation trades a real conflict cost against a deliverable that is off the critical path.


######## SLICE hash-join-wedge (Item 3): hash-join never-buildable / build-only slot resolution, the partition zero-byte strategy that lets an empty sizing side reach BUILD_PROBE, plus the two liveness primitives they sit on (zero-task query completion, opt-in query watchdog)

SUMMARY: On SOT this slice fixes the class of silent hangs a hash-partitioned exchange creates: when every build (or probe) row for an instance hashed to another CN, one side of a BUILD_PROBE hash join finishes with ZERO batches and the scheduler answers wait_for_build / wait_for_probe forever against an already-finished producer. Three coupled changes: (a) `broadcast_slots_to_discard` is generalized to `build_only_slots_to_discard` (applies to every BUILD_PROBE join, not just broadcast) and gains a mirror `never_buildable_slots` / `resolve_never_buildable_slots` that tears down slots whose build batch can never arrive — silently for INNER/SEMI, loudly for LEFT/ANTI/MARK where probe rows must still be emitted; (b) `sirius_physical_partition::get_next_task_hint()` runs the consumer's real `get_partition_strategy` with zero bytes when the sizing side finishes empty, instead of the ad-hoc `_num_partitions = 1` election that left the join in STANDARD mode where the resolution code is unreachable (the "masking election defect" in notes/2026-08-07-sf1-l4/QUERY-TIMEOUT-ANALYSIS.md); (c) two liveness primitives the fix and its tests need — `task_scheduler::complete_query_if_finished()` for a query that finishes with no task ever created, and the opt-in `SIRIUS_QUERY_WATCHDOG_SECS` engine watchdog that turns a wedge into a loud failure. Priority-wise: the hash-join wedge itself does NOT block Q1/Q6 (Q1/Q6 have no joins per notes/2026-08-07-sf1-l4/TPCH-PLAN-ANALYSIS.md), but the zero-task completion fix DOES — it is the empty-hash-partition hang on the two-phase-aggregate merge fragment, which is exactly the Q1/Q6 many-CN shape. The join wedge unblocked TPC-H q02 (the 30s deterministic engine-thread stall, "wedge" in the SF1/L4 sweep) and q07 (~33s hang, 3/3 deterministic, 2-CN); both now pass in the SOT bench tables (bench/rtxpro6000-2gpu/TPCH-STATUS.md: q02 512/573 ms exact, q07 1415/1420 ms).

SIZE~860  PR TITLE: fix(join): resolve hash-join slots that can never be built or probed

DEPENDS_ON: none-hard — every file in this slice compiles and every test runs against origin/dev as-is (verified: partition_sizing_input, get_partition_strategy, snapshot_build_probe_slots, per_partition_build_state, streaming_fragment/stream_session::close_input, query_window, drain_row_count, lineitem_parquet_path, sirius_pipeline::get_tasks_created/get_tasks_completed/is_query_terminal, task_creator::_task_scheduler all exist on DEV); internal (layer ordering): layer 3 (join wedge) depends on layer 2 (watchdog), because FRAG-6..FRAG-9 use the `watchdog_guard` RAII helper to set SIRIUS_QUERY_WATCHDOG_SECS=20/30 — without it a regression hangs CI forever instead of failing in seconds; internal (layer ordering, soft): layer 1 (zero-task completion) should land first — it is independent, tiny, and is the Q1/Q6-relevant piece (an empty hash-fanout partition on the two-phase-aggregate merge fragment); leaving it for last delays the highest-priority fix behind the largest PR; soft, against upstream: rebase after upstream #1606 (b8e8c83b, dense-count join) which changes partition_sizing_input's arity and adds a block above the edited hint-path region in sirius_physical_partition.cpp; PR #1644 (origin/stream-fragment-execution) already carries layer 1 verbatim (task_creator.cpp + task_scheduler::complete_query_if_finished) inside a 29-commit draft. Its description is raw material; if #1644 is closed as planned, layer 1 must be re-landed here.

LAYERS:
- fix(pipeline): complete a query that finished before any task ran: files=['src/creator/task_creator.cpp', 'src/pipeline/task_scheduler.cpp', 'src/include/pipeline/task_scheduler.hpp', 'docs/super-sirius/task-creator.md'] | ~55 lines. Independent root cause and the Q1/Q6-priority piece: a hash fan-out gives one CN zero keys, its input stream closes with zero batches between build() and run(), the pipeline finishes BEFORE prepare_for_query() installs the completion handler, no task is ever created, and execute() blocks forever. DEV's 5737ad7f (#1624) does NOT cover this: it signals from update_pipeline_status()'s finish TRANSITION, and here there is no transition during the query — the pipeline was already finished when the query started. Verify and then simplify against #1624: get_operator_for_next_task() already pushes the scheduled node's own pipeline into visited_pipelines, so the new `pipeline->update_pipeline_status(false)` call is likely redundant post-#1624 and the hunk may reduce to the all_ports_empty() gate + slot release + complete_query_if_finished(). Also rewrite the SOT comment's claim that 'every other completion signal rides on a task finishing' — #1624 made that false. Coordinate with open upstream #1642 (Jedi18, completion quiescence / work ledger), which is the declared follow-up to #1624 and touches the same signalling contract: this PR's non-drain, first-call-wins mark_completed() is compatible with a ledger, but say so explicitly in the description.
- feat(engine): opt-in query watchdog that fails a stalled query loudly: files=['src/sirius_engine.cpp', 'src/pipeline/task_scheduler.cpp', 'src/include/pipeline/task_scheduler.hpp', 'docs/super-sirius/configuration.md'] | ~95 lines. Off by default (SIRIUS_QUERY_WATCHDOG_SECS unset/0 keeps the exact current blocking future.get()), so it is a pure addition with zero behavior change for existing users. Must land before layer 3 because FRAG-6..FRAG-9 use it via `watchdog_guard` to turn a regression into a seconds-long failure instead of a hung CI job. Its dependents live in other slices and reference it by name: experimental/starrocks/docs/TUNABLES.md, experimental/starrocks/pixi.toml (60), configs/gb200-4gpu/engine-a.env + cluster4-numa.sh, configs/gb200-8gpu/relaunch.sh, bench/gb200-8gpu/sf{1000,3000,10000}/env.sh (300/600/1800), bench/rtxpro6000-2gpu (90), .claude/skills/tpch-bench/SKILL.md. Be honest in the description about the known limitation those docs already record: it is a no-scheduling-progress watchdog, so an in-flight long kernel makes no counter progress and a too-low value fails healthy queries; the value must sit strictly below the client query timeout to fire at all.
- fix(join): resolve hash-join slots that can never be built or probed: files=['src/include/op/sirius_physical_hash_join.hpp', 'src/op/sirius_physical_hash_join.cpp', 'src/op/sirius_physical_partition.cpp', 'test/cpp/operator/test_build_probe_scheduling.cpp', 'test/cpp/exec/test_streaming_fragment.cpp', 'test/cpp/utils/pipeline_conversion_test_utils.hpp', 'docs/super-sirius/operators.md'] | ~710 lines (≈320 production, ≈380 test after stripping the FRAG-9 cerr dump, ≈10 docs). The join fix and the partition zero-byte strategy MUST ship together: splitting them leaves a state that fixes nothing end-to-end. Without the partition change an empty sizing side elects 1 partition in STANDARD mode, `_partition_build_states` is never sized, and resolve_never_buildable_slots() is unreachable; without the join change the partition change merely moves the wedge from STANDARD into BUILD_PROBE. FRAG-6's two SECTIONs prove exactly this pairing: 'probe side closes empty' exercises the generalized build-only discard alone, 'build side closes empty' additionally needs the zero-byte strategy.

RISKS:
- Lock ordering / re-entrancy: the new zero-byte path calls `consumer->get_partition_strategy()` (which takes the hash join's op_state_mutex) from the task creator's MANAGER thread while holding the non-driver partition's `lock`, then takes `sizing_partition.lock` afterwards. The task-time path takes both partition locks together via `std::scoped_lock` and then op_state_mutex. Reviewers will ask about deadlock; the answer is that scoped_lock's std::lock back-off makes the pair acquisition order-free and the two paths are mutually exclusive on the partition locks, but this needs stating in the description.
- `get_partition_strategy()` is side-effecting and NOT idempotent: it flips `_join_mode` to BUILD_PROBE, whole-move-assigns a fresh `_partition_build_states` vector, sets `_broadcast`, and pre-sizes both input repos. Calling it a second time on a live join would drop hash-table state. The `!_num_partitions.has_value()` guard under the partition lock is the only thing preventing that — make the invariant explicit.
- The manager thread must never throw: the hint path deliberately returns `TaskCreationHint::READY` for the LEFT/ANTI/MARK case and re-detects it in `get_next_task_input_data()` so the throw lands inside the task creator's try/catch. This double-call of `resolve_never_buildable_slots()` is subtle and will draw review fire — it also means the pass runs twice per decision, and the second run re-drains `_never_buildable_destroyed_slots` again (correct but wasteful).
- `_never_buildable_destroyed_slots` and `_build_only_discarded_slots` grow unboundedly (`push_back` with no dedup) and are re-drained on EVERY hint pass. For a many-partition join under a busy scheduler this is O(passes × recorded slots) repo pops. Bounded in practice by the partition count, but worth a comment or a `std::set`.
- Silent row-dropping is now a code path: INNER/SEMI slots with a never-arriving build side have their parked probe batches freed. If `never_buildable_slots` ever fires while build data is genuinely still in flight (i.e. `is_pipeline_finished()` lies), rows disappear with no error. The LEFT/ANTI/MARK branch fails loudly precisely because it cannot be expressed as a drop — reviewers should be pointed at that asymmetry.
- The zero-byte `partition_sizing_input{0, ...}` feeds `compute_hash_join_partition_strategy` a total_bytes of 0. Confirm on DEV that this yields build_probe=true for the join types under test rather than falling into the small-table single-partition floor — FRAG-6/7/8/9 are the evidence, but the behavior is not unit-tested at the `compute_hash_join_partition_strategy` level and should be.
- Layer 2's watchdog polls `future.wait_for(250ms)` in a loop instead of blocking. On a healthy long query this is 4 wakeups/second on the engine thread for the whole query. Negligible, but it is a change to the default-path control flow even when the watchdog is off — actually no: when watchdog_secs==0 the code takes an early `future.get()` return, so the default path is byte-identical. Say this explicitly, it is the first thing a reviewer will check.
- FRAG-6..FRAG-9 are GPU integration tests that run real queries; FRAG-9 additionally reads the in-repo lineitem.parquet. If a regression reappears they rely on the layer-2 watchdog firing within 20-30s — if CI runs on a slow GPU where the legitimate query exceeds that, they become flaky. Consider raising the guard values or making them CI-configurable.

DESCRIPTION OUTLINE:
## Motivation

A hash-partitioned exchange can hand one instance a side of a join with ZERO batches — every build (or probe) row hashed to another compute node. Today that wedges the query, silently and forever.

Two concrete shapes, both reproduced in-process by the tests in this PR:

- The probe stream closes without ever delivering a batch for a partition. The slot holds build data, `select_build_probe_action` answers `wait_for_probe` against an already-finished producer, and nothing ever completes. `discard_build_only_slots_if_probe_complete()` exists for exactly this, but is gated on `_broadcast` — so it only rescues broadcast joins, not the partitioned case.
- The build stream closes without ever delivering a batch. There is no terminal transition at all: the state machine answers `wait_for_build` forever. And a second defect masks it — when the sizing side finishes empty, `sirius_physical_partition::get_next_task_hint()` elects `_num_partitions = 1` out of band, which leaves the join in STANDARD mode where the BUILD_PROBE resolution code is unreachable.

Observed as TPC-H q02 (deterministic ~30 s engine-thread stall, the one non-passing query in the SF1 2-CN sweep) and q07 (~33 s hang, 3/3 deterministic on 2 CNs). Both pass after this change.

## What changed

**`build_only_slots_to_discard` (was `broadcast_slots_to_discard`)** — same predicate, no longer broadcast-only. Every BUILD_PROBE-eligible join type (INNER/SEMI/LEFT/ANTI/MARK) emits only probe-derived rows, so a slot with no probe rows and none coming produces nothing regardless of how its build data got there. `discard_build_only_slots_if_probe_complete()` drops its `if (!_broadcast) return;`.

**`never_buildable_slots` / `resolve_never_buildable_slots()`** — the mirror. Once the build upstream is finished, a NOT_BUILT slot with no build batch can never be built. For INNER/SEMI an empty build means the partition emits nothing, so its parked probe batches are freed and the slot is DESTROYED. For LEFT/ANTI/MARK the probe rows MUST still be emitted, which this path cannot do, so it fails the query loudly rather than dropping rows — the failing index is returned from the hint path (which runs on the task creator's unguarded manager thread and must not throw) and re-detected in `get_next_task_input_data()`, where the throw lands inside the task creator's try/catch.

**Re-drain on every pass** — build/probe tasks race the hint pass, so a batch can land in an already-torn-down partition. `_never_buildable_destroyed_slots` and `_build_only_discarded_slots` record the torn-down slots and every pass re-drains them; without this a late batch keeps `all_ports_empty()` false forever and the pipeline can never finish.

**The zero-byte partition strategy** — when the sizing side finishes empty, ask the consumer for a real strategy with `total_bytes = 0` instead of electing a count out of band, so a hash join actually reaches BUILD_PROBE and its resolution can run. The old one-partition election survives for the no-consumer case (logged, not thrown — this runs on the manager thread). `has_build_concat` / `enable_build_concat_all` are hoisted from lambdas inside `get_next_task_input_data()` to file-scope statics so both paths share them; pure move, no behavior change.

## How it was verified

- Unit: six pure `[build_probe]` Catch2 cases over `build_probe_slot_view` vectors (three renamed, three new for `never_buildable_slots`). No GPU.
- Integration, in-process, no compute node: FRAG-6 (a join whose input stream closes empty, both directions as SECTIONs — the planner may swap build/probe), FRAG-7 (join cascade, empty stream feeds an upper join whose probe is another join's output), FRAG-8 (the q07-cn1 fragment shape: empty lower build, live upper build, grouped aggregate, hash-partitioned sink), FRAG-9 (the same at full q07 fidelity — real fp64/DATE/VARCHAR types over the in-repo lineitem.parquet). Each hung before this change; each completes with the correct empty result after.
- Cluster: TPC-H 22/22 on a 2-CN SF1 sweep, and q02/q07 pass in the 2-GPU SF100 tables.

## Not handled here

- Cancellation. A wedge that this PR does not cover still cannot be cancelled; the opt-in `SIRIUS_QUERY_WATCHDOG_SECS` watchdog (previous PR in this stack) turns it into a loud failure, which is what the FRAG tests rely on, but real query abort is separate work.
- MIXED_JOIN / STANDARD-mode empty sides. This PR resolves the BUILD_PROBE state machine only.
- The `_num_partitions = 1` fallback is kept, not removed, for consumers that are not partition-sizing consumers.

OPEN QUESTIONS:
- Should layer 1 (zero-task completion) be folded into the exchange-runtime slice instead of standing alone? Argument for standing alone: it is ~55 lines, has an independent root cause, and is the only piece of this slice that Q1/Q6 actually need (Q1/Q6 have no joins — TPCH-PLAN-ANALYSIS.md Q1: 'Joins: none'), so it should not queue behind a 700-line join PR. Argument for folding: its only real regression tests are the Rust GPU tests, which need the unmerged Rust Fragment bindings.
- How much of layer 1 survives DEV's 5737ad7f (#1624)? get_operator_for_next_task() already pushes the scheduled head's own pipeline into visited_pipelines, and the existing loop already calls update_pipeline_status(false) on it — post-#1624 that call itself signals completion on a finish transition. The residual gap is only the already-finished-before-the-query case (the end-of-stream hook fires between build() and run(), before prepare_for_query() installs the handler). Needs a live check on DEV before writing the description: does the hunk reduce to just the all_ports_empty() gate + slot release + complete_query_if_finished()?
- Does layer 1 conflict with open upstream #1642 (Jedi18, completion quiescence / work ledger)? #1624's own description names `fix/completion-quiescence-protocol` as the follow-up that will establish a quiescence contract for externally-driven producer callbacks. complete_query_if_finished() deliberately does NOT drain (the caller is the manager thread and a drain would self-deadlock on its own pool slot). If #1642 makes draining mandatory before mark_completed(), these two designs collide — worth a comment on #1642 before opening layer 1. I could not read #1642 from here (GitHub API rate-limited, no gh CLI).
- Should FRAG-6..FRAG-9 ship in the join PR or in the exchange/streaming-fragment PR? They live in test/cpp/exec/test_streaming_fragment.cpp, which is a streaming-fragment test file already registered in CMakeLists on DEV, but they test join scheduling. Splitting the file across two PRs risks a merge conflict on the append point; keeping them here is cleaner but puts 450 test lines in a join PR.
- Is layer 3 reviewable in one sitting at ~710 lines? A 4-layer alternative exists — 3a: hash_join.{hpp,cpp} + the six unit tests + FRAG-6's 'probe side closes empty' SECTION (~270 lines); 3b: sirius_physical_partition.cpp + FRAG-6's other SECTION + FRAG-7/8/9 (~440 lines). It works (3a alone fixes the probe-empty case end-to-end) but requires splitting FRAG-6's two SECTIONs across PRs, which is ugly. Caller's call.
- Should the watchdog (layer 2) be dropped and FRAG-6..9 rely on a plain CI timeout instead? That would shrink the stack by one PR but means a regression hangs the job rather than failing it in 20 s with a named error. Recommend keeping it, but the watchdog's own limitations (fails healthy queries whose kernels exceed the threshold) mean it will draw more review discussion than its size suggests.
- Confirm no upstream doc besides docs/super-sirius/operators.md:237 describes the discard as broadcast-only. I grepped docs/ and found only that line, but experimental/starrocks and notes/ were not audited for this slice.
- Verify on DEV that compute_hash_join_partition_strategy(total_bytes=0, is_build_side=true, build_foldable=true, ...) actually returns build_probe=true for INNER/SEMI at num_gpus>1. FRAG-6..9 are the end-to-end evidence, but there is no direct unit test of the zero-byte input and this is the linchpin of the partition change — consider adding one to test_build_probe_scheduling.cpp.

Every symbol the mapped hunks use exists on origin/dev, verified individually: sirius_physical_operator::all_ports_empty (src/include/op/sirius_physical_operator.hpp:757, public virtual) and the `slot` local in manager_loop (src/creator/task_creator.cpp, `auto slot = _bounded_pool->reserve()`); task_creator::_task_scheduler (src/include/creator/task_creator.hpp:224); completion_handler::mark_completed (src/include/pipeline/completion_handler.hpp:91) and report_error(std::string_view) (:72); task_scheduler::_query_mutex/_query/_completion_handler (src/include/pipeline/task_scheduler.hpp:199/200/223); sirius_pipeline::is_query_terminal (:194), get_tasks_created (:200), get_tasks_completed (:201); SiriusContext::get_query (src/include/sirius_context.hpp:531) and planner::query::get_pipelines (src/include/planner/query.hpp:87); invalid_input_exception already used in sirius_engine.cpp (:99). partition_sizing_input is THREE fields on DEV (src/include/op/sirius_physical_partition_consumer_operator.hpp:31-36), so the new aggregate init compiles as written; partition_strategy{num_partitions,broadcast,build_probe} (:41-44); get_partition_strategy (:111). repo->size(p) is already used at DEV src/op/sirius_physical_hash_join.cpp:977. std::format is available (<format> at hash_join.cpp:67) and duckdb::JoinTypeToString is used at :355. <optional> is in hash_join.hpp:43. SIRIUS_LOG_WARN is in scope in partition.cpp (log/logging.hpp at :25, used at :329). stream_input_spec is THREE fields on DEV (src/include/exec/streaming_fragment.hpp:41-46), so every FRAG-6..9 aggregate init compiles; query_window, drain_row_count, drain_values, sql_plan_source, lineitem_parquet_path, engine().sirius_pipelines all live in the DEV half of the test file. Both test files are already registered (CMakeLists.txt:695 and :783), so no CMake hunk is needed.

COMPLETENESS CHECKS THAT PASSED. The map's claim that it carries the WHOLE DEV..SOT delta of each shared file is true for hash_join.hpp (49/11, 3 hunks), hash_join.cpp (132/23), partition.cpp (84/36), task_creator.cpp (26/1), task_scheduler.hpp (24/0), task_scheduler.cpp (29/0), sirius_engine.cpp (65/1), test_build_probe_scheduling.cpp (58/8) and pipeline_conversion_test_utils.hpp (1/0) — I read each diff end to end; nothing from another slice is swept in and no hunk is omitted. The rename is complete: `git grep broadcast_slots_to_discard origin/dev` returns exactly hash_join.hpp:140, hash_join.cpp:983 and :1012, plus six hits in test_build_probe_scheduling.cpp, all covered by the mapped hunks; no other file on DEV or SOT references the old name.

TWO MAP CLAIMS I INDEPENDENTLY CONFIRMED. (1) The FRAG tests really do need only DEV infrastructure: the file's delta is a pure EOF append, so the fragment_fixture — including whatever optimizer setting FRAG-7's 'optimizer disabled' comment relies on — is byte-identical to DEV. (2) The exclusion of the streaming_fragment.cpp DOUBLE/FLOAT derive_key_cast_type hunk holds: FRAG-8 partitions on {0,1} = two INTEGERs, FRAG-9 on {0,1,2} = VARCHAR/VARCHAR/BIGINT(year()); the DOUBLE revenue column is index 3 and is not a key. (3) The watchdog really does cover the FRAG tests — streaming_fragment::run() calls _engine->execute() (src/exec/streaming_fragment.cpp), which is where wait_for_query_future is spliced in.

ONE RISK THE MAP DOES NOT LIST, worth a sentence in the PR description. The base sirius_physical_partition_consumer_operator::get_partition_strategy THROWS (src/op/sirius_physical_partition_consumer_operator.cpp:39-44, 'called on a non-sizing partition consumer operator'), and the new zero-byte call site runs on the task creator's UNGUARDED manager thread behind only a `consumer == nullptr` check. It is unreachable today — _downstream_consumer_op is only ever assigned for HASH_JOIN / NESTED_LOOP_JOIN / MERGE_GROUP_BY (src/op/sirius_physical_partition.cpp:100, :135, :155; HASH_GROUP_BY deliberately leaves it nullptr), and the enclosing block additionally requires _sibling_partition_op != nullptr, i.e. joins only — but nothing in the new code states that invariant, and any future sizing consumer that does not override get_partition_strategy turns this into an uncaught exception on the manager thread. The `consumer == nullptr` fallback should be widened to 'not a sizing consumer', or the invariant asserted in a comment.

BOTTOM LINE. I could not break the slice's own compile, and I have no way to run the GPU tests, so on the narrow reading ('DEV + these hunks builds and its tests run') I found no counterexample. I return self_contained=false on the instructed default because of the unresolved FRAG-10/watchdog_guard cross-reference and the shared append hunk: the stack as mapped, in the order proposed, cannot be applied without either duplicating watchdog_guard in the cardinality slice or reordering it behind this one. Confidence is high for the static analysis, medium for the FRAG-6..9 pass claim.


######## SLICE two-phase-agg-exchange (Item 5) — two-phase aggregation translation + CN exchange runtime + engine aggregate determinism

SUMMARY: This slice is everything that turns one StarRocks fragment per GPU into a working distributed aggregation: the translator learns to classify an FE aggregation node's phase and to lower an EXCHANGE_NODE to a native engine stream read; the compute node learns to park a sender fragment's output on the GPU, rendezvous it with a receiver whose sender set is complete, and dispatch that receiver off the RPC thread; and the C++ engine learns to make float sums bit-stable and to refuse an int64 SUM that could wrap. It is the direct enabler of the Q1/Q6-on-many-GPUs goal: without it the FE must be driven with `SET new_planner_agg_stage = 1`, which forces one finalized aggregation instance and therefore one GPU. Q6 needs the ungrouped partial→gather→merge path plus the decimal→FP64 lowering; Q1 needs all of that plus grouped two-phase over a hash-partitioned fan-out, the avg sum/count state expansion, the merge finalizing projection, and the aggregation-output/sort tuple materialization order. It supersedes mbrobbel's #1242 (materialized-parquet exchanges): rows never leave the device — a local hop is `Fragment::relay_from`, a cross-CN hop is packed cudf bytes, and nothing is re-parsed. At roughly 10.5k changed lines it cannot be one PR; the proposal below is a six-layer stack, each layer independently buildable, testable and reviewable in one sitting.

SIZE~10500  PR TITLE: feat(starrocks): two-phase aggregation over native GPU exchanges

DEPENDS_ON: ffi-fragment-rust (= draft PR #1598 / origin/stream/16-rust-fragment): rust/crates/sirius/src/lib.rs on origin/dev has NO Fragment type at all (257 lines vs 2835 on SOT). Every CN layer (L5, L6) and the L2 Rust half need Fragment::{declare_input_column, declare_input_sender, declare_output, declare_output_broadcast, declare_output_hash_key, build, relay_from, close_input, run, into_arrow, output_types, output_batch_count} plus stream_view_name(). The C++ side of these IS already merged in DEV via #1481.; decimal-fp64 + expression lowering (mbrobbel #1236 and siblings): Q6's sum(l_extendedprice*(1-l_discount)) and all four of Q1's sums go through translate_arithmetic's decimal->FP64 lowering and type_mapper's DECIMAL precision>18 -> Fp64 rule. Without them the translator still refuses decimal arithmetic ('crashes the engine projection') and neither Q1 nor Q6 translates. Must land BELOW this stack even though it is excluded from it.; CLONE_EXPR handling (expr_translator::translate_clone) and the year/month/day/length/char_length declared-return-type cast: the FE emits CLONE_EXPR whenever a projection maps two output slots to the same input column, and a merge receiver derives its stream schema from FE slot types, so a BIGINT-returning builtin is refused at the next hop. Small; either its own PR or folded into the decimal-fp64 one.; common-slot carrying (mbrobbel #1233): not needed by Q1/Q6, but node_translator's TranslatedRel gains a carried_slots field that every construction site touches. Landing it FIRST avoids rebasing every TranslatedRel literal in L3/L4; landing it after means one mechanical conflict per relation constructor.; staging-arena slice (src/exec/exchange_staging_arena.{hpp,cpp}, Context::staging_* + StagingArena + Fragment::export_packed/push_packed on the FFI): required by the nixl slice, NOT by L1-L6 as scoped. Order it after L5.; nixl-transport slice: strictly ABOVE L5 (it adds SenderSource::Remote, push_remote_frame, StagedBatch, SenderDrains/DrainTicket, dispatch_then_join, transmit_packed/exchange_nixl_md/request_staging_lease and the remote arm of route_destination). It is, however, on the critical path for the actual Q1/Q6-on-8-GPUs goal: one CN per GPU means every peer is remote by the host+port identity rule.

LAYERS:
- L1 — perf/fix(aggregate): bit-stable float sums and a loud int64 SUM overflow guard: files=['src/include/op/aggregate/aggregate_op_util.hpp', 'src/op/aggregate/aggregate_op_util.cpp', 'src/op/aggregate/gpu_aggregate_impl.cpp', 'src/op/merge/gpu_merge_impl.cpp', 'src/op/sirius_physical_ungrouped_aggregate.cpp', 'test/cpp/operator/aggregate/test_gpu_merge_impl.cpp'] | Pure C++ engine change, ~530 lines, no CMakeLists edit, zero dependency on anything else in the stack — it can be reviewed and merged in parallel with L2-L6 and is the only layer a non-StarRocks reviewer can evaluate alone. Two independent concerns share the same helper file: canonical row order routing float SUMs onto cuDF's atomics-free sort-based groupby (so a partial depends only on its batch's row multiset), and throw_if_int64_sum_could_overflow guarding the HUGEINT->BIGINT downcast. If a reviewer objects, split into L1a (determinism) and L1b (overflow guard); the only shared surface is the new header. Directly Q1-relevant: every Q1 sum is FP64 after the decimal lowering, and the merge is fed by N CNs in nondeterministic arrival order.
- L2 — feat(exec): declare exchange input cardinality to the optimizer: files=['src/include/exec/stream_bind_catalog.hpp', 'src/exec/stream_bind_catalog.cpp', 'src/exec/stream_plan_bindings.cpp', 'src/include/exec/streaming_fragment.hpp', 'src/exec/streaming_fragment.cpp', 'src/include/sirius_ffi.hpp', 'src/sirius_ffi.cpp', 'test/cpp/exec/test_stream_bind_catalog.cpp', 'test/cpp/exec/test_streaming_fragment.cpp', 'rust/crates/sirius-sys/src/lib.rs', 'rust/crates/sirius/src/lib.rs'] | This is upstream-ready commit 55e63371 minus its nixl/export_packed rows field, ~350 lines. Purely additive: an undeclared stream keeps today's behavior. Without it every stream source binds at cardinality 1, so a receiver fragment picks hash-join build sides blind — the q07 2-CN regression built on a multi-GB stream while a 2-row stream probed. Q1's merge fragment reads one stream, so it does not strictly need this, but any multi-CN plan with a join over an exchange does, and it is cheap and self-contained. Rust half depends on ffi-fragment-rust; the C++ half does not, so it can be split if #1598 lags.
- L3 — feat(starrocks): translate EXCHANGE_NODE as a native engine stream read: files=['experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/descriptor_table.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/type_mapper.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/lib.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/tests/translate.rs'] | ~900 src + ~400 test. The fragment-boundary primitive, and the direct supersession of mbrobbel's #1242: instead of materializing an exchange to parquet, translate_exchange emits a ReadRel over the engine's `sirius_stream_<id>` view, records the schema on TranslatedFragment.stream_inputs so the caller can declare it, and threads ExchangeInput (node_id, stream_view, sender names) in from the CN. Carries the pieces the read needs: named_struct_for_tuples for a multi-tuple exchange row, duckdb_type_name for the engine declaration, the merging-exchange SortRel, and the whole output_partition_columns block in lib.rs (bare-SLOT_REF-only hash keys, one leg of the cross-sender hash-parity contract). Ships without any aggregation phase change: EXCHANGE_NODE becomes translatable, two-phase aggregation still refuses. Consider peeling the descriptor_table slot_global_index slot-id fallback into a 60-line PR beneath this.
- L4 — feat(starrocks): two-phase aggregation translation: files=['experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/agg_phase.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/partial_state.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/expr_translator.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/type_mapper.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/lib.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/tests/translate.rs', 'rust/crates/sirius/src/lib.rs'] | ~1200 src + ~1900 translator tests + ~760 lines of GPU merge-shape oracle in the sirius crate. The heart of the slice, and the layer most likely to need splitting again: (4a) agg_phase.rs classifier replacing BOTH legacy guards atomically + partial_state.rs wire-type model + the Partial/Merge translation with function substitution (count->sum) and the merge finalizing projection; (4b) the avg expansion (expand_avg, PartialExpansion, expanded_output_names, merge_state_columns, avg_from_state) plus the materialization-order fixes (grouping_materialization_order, sort_materialization_order). Q6 needs only 4a; Q1 needs both, because Q1 has three avg measures and a GROUP BY/ORDER BY whose FE key order is not the output tuple's slot order. The atomicity of the guard replacement is non-negotiable: the node-level need_finalize check alone does not stop a merge node (new-optimizer plans always set intermediate_tuple_id == output_tuple_id), so a half-landed 4a would silently double-aggregate.
- L5 — feat(starrocks-cn): multi-fragment execution with an on-GPU local exchange: files=['experimental/starrocks/src/local_exchange.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/fragment_executor.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/engine.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/compute_node_service.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/result_store.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/lib.rs'] | ~2900 lines, the CN runtime. FragmentExecutor::execute(&TranslatedPlan) becomes run(FragmentRun) -> Option<FragmentResult>: an intermediate fragment parks its output once (one output stream per destination) and a receiver relays it in with Fragment::relay_from — no Arrow, no copy, rows stay on the device. LocalExchange is the receiver-first rendezvous StarRocks dispatch requires (the FE dispatches the receiver before the senders finish). A dedicated fragment-dispatch thread takes ready receivers off the RPC threads so the sender RPC returns immediately, and ResultStore grows reserve/wait_ready/fail_query so an intermediate fragment's failure reaches the result instances the FE actually polls instead of wedging fetch_data for 600 s. Ships nixl-free: DestinationRoute::Remote is a loud error naming the missing transport, so this layer is single-process multi-fragment only. Recommend splitting into 5a (fragment_executor + engine + local_exchange: the park/relay mechanics, ~1300) and 5b (compute_node_service + result_store: dispatch, rendezvous wiring, failure propagation, ~1600).
- L6 — test(starrocks-cn): engine-derived conformance gate for the partial-state wire types: files=['experimental/starrocks/src/wire_type_parity.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/lib.rs'] | ~730 lines, all test, GPU + engine gated. Closes the one hole L4 cannot close by itself: partial_state::wire_columns is a hand-written Rust mirror of what the engine binds, and nothing mechanical kept the two in agreement. For every model row it translates the real partial and merge fragments and compares the engine's own Fragment::output_types against the receiver's declared stream schema — the exact comparison relay_from/push_packed make on a live hop. A drift becomes a CI failure instead of a refused hop (or, before the hop guards existed, reinterpreted bits). Small, mechanical, and the natural place to point a reviewer who is nervous about L4's type model.

RISKS:
- L1's canonicalize_row_order adds a full cudf::sorted_order + gather over the input table on EVERY grouped aggregate that has any FLOAT32/FLOAT64 SUM — and because the translator lowers DECIMAL(p>18) to FP64, that is effectively every TPC-H aggregate on the StarRocks path (q01, q03, q05, q09, q10, ...). It also disables the STRING dict-encode optimization on that path and forces the sort-based groupby instead of the hash groupby. No before/after measurement exists anywhere in the tree. This must be benchmarked (q01/q09, SF100, single GPU) before upstreaming; if the cost is material, gate it behind a config flag rather than making it unconditional.
- L1's determinism guarantee is per-operator-invocation, and the invocation is per BATCH (sirius_physical_grouped_aggregate.cpp loops over input batches). notes/2026-08-20-rtx-sf500/PLAN-07-q15-float-determinism.md is explicit that this exact code shipped on the campaign branch and q15 STILL flakes: if the same rows are cut into different batches between two runs, the partial multiset differs and the canonical merge faithfully produces a different canonical total. The PR description must NOT claim it fixes q15; it makes each stage bit-stable and leaves row->batch decomposition out of the guarantee.
- throw_if_int64_sum_could_overflow is a behavior change on a hot path: a query that previously wrapped silently now throws, and the bound is deliberately conservative (rows * max(|min|,|max|)), so it can refuse a sum that would have fit. There is NO test for it anywhere in the delta. A user summing large BIGINTs sees a new hard failure with only a suggestion to cast to DOUBLE.
- L5 as scoped is single-process: it lets one CN run a multi-fragment plan, which is exactly what Q1/Q6 across many GPUs does NOT need, because one CN per GPU makes every peer remote under ExchangeIdentity's host+port rule. Landed alone it is architecture with no user-visible win until the nixl slice lands on top. State that plainly in the PR rather than implying multi-GPU works.
- L5's engine.rs parks a live Fragment (holding GPU memory) per destination with a claim count, and drops it when the last destination releases. A receiver that never arrives — a cancelled or failed query — pins that memory for the process lifetime; there is no timeout or GC. The poisoned map and drop_parked cover the failure paths the nixl slice exercises, but the pure-local layer has no eviction story.
- L4's avg expansion changes the fragment's output ROW WIDTH relative to the FE's tuple, which every consumer of the descriptor table would otherwise mis-index. The guards are real (partial expansion must be the fragment root; no conjuncts above it; no output_exprs; hash-partition keys must be grouping keys) but they are a fence around a genuinely fragile invariant. A plan shape that slips past them reads the wrong column — a wrong number, not an error.
- L4 relies on `merge(count) = sum`. If the substitution is ever dropped or mis-keyed, the plan is still valid Substrait and the engine executes it happily — counting arriving rows instead of summing partial counts. Silently wrong, no schema guard catches it. L6 does not catch it either (it checks types, not values); only merge_aggregation_translates_with_substituted_functions and the merge-shape GPU oracle do.
- L3 changes the meaning of `TranslatedPlan` for every existing caller (two new public fields, a new translate_fragment_with_exchange_inputs entry point). Any out-of-tree consumer of the translator crate breaks at compile time — acceptable, but worth naming.
- The stack's Q1/Q6 payoff depends on excluded work (decimal->FP64 lowering, CLONE_EXPR, the FE-narrowed-builtin cast). Landing L1-L6 without those means the stack is correct and tested but Q1 and Q6 still do not translate. Sequence the mbrobbel fixes underneath or the stack is unfalsifiable end to end.

UPSTREAM: upstream-pr/dev (84ea4ab5) is 12 commits ahead of DEV and does NOT touch the StarRocks translator or CN at all (only experimental/starrocks/Cargo.lock moved, via the syn 3.0.3->3.0.4 bump #1676). So L3, L4, L5 and L6 rebase cleanly onto upstream dev today; the only care needed is regenerating Cargo.lock.\n\nThe one real conflict is L1 against #1605 (54506fa0, 'perf(aggregate): compute COUNT DISTINCT group labels via distinct + key_remapping'). Both change src/op/aggregate/gpu_aggregate_impl.cpp at the SAME anchor: upstream's hunk is `@@ -139,41 +142,10 @@` inside local_grouped_aggregate, which deletes the 35-line 'Single-label group keys for the sorted-groupby path' comment block and swaps cudf::encode for detail::make_group_key_labels; the SOT hunk is `@@ -139,6 +142,32 @@` at the same line, inserting the float_sum_value_cols scan + canonicalize_row_order call immediately ABOVE that comment. Resolution is mechanical — re-anchor the inserted block above the now-shorter comment — but git will flag it. Upstream also adds `#include \"op/aggregate/group_key_labels.hpp\"` in the same include hunk where SOT adds `#include \"op/aggregate/aggregate_op_util.hpp\"`, and adds a `catch (const rmm::cuda_error&)` arm in the label-encode try block that SOT does not have; keep upstream's. The `use_canonical_sorted_groupby` gate on the STRING dict-encode (SOT `@@ -261,7 +290,10 @@`) and the presorted groupby ctor (`@@ -306,7 +338,16 @@`) sit below upstream's `@@ -215,9 +187,9 @@` and `@@ -227,15 +199,15 @@` hunks and should apply with only offset drift.\n\nUpstream leaves src/op/merge/gpu_merge_impl.cpp, src/op/aggregate/aggregate_op_util.{hpp,cpp}, src/op/sirius_physical_ungrouped_aggregate.cpp and test/cpp/operator/aggregate/test_gpu_merge_impl.cpp untouched, so the rest of L1 is conflict-free.\n\nCMakeLists.txt: upstream adds group_key_labels.cpp, dense_count_join_impl and two new test files (+14 lines). This slice adds NOTHING to CMakeLists (aggregate_op_util.cpp and test_gpu_merge_impl.cpp are already registered on dev; the five SOT additions all belong to the staging-arena and byte-range slices), so there is no conflict from this stack. DESCRIPTION OUTLINE:
MOTIVATION. Today a StarRocks-fronted Sirius cluster can only run TPC-H with `SET new_planner_agg_stage = 1`, which forces the FE to emit a single finalized aggregation instance. That was a deliberate stopgap from the single-node multi-fragment experiment, when the compute node had no real exchanges and ran fragments sequentially by feeding one's materialized result to the next. A two-phase aggregation is inherently an exchange construct — N partial states produced on N instances, gathered and merged — so the workaround is exactly what pins the whole query to one GPU. Removing it is the prerequisite for running TPC-H Q1 and Q6 across many GPUs with one compute node per GPU. It also supersedes the materialized-parquet exchange approach in #1242: rows never leave the device.\n\nWHAT CHANGED, layer by layer.\n(1) Engine aggregate determinism. Float SUMs are routed through a canonical (group keys, float values) row order and cuDF's atomics-free sort-based groupby, so a partial depends only on its batch's row multiset rather than on atomicAdd scheduling; the same canonicalization runs at merge, where the concatenation order is exchange arrival order. Separately, a new pre-check refuses any 64-bit integer SUM whose `valid_rows * max(|min|,|max|)` bound exceeds the accumulator — the loud guard on the HUGEINT->BIGINT downcast the plan generator performs because cuDF has no INT128.\n(2) Declared stream cardinality. An exchange input stream reached DuckDB's optimizer with no row count and was assumed to hold one row, so join order and build-side selection were chosen blind at every fragment boundary. `Fragment::declare_input_cardinality` carries the count the CN already knows (its senders are parked or staged) through the bind catalog to the stream-source TableFunction's cardinality callback. Purely additive: an undeclared stream keeps today's behavior.\n(3) EXCHANGE_NODE as a native stream read. The receiver's exchange boundary becomes a ReadRel over the engine's `sirius_stream_<id>` view; the sender's batches move across with `relay_from` as native GPU handles. The translator records each stream's schema so the caller can declare it (a stream has no file to infer one from), and resolves a hash-partitioned sink's keys to output column indices while the row layout is still in hand.\n(4) Two-phase aggregation. `agg_phase::classify` reads the phase from `need_finalize` x per-measure `is_merge_agg` and replaces both legacy guards in one commit — the node-level check alone does not stop a merge node, since new-optimizer plans always set the two tuple ids equal, so a partial replacement would silently double-aggregate. `partial_state::wire_columns` models what the engine BINDS for each state, because the FE's intermediate slot type lies: it says DECIMAL128 for a decimal sum the translator has already cast to FP64, and an opaque VARBINARY for avg. One pure function types both ends of the hop, so they agree by construction. Merge measures substitute functions (sum->sum, min->min, max->max, count->SUM) because the engine ignores Substrait's phase field and executes exactly what the function names say; the label is set afterwards, advisory only. avg is the one state that is not a single column — one FE slot, a Sirius sum plus a count — so it expands into two partial measures, two merged sums, and a finalizing division with SQL's empty-input NULL. Grouping keys and sort-tuple slots are emitted in the descriptor's materialized order rather than GROUP BY / ordering order, because that is the order every consumer above resolves the row through.\n(5) CN multi-fragment runtime. A sender fragment parks its output on the GPU once, with one output stream per destination; a receiver-first rendezvous matches the FE's dispatch order; a dedicated dispatch thread runs ready receivers off the RPC threads so a sender RPC returns immediately; and the result store grows per-query failure propagation so an intermediate fragment's failure reaches the instances the FE actually polls instead of wedging fetch_data until timeout.\n(6) Conformance gate. For every row of the wire-type model, translate the real partial and merge fragments, build each through the real engine, and assert the engine's own `output_types` against the receiver's declared schema — the same comparison a live hop makes.\n\nHOW VERIFIED. Catch2 `[deterministic_agg]` and `[streaming_fragment]` FRAG-10; `[stream_bind_catalog]` CAT-10; the translator crate's `cargo test --workspace --no-default-features` suite (phase classification, wire types end to end, avg expansion, materialization order, exchange stream reads); GPU-gated merge-shape oracles in the `sirius` crate that run partial -> hop -> merge and compare against the one-shot answer; CN service tests covering receiver-first dispatch, intermediate-failure propagation and cancel; and the engine-linked wire-type parity suite.\n\nINTENTIONALLY NOT HANDLED. Cross-node exchange (nixl transport, staging arena, `transmit_packed`) — a remote destination is a loud error naming how to enable it. 3/4-phase DISTINCT plans, `multi_distinct_*`, and opaque VARBINARY states (ndv, hll, max_by) are refused by name. Decimal-exact sums: two-phase inherits the existing FP64 lowering and does not change it. Bit-reproducibility across different row->batch decompositions (see notes/2026-08-20-rtx-sf500/PLAN-07): each stage is now bit-stable, but batching is not part of the guarantee, so TPC-H q15's exact float equality can still flake. Engine phase awareness, operator changes, and per-query lifecycle isolation (dispatch still blocks on a fragment) are out of scope.

OPEN QUESTIONS:
- Does L5 ship nixl-free or with the nixl_transport.rs shell? Nixl-free removes ~280 lines and 109 coupling points from compute_node_service.rs, but forces the nixl slice to re-touch execute_fragment_with_inputs, route_destination, local_exchange, fragment_executor and engine. My recommendation is nixl-free — but note that nixl is on the actual critical path for the stated goal (one CN per GPU means every peer is remote), so the two must be planned together even if reviewed apart.
- What is the measured cost of canonicalize_row_order on q01/q09 at SF100 on one GPU? Nothing in the tree measures it, and it fires on essentially every TPC-H aggregate because DECIMAL(p>18) lowers to FP64. If it is material, does it become a config flag (and what is the default)?
- Are the mbrobbel fixes #1233/#1235/#1236 going to land upstream on their own, or should this stack absorb #1236 (decimal->FP64) since neither Q1 nor Q6 translates without it? Absorbing it makes L4 bigger but makes the stack independently demonstrable.
- Should the aggregation-output/sort-tuple materialization-order fixes (grouping_materialization_order, sort_materialization_order, grouping_slot_id, sort_tuple_slot_id + ~450 lines of q03-shaped tests) be their own PR beneath L4? They are a self-contained 'wire order, not slot id' correctness fix with an independent motivation, and splitting them makes L4 meaningfully smaller.
- Is the descriptor_table slot_global_index slot-id fallback (stale tuple ids from a multi-stage aggregation, the q16 shape) a separate 60-line PR, or does it ride with L3? Its test currently sits in the exchange group in translate.rs.
- Do main.rs's --gpu-device/--gpu-memory-limit/--gpu-memory-fraction/--host-memory-limit + engine_settings.rs, and lib.rs's EngineReadiness + HTTP health listener, block the 8-GPU benchmark? Measured evidence in the delta says 2 of 4 CNs were auto-blacklisted ~2 s after every cluster start without the HTTP listener, and q14/SF100 ran 48.9/0/0/51.1 percent across four CNs instead of ~25 each. That makes them operationally required even though they are semantically orthogonal — should they be a sibling 'cn-cluster-bringup' slice sequenced alongside, rather than deferred?
- Should throw_if_int64_sum_could_overflow land at all in L1, or wait until it has a unit test and an opt-out? As written it is an untested new hard failure on a hot path.
- Which of the old draft PRs' descriptions should be harvested for which layer? #1644 'Stream fragment execution' (29 commits, origin/stream-fragment-execution) is the closest prior art for L5, #1598 for the ffi-fragment-rust dependency, and #1686 for the umbrella framing. All are to be closed; confirm the new PRs should cite them as superseded.
- L4's node_translator.rs and translate.rs hunks assume the common-slot carrying (#1233) landed FIRST, so TranslatedRel already carries `carried_slots`. If #1233 lands after, every TranslatedRel construction site in L3/L4 needs a mechanical follow-up. Which order?

######## SLICE cardinality — Item 6a — Exchange input cardinality declaration (commit 55e63371, 17 files, +471/-37)

SUMMARY: A `sirius_stream_source` relation binds with no rows behind it, so DuckDB's optimizer estimated cardinality 1 for every exchange input and picked hash-join build sides blind at every fragment boundary — on the 2-CN q07 it built on a multi-GB lineitem-derived stream while a 2-row nation stream probed (14.8s -> 164s at SF500). This slice plumbs a row count end to end: `stream_input_spec/stream_input_binding::estimated_rows`, a `stream_bind_catalog::estimated_rows(id)` reader, a `stream_source_cardinality` TableFunction callback that feeds `LogicalGet::EstimateCardinality`, `Fragment::declare_input_cardinality` and `Fragment::output_row_count` on the C++ FFI, an extra `rows` out-param on `export_packed`, the matching Rust bindings, and the CN wiring that sums local parked + remote staged counts before `build()`. Declaring is optional and additive: an undeclared stream returns `nullptr` from the callback and keeps today's exact behavior. The engine half is the load-bearing part for Q1/Q6 on many GPUs — with one CN per GPU every fragment boundary is an exchange input, so every receiver fragment is currently planned against a fictitious cardinality of 1. `create_streaming_source_plan` on dev already calls `op.EstimateCardinality(context)`, so the missing piece upstream is literally the callback plus the value that feeds it.

SIZE~500  PR TITLE: feat(exec): declare exchange input cardinality to DuckDB's optimizer

DEPENDS_ON: NONE for layer 1 (cardinality-engine): every symbol it needs is already on origin/dev — stream_bind_catalog + catalog_for (src/include/exec/stream_bind_catalog.hpp:85), stream_source_bind_data / register_stream_source_function (src/exec/stream_plan_bindings.cpp), sirius_physical_operator::estimated_cardinality (src/include/op/sirius_physical_operator.hpp:423), create_streaming_source_plan's op.EstimateCardinality(context) call (src/planner/sirius_plan_get.cpp:~224), the C++ ffi::Fragment class incl. output_repository/output_batch_count (merged as #1481), and the FRAG fixture helpers. It can be the bottom of the whole campaign stack.; exchange-staging-arena slice (layer 2 only — Fragment::export_packed and Context::staging_* do not exist on dev; src/exec/exchange_staging_arena.{hpp,cpp} are new, +338/+148, and the `rows` out-param is a signature change to a function that slice introduces); rust-fragment-bindings slice, ex-draft PR #1598 / origin/stream/16-rust-fragment (layer 2 only — origin/dev's rust/crates/sirius-sys/src/lib.rs is 61 lines and rust/crates/sirius/src/lib.rs is 257 lines, exposing only Context + execute_substrait; there is no Rust `Fragment`, no `PackedBatch`, nothing to hang declare_input_cardinality / output_row_count / PackedBatch.rows on); cn-exchange-runtime slice (layer 3 only — engine.rs run_fragment_inner/export_next, fragment_executor.rs StagedBatch, local_exchange.rs and compute_node_service.rs's packed-frame path are all new in this campaign); nixl-transport slice (layer 3 only — experimental/starrocks/patches/nixl-exchange-proto.patch and the PTransmitPackedParams message it adds, plus nixl_transport.rs mod agent_tier, must exist before `optional uint64 rows = 10` and `rows: batch.rows` can be applied to them)

LAYERS:
- cardinality-engine (stacked/exec-stream-cardinality) — C++ engine + FFI + tests + docs, lands FIRST and standalone on upstream dev: files=['src/include/exec/stream_bind_catalog.hpp', 'src/exec/stream_bind_catalog.cpp', 'src/exec/stream_plan_bindings.cpp', 'src/include/exec/streaming_fragment.hpp', 'src/exec/streaming_fragment.cpp', 'src/include/sirius_ffi.hpp', 'src/sirius_ffi.cpp', 'test/cpp/exec/test_stream_bind_catalog.cpp', 'test/cpp/exec/test_streaming_fragment.cpp', 'docs/super-sirius/streaming-fragments.md'] | ~340 changed lines, one sitting. This is the whole optimizer fix and it depends on NOTHING else in the campaign: stream_bind_catalog, catalog_for, stream_source_bind_data, the sirius_stream_source TableFunction, sirius_physical_operator::estimated_cardinality, the C++ ffi::Fragment (merged as #1481) and create_streaming_source_plan's existing op.EstimateCardinality(context) call are all on origin/dev. It ships the declaration (stream_input_spec/binding::estimated_rows), the reader (stream_bind_catalog::estimated_rows), the consumer (stream_source_cardinality -> stream_source.cardinality), the two new FFI entry points (declare_input_cardinality, output_row_count), and both tests, including FRAG-10's direct plan-shape proof that the build side flips. Drop the one `watchdog_guard watchdog("20");` line from FRAG-10 and omit the watchdog_guard struct. Deliberately NOT included: export_packed's rows out-param, because export_packed itself is not on dev.
- cardinality-ffi-rows (stacked/ffi-packed-batch-rows) — export_packed row count + Rust bindings: files=['src/include/sirius_ffi.hpp', 'src/sirius_ffi.cpp', 'rust/crates/sirius-sys/src/lib.rs', 'rust/crates/sirius/src/lib.rs'] | ~85 changed lines. Two independent reasons this cannot ride layer 1: export_packed does not exist on dev (arena slice), and there is no Rust Fragment on dev (rust-fragment-bindings slice, ex-#1598). Rebase this on top of BOTH. It carries the `std::uint64_t& rows` third out-param on Fragment::export_packed plus the two lines that fill it, the cxx declarations for declare_input_cardinality / output_row_count / export_packed's rows, the safe Rust wrappers, the `pub rows: u64` field on PackedBatch, and the two extended Rust round-trip tests. If the arena and rust-fragment slices are themselves stacked, the cheapest packaging is to fold this layer's ~10 C++ lines into the arena PR (so export_packed is born with `rows`) and this layer's ~70 Rust lines into the rust-fragment PR — see open_questions.
- cardinality-cn (stacked/cn-declare-input-cardinality) — StarRocks CN plumbing + wire field: files=['experimental/starrocks/patches/nixl-exchange-proto.patch', 'experimental/starrocks/src/fragment_executor.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/engine.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/compute_node_service.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/local_exchange.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/nixl_transport.rs'] | ~72 changed lines. Everything here is new-in-campaign code, so this must sit on top of the CN exchange runtime and nixl slices. It is what actually makes the fix fire in a multi-CN Q1/Q6 run: run_fragment_inner sums local parked rows (output_row_count) and remote staged rows (the wire's `rows` field) per stream and calls declare_input_cardinality before build(), degrading to a warn! when any contributor's count is unknown. Note the split in CI coverage: `mod engine` is #[cfg(feature = "sirius-engine")] so the declaration block is not built by the --no-default-features lane, but StagedBatch.rows, compute_node_service and nixl_transport are, and transmit_packed_frames_feed_a_dispatched_receiver exercises the wire round-trip there.

RISKS:
- stream_input_binding is a plain aggregate and `estimated_rows` is inserted BEFORE the `built` back-pointer, so every positional brace-init has to move in lockstep. The four in-tree sites are all updated here (src/exec/streaming_fragment.cpp:141, src/sirius_ffi.cpp:320, test/cpp/exec/test_stream_bind_catalog.cpp:40 and :199 on dev numbering), but any in-flight sibling branch in this campaign that constructs a stream_input_binding will break at compile time on rebase. Same for stream_input_spec, though there the field is appended last so 3-field inits still compile.
- Enabling TableFunction::cardinality on sirius_stream_source changes the plan surface for every existing consumer of the streaming path, not just CNs. Mitigated because stream_source_cardinality returns nullptr for an undeclared stream, which is bit-for-bit today's behavior, and FRAG-10's third SECTION pins exactly that — but it is the thing an upstream reviewer will push on, so the PR description must lead with it.
- Fragment::output_row_count throws sirius::invalid_input_exception on a spilled (non-GPU) parked batch and on an unknown output stream, while the adjacent Fragment::output_batch_count next to it never throws and returns 0 on a fragment with no streaming sink. A caller that treats the two symmetrically will fail a whole fragment where the intent was 'skip the declaration'. The Rust side gets this right (`.ok()` in engine.rs) but the C++ contract is asymmetric and only documented in a comment.
- FRAG-10 is tagged [integration]: it needs a GPU and test/cpp/integration/data/duckdb/integration.duckdb, so it does not run in a no-GPU lane. On such a lane the only automated signal for layer 1 is CAT-10, which exercises the catalog accessor but not the optimizer callback or the build-side flip.
- FRAG-10's 'no declared cardinality still plans and runs' SECTION asserts `live_catalog->get(0).built->estimated_cardinality <= 1`, which pins DuckDB's default estimate for a zero-stat LogicalGet. A DuckDB version bump that changes that default turns a documentation assertion into a red build.
- declare_input_cardinality takes std::uint64_t and stores it unvalidated; it is later narrowed to duckdb::idx_t in stream_source_cardinality and to std::size_t in sirius_physical_operator::estimated_cardinality. A caller passing a bogus u64 (e.g. a wrapped subtraction) gets silent truncation rather than an error.
- Dropping `watchdog_guard watchdog("20");` from FRAG-10 to make layer 1 standalone removes the hang backstop: if the two-stream join ever wedges under run(), the suite blocks instead of failing in 20s. Acceptable for this small local plan, but note it in the PR so it can be restored once the sirius_engine.cpp watchdog slice lands.
- engine.rs's local_rows sum silently yields None (and therefore skips the declaration for the whole stream) if `parked_slots.get(slot)` or `parked.get(&park_id)` misses — the same code path as a legitimately unknown count. A wiring bug there degrades to blind planning with only a warn!, which is exactly the pre-fix regression this slice exists to prevent, and is hard to notice in a benchmark run.
- The proto field `optional uint64 rows = 10` is added to a patch applied to the vendored StarRocks submodule. Field number 10 must not collide with anything upstream StarRocks adds to PTransmitPackedParams; since the message itself is Sirius-added by the same patch, the risk is only against a future revision of that patch.

UPSTREAM: Layer 1 has ZERO conflicts with upstream. `git diff origin/dev upstream-pr/dev --numstat` (12 commits, 84ea4ab5) touches none of the nine engine/test files layer 1 needs: src/include/exec/stream_bind_catalog.hpp, src/exec/stream_bind_catalog.cpp, src/exec/stream_plan_bindings.cpp, src/include/exec/streaming_fragment.hpp, src/exec/streaming_fragment.cpp, src/include/sirius_ffi.hpp, src/sirius_ffi.cpp, test/cpp/exec/test_stream_bind_catalog.cpp, test/cpp/exec/test_streaming_fragment.cpp. Three near-misses, all checked and clear: (1) upstream #1547 rewrites src/planner/sirius_plan_get.cpp (+9/-40 — it deletes the file-local `pinned_native_types_match_columns` and changes `find_pinned_entry_for_duckdb_table`'s signature) but only inside `create_plan(LogicalGet&)`; `create_streaming_source_plan`, the sole consumer of this slice's callback and the site of the existing `op.EstimateCardinality(context)` call, is untouched. (2) upstream changes src/include/op/sirius_physical_operator.hpp (+34/-10) but not the `estimated_cardinality` member (line 423) or the constructor that takes it (line 407). (3) upstream changes CMakeLists.txt (+13/-1), but this slice needs no CMakeLists change at all — all four of its source/test files are already registered on dev at lines 295, 296, 693, 695. Also note upstream added CONTRIBUTING.md (+122) and .github/pull_request_template.md changes plus a `.claude/skills/pr-digest` skill — the new reviewability rules apply to these PRs and should be read before writing the descriptions. Layers 2 and 3 have no upstream conflicts either (rust/crates/* and experimental/starrocks/src/* are untouched by upstream except experimental/starrocks/Cargo.lock +9/-9); their only conflicts are with sibling slices of this campaign, which is what the stack ordering handles. Finally: the cardinality code is verbatim-identical between 55e63371 and SOT HEAD efa312f4 — the later commits that touch these files (f5365d1e's pin_table FFI adds +29 to sirius_ffi.hpp and +58 to sirius_ffi.cpp; the scan-ranges work adds +211 to compute_node_service.rs) do not modify a single line of this slice, so cherry-picking 55e63371 is safe. No prior draft PR covered this ground: `git grep declare_input_cardinality` finds nothing on origin/stream-fragment-execution (#1644), origin/stream/16-rust-fragment (#1598) or origin/demo-multi-cn; only origin/demo-multi-cn-after-rebase (802106c0, a rebase snapshot of the same campaign) has it. This is new material, so its PR description has to be written from the commit message rather than lifted from an existing draft.

DESCRIPTION OUTLINE:
MOTIVATION. A `sirius_stream_source` relation has no rows behind it at bind time, so DuckDB's optimizer estimates cardinality 1 for every exchange input. On `origin/dev`, `sirius_physical_plan_generator::create_streaming_source_plan` already asks for `op.EstimateCardinality(context)` (src/planner/sirius_plan_get.cpp) — it just never gets a real answer, so the value that reaches `sirius_physical_streaming_source::estimated_cardinality` is always 1. Consequence: at every fragment boundary the join-order optimizer and the hash-join build/probe flip are decided blind. Concretely, on a 2-CN TPC-H q07 the receiver fragment built its hash table on a multi-GB lineitem-derived stream while a 2-row nation stream probed: 14.8s -> 164s at SF500. This scales with GPU count — with one StarRocks CN per GPU, *every* input of *every* non-leaf fragment is an exchange stream, so the blindness is total.

WHAT CHANGED. The row count becomes an optional, caller-supplied property of a declared stream, carried from the declaration site to the optimizer:
- `stream_input_spec::estimated_rows` and `stream_input_binding::estimated_rows` (`std::optional<std::uint64_t>`), forwarded by `streaming_fragment::build()` and by `Fragment::Impl::resolve_inputs`/`declare_bindings`.
- `stream_bind_catalog::estimated_rows(stream_id_t)` — a deliberately non-throwing reader (unlike `get()`), because DuckDB can ask for cardinality outside the window where a fragment's declarations are alive.
- `stream_source_cardinality()` in src/exec/stream_plan_bindings.cpp, wired as `stream_source.cardinality`. It returns `NodeStatistics(rows)` when a count is declared and `nullptr` in every other case (no bind data, wrong bind type, no registered state, no catalog on the connection, undeclared stream, declaration without a count). Same shape as the existing `sirius_read_parquet.cardinality = SiriusReadParquetCardinality` wiring in src/sirius_extension.cpp.
- FFI: `Fragment::declare_input_cardinality(stream_id, rows)` (summed over a stream's senders by the caller, last call wins, rejected after `build()`), and `Fragment::output_row_count(stream_id)` — rows parked on an output stream counted without draining it, which is what a local relay's receiver feeds into its own declaration.
- [layer 2] `Fragment::export_packed` gains a `rows` out-param so a transport can carry each batch's exact count to the receiver; the Rust bindings expose all three and `PackedBatch` gains `rows`.
- [layer 3] CN plumbing: `optional uint64 rows = 10` on `PTransmitPackedParams`, `StagedBatch::rows`, and a block in `run_fragment_inner` that sums local parked rows + remote staged rows per stream and declares before `build()`.

Declaring is optional and additive. An undeclared stream produces `nullptr` from the callback and takes exactly the pre-existing code path, so nothing that does not opt in changes behavior.

HOW VERIFIED. `stream_bind_catalog CAT-10` covers the accessor's contract: nullopt for an undeclared id (not a throw), nullopt for a declaration without a count, verbatim round-trip, replacement on redeclare, drop on erase. `FRAG-10` ([integration][streaming_fragment][stream_cardinality]) is the real proof: it builds a two-stream equi-join fragment and asserts on the built physical tree that the declared counts reached the lowered `sirius_physical_streaming_source` verbatim (2 and 100'000) and that the tiny stream ended up under `join->children[1]` — DuckDB's build side — in BOTH directions, so the assertion cannot pass on the optimizer's accidental default order. A third SECTION pins the pre-fix blindness as documentation (`estimated_cardinality <= 1` on both sources when nothing is declared) and proves the undeclared path still plans and runs. All three sections then push, close, `run()` and check the join's 5 rows. On the Rust side `packed_hop_matches_relay_hop` asserts the LOCAL source of truth (`output_row_count(0) == 3`) and the REMOTE one (per-batch `rows` summing to 3 across `export_packed` frames), and `zero_row_export_is_metadata_only_and_holds_no_lease` pins `rows == 0` on a metadata-only frame. On the CN, `transmit_packed_frames_feed_a_dispatched_receiver` shows the count riding the wire into the staged batch.

WHAT IS INTENTIONALLY NOT HANDLED. (1) Nobody in the engine declares a count on its own — the value always comes from a caller that already holds the stream's batches; this PR adds the mechanism, not a policy or an estimator. (2) `output_row_count` throws on a spilled (non-GPU) parked batch rather than counting it; spilled-batch row counting is out of scope and callers are expected to skip the declaration in that case. (3) Only a total row count is carried — no per-column NDV, no min/max, no `max_cardinality`, so the optimizer gets base-relation sizing but not selectivity. (4) The declaration is not re-derivable after `build()`; it is a plan-time input only. (5) `sirius_stream_source` still refuses projection pushdown, so the count is always for the full declared schema.

OPEN QUESTIONS:
- Layer 2 packaging: should the ~10 C++ lines (export_packed's `rows` out-param) be folded into the exchange-staging-arena PR so export_packed is born with the field, and the ~70 Rust lines folded into the rust-fragment-bindings PR (ex-#1598)? That removes a whole PR from the stack and removes a signature change to a function introduced two PRs earlier, at the cost of putting a little cardinality concern inside two otherwise-unrelated PRs. Recommendation: yes for the C++ half, undecided for the Rust half.
- Same question for the proto field: fold `optional uint64 rows = 10` into the nixl PR that introduces experimental/starrocks/patches/nixl-exchange-proto.patch, so layer 3 does not have to churn the patch's `index` blob hash and two @@ hunk headers just to add five lines?
- Should `Fragment::output_row_count` return `std::optional<std::uint64_t>` (or 0) instead of throwing on a spilled parked batch? Every caller today does `.ok()` / skip, and the sibling `output_batch_count` never throws — the throw is a contract wart that upstream review will likely flag.
- Keep or soften FRAG-10's `estimated_cardinality <= 1` assertion for the undeclared path? It documents the bug this PR fixes, but it pins a DuckDB internal default that a version bump could move.
- The commit message claims coverage by "stream_bind_catalog CAT-7/9/10"; CAT-9 is untouched and CAT-7 only gets a mechanical brace-init update. The PR description should say CAT-10 + FRAG-10. Confirm before reusing the commit text.
- Does upstream want `declare_input_cardinality` to be last-call-wins (as implemented — the caller sums over senders) or additive per call (accumulate, matching the per-sender shape of `declare_input_sender`)? The FFI doc says summed-by-caller; a reviewer may reasonably expect the API to do the summing.
- Doc placement: add the cardinality paragraph to docs/super-sirius/streaming-fragments.md (the `stream_bind_catalog` snippet + Contracts list + the `fragment_spec`/`stream_input_spec` snippet all live there) or to docs/super-sirius/optimizations.md, which is where plan-quality mechanisms are catalogued? Nothing on SOT documents this at all, so it is a free choice.
- Should FRAG-10 keep `watchdog_guard` (which would drag in the src/sirius_engine.cpp `wait_for_query_future` / task_scheduler `fail_stalled_query` slice as a hard dependency, +65 lines), or drop it now and restore it in the watchdog PR? Dropping it is what makes layer 1 land first, ahead of everything else in the campaign.


######## SLICE pin-table — Item 6b — pin_table series (FFI + ADMIN EXECUTE, pinned file-subset scans, FE whole-file ranges)

SUMMARY: The DEV..SOT pin-table delta is three genuinely separable changes plus docs/bench. (1) `4a7ea75d` teaches the scan manager to serve a pinned parquet table to a scan over a strict SUBSET of the pinned file set, by recording per-chunk source-file provenance at pin time (coalescer file-boundary mode) and selecting covered chunks at serve time — this is the piece that makes one-CN-per-GPU pinning actually pay off, since StarRocks hands each CN a per-query file subset, and it is 100% engine-side C++ that depends on nothing else in the campaign. (2) `f5365d1e` splits `RegisterPinTableFunctions` out of `RegisterGPUFunctions` and adds `ffi::Context::pin_table/unpin_table` + the sirius-sys/sirius Rust bridge — also standalone against DEV. (3) the CN half of `f5365d1e` (`admin_command.rs`, `execute_command` RPC, `EngineRequest::PinTable`) plus `a4608cf0`'s FE patch is the only part that hard-depends on the multi-CN slice. Verified answers to the two open questions: `4a7ea75d` does NOT depend on the byte-range slice (`has_byte_ranges` / `resolved_file_ranges` come from `0bc03cca`, and only one context line touches it), and against upstream `origin/dev..upstream-pr/dev` a real `git merge-tree` produces exactly two conflicts in this slice's files, both trivial. No CMakeLists change is needed anywhere in this slice.

SIZE~1300  PR TITLE: feat(scan): serve pinned parquet tables to file-subset scans

DEPENDS_ON: multi-CN / StarRocks-CN multi-fragment slice (0bc03cca) — REQUIRED BY LAYER 3 ONLY. DEV's experimental/starrocks has none of: FragmentRun/SenderSlot/StagedBatch, EngineRequest/engine_thread/engine_call, ServiceCore, SiriusComputeNodeService::with_executor(executor, identity), prpc::Reply, scripts/apply-starrocks-patches.sh. DEV's FragmentExecutor trait is a single `execute(&self, &TranslatedPlan)`. Layers 1 and 2 do NOT depend on it.; exchange-staging-arena / streaming slice — SOFT, layer 2 only. f5365d1e's rust/crates/sirius/src/lib.rs hunk assumes `SiriusContext::inner` is a `RefCell<UniquePtr<Context>>` (that change is in 0bc03cca). On DEV it is a plain `UniquePtr`, so the safe wrapper must be written with `&mut self` (one-line adaptation) or bring the RefCell change along.; byte-range slice — NOT A DEPENDENCY, only a merge-order note. `has_byte_ranges` (cache_entry_info field + parquet_ingestible_table_info::has_byte_ranges()) and `resolved_file_ranges` come from 0bc03cca, never from 4a7ea75d. The single point of contact is one line of context inside `can_serve_with_columns`: if the byte-range slice lands first, the pin-subset PR keeps the `if (has_byte_ranges || p->has_byte_ranges()) return {};` guard above the file-set check unchanged; if the pin-subset PR lands first, the byte-range PR inserts that guard above the `matches_parquet_file_set(...) == miss` check instead of above `!matches_parquet_files(...)`. Either order is a one-line rebase.; LAYER 2 depends on LAYER 1 only for stack ordering of src/sirius_extension.cpp (disjoint hunks — 995/1511/1537/1560 vs 1893/1954 — so either order compiles; stacking just avoids a rebase).; LAYER 3 depends on LAYER 2 (needs sirius::PinTableSpec and ffi Context::pin_table) and on the multi-CN slice.; LAYER 4 (bench kit) depends on LAYER 3 and on the bench slice (bench/bench.sh, bench/rtxpro6000-2gpu/BUILD-SIRIUS-STARROCKS.md, the cluster launch scripts).

Ships real user-visible value on the plain DuckDB path too — a glob pin now serves a query over any subset of the pinned files instead of falling through to disk — and is the single change that makes one-CN-per-GPU pinning pay off for Q1/Q6. Pure refactor + new API: pin_table/unpin_table registration is lifted into RegisterPinTableFunctions (no behavior change on the extension path — same TableFunctionSet, same named parameters) and reused on the FFI's embedded DuckDB, so ffi::Context::pin_table runs the identical table function through the Relation API. Independent of L1; stacked only to serialize the two disjoint sirius_extension.cpp regions. Note the &mut self vs RefCell adaptation in the safe crate.
- L3 — feat(cn): pin tables on a StarRocks compute node through ADMIN EXECUTE: files=['experimental/starrocks/src/admin_command.rs', 'experimental/starrocks/src/compute_node_service.rs (partial)', 'experimental/starrocks/src/engine.rs (partial)', 'experimental/starrocks/src/fragment_executor.rs (partial)', 'experimental/starrocks/src/lib.rs (partial)', 'experimental/starrocks/patches/files-query-whole-file-ranges.patch', 'docs/pinned-tables-repro.md (CN section + 600 s ceiling)'] | ~580/2. Ten Rust tests, all in the --no-default-features CI build. Includes the documented hard 600 s ADMIN EXECUTE ceiling and the do-not-retry guidance.
