RISKS:
- Untested provenance path. No test in SOT references `chunk_file_paths`. The pin-time recorder in src/pin_table.cpp, the three `insert_pinned_entry*` arity validations, the merge adopt-or-throw branch, and the whole file-subset branch of `try_match_cached_entry` ship with zero coverage; only `matches_parquet_file_set` and `build_cached_scan_plan(allowed_chunks)` are tested. Upstream's PR-reviewability bar will likely flag this — add the three gap tests before opening L1.
- Silent correctness hazard if provenance and chunk storage ever drift. `pinned_entry::chunk_file_paths` is parallel to whichever of device_chunks / host_chunks / data_tables backs the entry; the insert paths validate arity but the serve path indexes `entry.chunk_file_paths[c]` against chunk indices produced by `build_cached_scan_plan`. A future path that appends chunks without appending provenance would serve another file's rows. The commit already mitigates by making the sinks push provenance UNCONDITIONALLY (unlike chunk_stats) — call this invariant out in the PR body.
- Behavior change on the existing exact-match path is narrow but real: `matches_parquet_files` is now `matches_parquet_file_set(...) == exact`, and the new function early-returns miss when `files.size() > resolved_file_paths.size()`. Duplicate handling is deliberately asymmetric (exact stays duplicate-symmetric, subset requires duplicate-free on both sides). Reviewers will ask about this; the reasoning is in the doc comment and covered by the new test.
- L1 changes pin-time chunking for every parquet pin (`batch_within_file_boundaries = true` in build_parquet_pin_info), so a many-small-files pin now produces at least one chunk per file. On a dataset with thousands of tiny files this raises chunk count and per-chunk overhead versus today's bundling. Worth a sentence in the PR body and, ideally, a measured note.
- L2 moves the pin_table/unpin_table registration. If anything outside RegisterGPUFunctions relied on the exact ordering of catalog registrations, the split would surface there; nothing found, but the PR body should assert the equivalence explicitly (same TableFunctionSet, both arities, same five named parameters).
- L2's ffi Context::pin_table returns a fabricated summary string ("pinned '<name>' tier=<tier>") rather than the table function's own output rows, which it drains and discards. Fine for the CN's one-line ADMIN EXECUTE reply, but a reviewer may ask why the real result is thrown away.
- L3's grammar deliberately has no quoting layer, so paths/names/columns containing spaces or '=' are unsupported. That is a documented design choice; state it in the PR body so it is not read as a bug.
- L3 repurposes StarRocks' execute_script RPC. Upstream BEs treat that payload as an arbitrary C++ debug script; this CN accepts only two verbs and rejects everything else by name. The PR body must lead with that, since 'we implemented ADMIN EXECUTE' reads as a remote-code-execution surface until the reader sees the closed grammar.
- L3's FE patch (files_query_whole_file_ranges) trades load balance for cacheability: an instance can overshoot by up to one file. Defaults to false, so it is opt-in, but the layout guidance (many files, each well under totalBytes/instances) belongs in the PR body, not only in the bench README.
- The 600 s ADMIN EXECUTE ceiling is an unfixable-from-here operational hazard: on timeout the FE reports failure while the CN keeps materializing, with no cancel, and a retry queues a second full materialization. Documented in L3, but it is the kind of thing a reviewer will want surfaced in the log line and not just the docs.

DESCRIPTION OUTLINE:
MOTIVATION (L1). A pinned parquet table only served a scan whose resolved file set equalled the pinned set exactly. StarRocks distributes a FILES() scan's files across compute nodes per query, so a whole-glob pin on each CN never matched its per-query assigned subset: the pin consumed GPU/host memory and served nothing, and every query still paid file I/O plus decode. The same gap exists on the plain DuckDB path — pin a glob, query two of its three files, fall through to disk.\n\nWHAT CHANGED (L1). Pin time: parquet_batch_coalescer gains a file-boundary mode (build_parquet_pin_info sets parquet_ingestible_table_info::batch_within_file_boundaries; the query read path keeps fused batches) so no chunk ever bundles two files, and materialize_pin_batches records each chunk's canonicalized, sorted, deduplicated source files. All three insert paths (GPU, host, compressed-device) store that provenance on pinned_entry::chunk_file_paths, validated parallel to the chunk vector; the GPU merge path adopts it when the existing entry has none and throws on a disagreement. Serve time: cache_entry_info::matches_parquet_file_set classifies a scan as exact / strict subset / miss, with subset requiring duplicate-free canonical sets on both sides (a duplicated pinned path means that file's chunks were materialized twice, and serving them to a scan that names the file once would double its rows; exact matching stays duplicate-symmetric, preserving today's semantics). try_match_cached_entry selects exactly the chunks whose provenance the scan covers and passes them to build_cached_scan_plan as allowed_chunks, applied BEFORE zone-map pruning so the all-pruned sentinel can only pick an allowed chunk. Entries with no provenance (pre-existing pins, duckdb-native pins) keep exact-only semantics, as do byte-range scans. find_pinned_entry_for_parquet_files — the plan-time compressed-materialization residency gate — accepts a subset under the same provenance condition, keeping the one-matcher invariant with the serve path.\n\nHOW VERIFIED. New Catch2 case in test_can_serve_with_columns.cpp covering exact / subset / miss / superset / empty / duplicate poisoning / duckdb-entry; new case in test_cached_serving_hardening.cpp covering the allowed-chunks restriction (identity plan over the allowed set, out-of-range indices dropped, empty set yields an empty plan the caller must treat as a miss). Recommended additions before merge: an insert_pinned_entry arity/adopt/mismatch trio, a coalescer file-boundary case in test_parquet_scan_sizing.cpp, and an integration case in the existing test_pin_table_* family. End to end on 2x RTX PRO 6000 (SF100 lineitem, 2 CNs, Q6 shape): 0.63 s unpinned -> 0.09 s pinned, values byte-identical to the DuckDB oracle; log line 'pinned entry ... serves operator ... as a file subset: N/M files, K/L chunks'.\n\nINTENTIONALLY NOT HANDLED (L1). Byte-range split scans still never serve from, or produce, a pin (a ranged scan holds a fraction of each file's rows). Duckdb-native pins get no provenance. A superset scan (naming a file the pin lacks) is a miss, not a partial serve. The FE-side lever, the FFI surface, and the CN admin channel are the follow-up PRs in this stack.\n\nSTACK (each PR self-sufficient, merged bottom-up). L2 feat(ffi): split RegisterPinTableFunctions out of RegisterGPUFunctions and register it on the FFI's embedded DuckDB, add Context::pin_table/unpin_table running the same table function through the Relation API (so bind validation, transactions, and the pin execution window match CALL pin_table exactly), and bridge both through sirius-sys and the safe sirius crate (PinTableSpec). Compression still engages purely from sirius.yaml. L3 feat(cn): implement the previously-unimplemented execute_command RPC — the receiving end of ADMIN EXECUTE ON <node_id> '<script>' — with a closed, line-oriented, non-executable grammar (pin_table key=value..., unpin_table <name>), dispatched through the engine thread so pins serialize with fragment runs; plus the FE patch files_query_whole_file_ranges, without which StarRocks cuts byte ranges for essentially every distributed FILES() query and no pin ever serves. Documents ADMIN EXECUTE's hard 600 s FE ceiling and the do-not-retry guidance. L4 (optional / bench slice): the reproducible pinned-vs-unpinned TPC-H kit.\n\nUPSTREAM MERGE NOTES. Rebasing onto upstream-pr/dev produces exactly two conflicts across this whole slice, both mechanical — see the upstream_conflict_notes field.

OPEN QUESTIONS:
- Does the byte-range slice or this one land first upstream? Either works (one-line adjustment to where the has_byte_ranges guard sits inside can_serve_with_columns), but the answer decides which PR carries that line.
- Is the multi-CN slice going upstream at all, and when? Layer 3 is unreviewable without it (DEV's FragmentExecutor trait is a single execute() method). If the multi-CN slice slips, layers 1 and 2 should still go now — layer 1 alone is the Q1/Q6 win once the FE lever exists.
- For layer 2's safe Rust wrapper: adapt pin_table to `&mut self` on DEV's `UniquePtr` inner, or bring the `RefCell<UniquePtr<Context>>` interior-mutability change (from 0bc03cca) into this PR with its own justification? The RefCell change is a public-API-shape decision that deserves its own review.
- Where does the FE patch belong? patches/files-query-whole-file-ranges.patch is only consumable via scripts/apply-starrocks-patches.sh, which does not exist in DEV. Options: ship it in the multi-CN slice's patches directory, or carry a minimal apply script in layer 3.
- Should ffi::Context::pin_table return the table function's actual result rows instead of the synthesized "pinned '<name>' tier=<tier>" string? The CN only needs one line, but discarding the real output is a reviewable choice.
- Is a two-line log/summary enough mitigation for the 600 s ADMIN EXECUTE ceiling, or should the CN reject a pin it can predict will exceed it (e.g. by estimated bytes) rather than let the FE time out with no cancel?
- test/tpch_performance/tpch_pin_columns.py's docstring cites `sirius_scan_manager.cpp:138-178` by line number — rewrite to name the function before shipping, or drop the file from this slice entirely?
- Should benchmarks/pinned/generated/cn*.yaml be gitignored upstream rather than simply omitted from the PR?

######## SLICE docs-bench-notes

SUMMARY: The docs/bench/notes surface of DEV..SOT is 268 files and ~51.5k added lines, but only ~63 files / ~5.8k lines are portable, box-agnostic artifacts worth upstreaming; the rest is per-box campaign state (bench/a100x8, bench/rtxpro6000-2gpu/results, bench/gb200-*/sf*/TUNING-DISCOVERY.md, experimental/starrocks/configs/gb200-*, 28 EXPLAIN dumps under benchmarks/tpch/plans/), the .claude/skills (which hardcode /home/prestouser/aocsa/sirius and presto-gb200-gcn-* box facts), and notes/ (52 files, ~24k lines of dated design diaries). The load-bearing pieces for "Q1/Q6 across many GPUs, one CN per GPU" are experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/cluster8.sh (one CN per GPU, fully parameterized by NUM_CNS/PORT_BASE, no hardcoded paths), the tpch/ harness (bench.sh + analyze.py + 22 FILES() queries + README documenting the harness contract and its traps), a real DuckDB correctness oracle (bench/rtxpro6000-2gpu/tools/{oracle.py,compare.py}) that the harness otherwise lacks, and the pinned-vs-unpinned kit (experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/pinned/, 6 files) that is the direct reproduction of the pinned Q1/Q6 speedups. bench/rtxpro6000-2gpu/BUILD-SIRIUS-STARROCKS.md (549 lines) has zero hardcoded host paths despite its directory name and is the source-of-truth build doc every other artifact points at. Split into 4 PRs bottom-up: docs-only (zero code dependency, lands immediately), the multi-CN bench harness + build doc (after the CN multi-fragment slice), the pinned kit + CN pinning guide (after the pin_table FFI/ADMIN EXECUTE, file-subset scan, and FE whole-file-ranges slices), and the 2-host/8-GPU layer last. Drop notes/ and .claude/skills entirely; ship the .claude/settings.json hook fix standalone since it is a real bug fix independent of everything else.

- experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/cn-2host.sh [whole-file] 194 lines (from 0bc03cca, +36 in 802106c0, +36 in efa312f4). Two-physical-host CN bring-up. Layer 4. Depends on the fabric staging arena (SIRIUS_EXCHANGE_STAGING_ARENA=fabric) from the exchange-arena slice — cudaMalloc's IPC handle is node-local, so without fabric UCX falls back to a ~0.4 GB/s host bounce, below the 2.0 GB/s canary floor, and the peer is refused.
- experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/stop-cn-2host.sh [whole-file] 80 lines, new in 802106c0. Companion teardown. Layer 4.
- experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/2NODE-REPLICATE.md [whole-file] 431 lines, heavily rewritten in 802106c0 (+288/-104). The working two-host procedure (supersedes notes/2026-08-11-2node-gb200/, which is explicitly historical). Measured 2026-08-11/12 on presto-gb200-gcn-17 + gcn-18: 1 CN per host, 98 GB/s canary. Carries two load-bearing traps: do NOT put rc_mlx5 in UCX_TLS (CN fails to start; nvidia_peermem is not loaded), and engine A is run_mode=shared_data while engine B must stay shared_nothing (FILES() FileScanNode looks up BEs, not CNs). Layer 4; de-hostname before landing.
- bench/gb200-8gpu/sweep.sh [whole-file] 52 lines. The 8-CN / 2-host sweep wrapper; selects bench/gb200-8gpu/sf<N>/env.sh, sets pipeline_dop and FE query_timeout, writes a MANIFEST.txt, and sed-patches q11's SF1 fraction (restoring via a trap). Layer 4, and only after two fixes: it hardcodes `source /scratch/prestouser/aocsa/env.sh` and defaults OUT to /scratch/prestouser/aocsa/bench-results/. The `sed -i` + `git checkout HEAD --` on a tracked query file is also hostile in a shared worktree — generate q11 into $OUT instead.

TESTS:
- No automated tests. This slice is docs + shell/python operator tooling; nothing here is registered in the top-level CMakeLists.txt and nothing adds a Catch2 case or a Rust test fn.
- Verification is manual and must be stated in each PR description. Layer 1: `grep -o '](\\./[^)]*' docs/*.md docs/super-sirius/README.md` resolves (the docs/README.md -> pinned-tables-repro.md link and the super-sirius README row), plus one end-to-end run of docs/pinned-tables-repro.md sections 0-4 against ./build/release/duckdb at a small SF confirming byte-identical results across unpinned / gpu / host / compressed.
- Layer 2: `bash -n` and `shellcheck` on cluster8.sh, cluster8-cfg.sh, cluster8-numa.sh, collect-host-facts.sh, bench.sh, run-comparison.sh, setup-engine-b.sh, gen-tpch.sh, clean-telemetry.sh; `python3 -m py_compile` on analyze.py, oracle.py, compare.py, drift.py, regress.py, cn-distribution.py; then one NUM_CNS=2 cluster8.sh bring-up + `bench.sh out.csv 2 q01 q06` + oracle.py/compare.py showing 0 mismatches. The oracle run IS the test — it is the only correctness gate in the harness.
- Layer 3: the 4-point checklist already written into experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/pinned/README.md '## Verify before quoting anything' — (1) `grep 'PIN CN'` in the sweep log shows no 0s/1s pin after a restart, (2) each CN logs `pinned entry 'lineitem' serves operator '0' as a file subset: N/M files` and the per-CN N sums to the full file count, (3) `compressing with plan for N column(s)` appears per pin (a `pinning uncompressed` warning means the plan dir or table name did not match), (4) DuckDB-oracle diff of arm A vs arm B row output on the pin-served queries.
- Layer 4: the 2NODE-REPLICATE canary check — expect ~98 GB/s in the cluster log, not 0.4, and `nvidia-smi nvlink -gt d` deltas must move.

EXCLUDE:
- notes/ in its entirety — 52 files, ~24k lines of dated design diaries (2026-08-11-2node-gb200/2NODE-BRINGUP-PLAN.md 1280, 2026-08-20-rtx-sf500/PLAN-01..09 ~8.8k combined, 2026-07-26-streaming/{old,new}-exchange-design.md 1334). notes/README.md itself says 'Not the source of truth for building, bringing up a cluster, or running a benchmark' and warns readers not to quote its timings. notes/OPEN.md is a private work queue keyed to one campaign. The only content worth rescuing is the trap list, which should be inlined into the scripts and READMEs that carry it (see bench.sh and cluster8.sh notes above).
- The DECODE_PUSHDOWN_PLAN.md -> notes/2026-08-decode-pushdown/ rename (0/0, pure R100). It only makes sense if notes/ ships. Leave the file at the repo root upstream; propose deleting or relocating it in a separate cleanup PR if it bothers anyone.
- .claude/skills/{tpch-bench,cn-tuning,tpch-cn-sweep,build-sirius-starrocks,tpch-2host}/SKILL.md — 5 new skills, 423 lines. tpch-bench (250) is a box-specific ops manual: it hardcodes /home/prestouser/aocsa/sirius, /home/prestouser/aocsa/tpch_parquet_sf100, /home/prestouser/aocsa/tools, `lineitem count(*) = 600,037,902`, the JDK path, and 'no docker'. All five are mostly ../../../ links into bench/ and notes/ files this slice does not land. Their real value is the trap lists, which belong in the scripts. Recommend NOT upstreaming; if wanted later, do it as a separate PR after the runbooks land and after the box facts are stripped.
- experimental/starrocks/configs/gb200-4gpu/ and configs/gb200-8gpu/ — 16 files, ~2.5k lines. relaunch.sh hardcodes REMOTE_HOST=presto-gb200-gcn-09, FE_HOST=10.87.140.53, CN09_HOST=10.87.140.44; engine-a.env (457) is one box's tuned values; engine-b/{be1,be2}.conf and sensitivity-4be/be{1..4}.conf are stock-StarRocks configs for a specific 4-BE experiment.
- experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/plans/ — 28 EXPLAIN/verbose/costs/analyze/logical dumps, ~4.5k lines (q02.verbose 402, q08.verbose 351, q05.verbose 279...). Point-in-time FE output; stale the moment the optimizer changes. Pure noise.
- experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/results/{sf1-2026-08-07-A.csv, sf1-2026-08-07-B.csv, sf1-2026-08-07.png} and experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/pinned/generated/cn{0,1,2,3}.yaml — committed run output and generated config.
- bench/a100x8/ (10 files, ~1.4k lines: TUNING.md 239, plot_study1.py 233, SCALE-OUT-SUMMARY.md 233, HARDWARE.md 204, CONFIGURATIONS.md 197, engine-a-sirius.yaml 144, results/*.log + *.csv, study1-scaleout.png binary) — one campaign on one 8x A100 box.
- bench/rtxpro6000-2gpu/results/ (16 CSVs, ~800 lines) and bench/rtxpro6000-2gpu/{STATUS.md 87, TPCH-STATUS.md 131, SF500-CONFIG-AND-ARCHITECTURE.md 200, README.md 61, SIRIUS-TUNING-RUNBOOK.md 348} — per-box status and results. SIRIUS-TUNING-RUNBOOK.md is the only borderline case: cn-tuning/SKILL.md calls it 'how the knobs work' (i.e. method, not values). If it survives a hard pass for box-specific numbers it could join layer 2 as experimental/starrocks/docs/TUNING.md; otherwise drop.
- bench/gb200-4gpu/ (HARDWARE.md 171, SIRIUS-TUNING-RUNBOOK.md 341, BUILD-AND-SMOKE.md 251) and bench/sf500-gb200/ (sirius-sf500.yaml 116, README.md 5) — box facts and per-box presets. BUILD-AND-SMOKE.md duplicates BUILD-SIRIUS-STARROCKS.md for one box.
- bench/gb200-8gpu/sf{1000,3000,10000}/{README.md,TUNING-DISCOVERY.md,queries-common.txt,queries-heavy.txt} and bench/gb200-8gpu/{README.md 21, SIRIUS-TUNING-RUNBOOK.md 177} — fleet-specific ('This is the whole fleet', 'Do not start SF10000', gcn-09 + gcn-18, /scratch/sirius/datasets/tpch_sf3000). The sf*/env.sh files are the only reusable shape (a knob-set-per-scale-factor pattern) and only if landed as one generic example.
- experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/{2NODE-ENGINE-B-TUTORIAL.md 921, 2NODE-ENGINE-B-RESULTS.md 155, 8GPU-NVLINK-RUNBOOK.md 549, host-facts-presto-gb200-gcn-18.txt 156} and benchmarks/nixl-nvlink/ (smoke-test.sh 226, arena-sweep.sh 176, study3-cost.sh 130, script-box.sh 130, study1-run.sh 106) — two-host and NVLink study scripts keyed to named hosts. 8GPU-NVLINK-RUNBOOK.md §5/§8 is referenced by tpch-bench/SKILL.md; if the skills are dropped it has no remaining consumer.

- .pixi (a 1-line gitlink to a local path) and experimental/starrocks/rmm_log.txt (2 lines of RMM output) — accidental, exclude from every PR in the stack (already flagged as a global decision).
- src/exec/stream_lifecycle.{hpp,cpp} and test/cpp/exec/test_stream_lifecycle.cpp — dead, global exclusion, not in this slice but restated so no doc in this slice references stream_lifecycle.
- bench/rtxpro6000-2gpu/tools/.gitignore — 2 lines, both the identical pattern `sirius-cn-*/`. Drop, or fold a single correct entry into the relocated tools directory.
- Any C++/Rust hunk. This slice touches no file under src/, rust/, or test/cpp, and adds nothing to the top-level CMakeLists.txt. The one adjacent code hunk is bc2e90d4's 7-line comment in experimental/starrocks/src/compute_node_service.rs documenting the 600 s ADMIN EXECUTE ceiling — it belongs with the CN admin-channel slice (f5365d1e), not here, even though its doc half ships in layer 3.

LAYERS:
- docs: pinned-table reproduction guide + super-sirius index fix (+ pre-commit hook scoping): files=["docs/pinned-tables-repro.md (sections 0-4, Measured reference, Notes and caveats — ~172 of 217 lines; the 'Pinning from StarRocks' section is held for layer 3)", 'docs/README.md', 'docs/super-sirius/README.md', '.claude/settings.json'] | ~190 lines, zero dependency on any other slice — every SQL statement it documents (pin_table, unpin_table, pin_table_compression, pin_table_input_compression_plan_dir and the two tuning knobs) already exists on origin/dev, and docs/super-sirius/streaming-sessions.md already carries the '### Partition fan-out' section the index row now advertises. Can be opened against upstream dev today, independent of the whole stack, which makes it the natural bottom of the stack and a cheap first merge. The .claude/settings.json hunk is unrelated in subject but is a one-line, obviously-correct bug fix (the pre-commit gate currently fires an agent on every Bash call, not just git commit); split it out if a reviewer objects to mixing it in.
- bench: multi-CN TPC-H harness, build doc, and the DuckDB correctness oracle: files=['experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/cluster8.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/cluster8-cfg.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/cluster8-numa.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/collect-host-facts.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/README.md', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/bench.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/analyze.py', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/run-comparison.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/setup-engine-b.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/QUERY-DEVIATIONS.md', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/queries/q01.sql .. q22.sql (22 files)', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/tools/oracle.py (relocated from bench/rtxpro6000-2gpu/tools/)', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/tools/compare.py (relocated)', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/tools/drift.py (relocated, optional)', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/tools/regress.py (relocated, optional)', 'experimental/starrocks/docs/BUILDING.md (relocated from bench/rtxpro6000-2gpu/BUILD-SIRIUS-STARROCKS.md)', 'experimental/starrocks/docs/TUNABLES.md', 'experimental/starrocks/DEMO.md', 'experimental/starrocks/scripts/clean-telemetry.sh', 'bench/common/gen-tpch.sh', 'bench/common/RETARGETING.md'] | ~3.2k lines and the direct enabler of the Q1/Q6-many-GPU priority: cluster8.sh is 'one CN per GPU, N of them' and is already fully parameterized with no hardcoded clone path. Must land after the CN multi-fragment + nixl + staging-arena slices, since DEMO.md describes deleted-ExchangeFile behavior and TUNABLES.md documents src/tunable.rs. Land bench.sh WITH the Alive-column fix (awk -F'\t' '$9=="true"' instead of grep -c true) and the oracle wired in, so the harness ships with a correctness gate rather than acquiring one later. If a reviewer calls this too large for one sitting, the clean cut is: PR 2a = cluster8*.sh + BUILDING.md + TUNABLES.md + DEMO.md (bring-up), PR 2b = tpch/ harness + oracle + RETARGETING.md (measurement).
- bench: pinned-vs-unpinned TPC-H kit and the StarRocks CN pinning guide: files=['experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/pinned/README.md', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/pinned/gen-config.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/pinned/up.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/pinned/restart.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/pinned/pin-all.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/pinned/compare.py', "docs/pinned-tables-repro.md ('Pinning from StarRocks' section + the ADMIN EXECUTE 600 s ceiling paragraph — the ~45 lines held back from layer 1)", 'test/tpch_performance/starrocks_performance_test.py', 'test/tpch_performance/tpch_pin_columns.py'] | ~1.4k lines. This is the reproduction of the headline result (2.07x on pin-served queries, 5.85x on q06 host-tier / 12.6x GPU-tier) and therefore the payoff PR of the whole campaign, but it is hard-blocked on three separate code slices: pin_table over the FFI and ADMIN EXECUTE (f5365d1e), file-subset pin serving in the scan manager (4a7ea75d), and the FE files_query_whole_file_ranges lever (a4608cf0). Land it last among the useful layers so its instructions are executable the day they merge. Resolve the starrocks_performance_test.py vs benchmarks/pinned/ overlap before opening it.
- bench: two-host and 8-GPU cross-node bring-up (optional / defer): files=['experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/cn-2host.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/stop-cn-2host.sh', 'experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/2NODE-REPLICATE.md', 'bench/gb200-8gpu/sweep.sh', 'bench/gb200-8gpu/sf1000/env.sh (as the single generic per-scale example)'] | ~850 lines. Genuinely useful (98 GB/s cross-host NVLink, the fabric-arena requirement, the rc_mlx5 and shared_data/shared_nothing traps) but the most host-entangled of the surviving set: 2NODE-REPLICATE.md is written around presto-gb200-gcn-17/18 and sweep.sh sources /scratch/prestouser/aocsa/env.sh. Defer until layers 1-3 are merged, then land only if the de-hostnaming is done properly; otherwise drop and keep it out-of-tree.

RISKS:
- Landing benchmarks/tpch/bench.sh unchanged ships a harness with no correctness gate: bench.sh:73-75 scores `pass` on exit code + non-empty file + no ERROR on line 1, and analyze.py reads only status and ms. A query returning 1 row instead of 100000 is recorded as a fast WIN. Upstream users will quote numbers from it. Mitigate by landing oracle.py/compare.py in the same PR and by fixing the Alive miscount (bench.sh:46-47 and run-comparison.sh:24 both use `grep -c true`, which matches SystemDecommissioned / ClusterDecommissioned / HasStoragePath, so a booting node with Alive=false counts).
- cluster8.sh does not clear CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES. engine.rs:207-216 lets an already-exported value win over --gpu-device with only a warn!, and the CN's ensure_gpu_unclaimed preflight (main.rs:265-270) short-circuits whenever --gpu-memory-limit is set — which cluster8.sh always does. The result is all N CNs on one GPU, a cluster that still answers queries and still records numbers. This is exactly the failure mode that would discredit a multi-GPU scaling claim. Fix in the PR.
- bench/common/RETARGETING.md ships six dead relative links (HARDWARE.md, engine-a-sirius.yaml, engine-a-sirius.env, engine-b-starrocks.conf, engine-c-cudf-polars.env, PLAN.md) because bench/common/ contains only RETARGETING.md and gen-tpch.sh. A docs PR with broken links is an easy reviewer rejection.
- Several surviving docs cross-reference files this slice deliberately drops: benchmarks/tpch/README.md links notes/OPEN.md M1.4; benchmarks/pinned/README.md links bench/rtxpro6000-2gpu/BUILD-SIRIUS-STARROCKS.md via ../../../../ and cites 'Raw numbers in bench/rtxpro6000-2gpu/ history'; notes/README.md and all five skills link into bench/ and notes/. Every link must be re-pointed or inlined, and the relocations proposed above (tools/ and BUILDING.md) change the depth of several ../ chains.
- benchmarks/pinned/README.md says 'Branch with the pinning stack (feat/pin-table-cn until merged)' and DEMO.md opens 'This worktree is dev plus the repository-backed streaming work plus the multi-fragment-execution compute node'. Both read as branch-local scratch notes and must be rewritten for a merged world.
- bench/gb200-8gpu/sweep.sh does `sed -i` on the tracked file experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/queries/q11.sql and restores it with `git checkout HEAD --` in an EXIT trap. In a shared checkout (or if the trap does not fire) this silently mutates a committed query. Generate the patched q11 into $OUT instead.
- docs/pinned-tables-repro.md hardcodes /home/ubuntu/sirius/src/compression/simpatico_codegen/plans/tpch_sf1000 and /opt/dlami/nvme/tpch/... . Harmless as illustrative paths but they should be repo-relative or obviously-placeholder.
- The 'Measured reference' tables in docs/pinned-tables-repro.md and benchmarks/pinned/README.md are single-box, single-date measurements presented as fact. Upstream will treat them as claims. Either keep the box/date/SF qualifiers prominent (they currently are) or move the numbers behind a 'measured on <box>, not a TPC-compliant benchmark' disclaimer like the one benchmarks/tpch/README.md already carries in its Fairness notes.

UPSTREAM: Very low conflict risk — this slice is almost entirely new files in directories upstream does not touch. Checked against upstream-pr/dev (84ea4ab5, 12 commits ahead of DEV): the only overlap in docs/ is `git diff origin/dev upstream-pr/dev --stat -- CONTRIBUTING.md docs/ .github/` = CONTRIBUTING.md (+122, the #1664 reviewability section), docs/sirius-for-research.md (+47, new), docs/super-sirius/{configuration.md +9, operators.md +27, optimizations.md +27, physical-plan-generation.md +24}, .github/pull_request_template.md (+4/-2). None of those files are touched by this slice. docs/super-sirius/README.md is NOT modified upstream, so the index-row and reading-order hunks apply clean. docs/README.md is NOT modified upstream. The three modified files in the whole slice are docs/README.md (+4/-1), docs/super-sirius/README.md (+4/-3) and .claude/settings.json (+1/-1); everything else is an add. Two second-order notes: (1) the `<!-- last-updated-commit: f03762fa -->` trailer at the bottom of docs/super-sirius/README.md will need bumping and is the kind of line the `update-docs` skill also rewrites, so expect a trivial conflict if another slice touches it; (2) upstream #1547 (pinned-entry lookup in the scan manager) and #1555 (per-chunk MVCC fused scan-filter) both land in src/scan_manager/, which is the code layer 3's pinning documentation describes — re-verify the `serves operator ... as a file subset: N/M files` log line still reads that way on upstream dev before quoting it in the doc, since #1547 reworked the pinned-entry lookup path. Also confirm the byte-range stack (#1636-#1639, based on a5cfa8fd and 21 dev commits stale) does not resurface a competing scan-range doc.

DESCRIPTION OUTLINE:
(Title above is for LAYER 1 only. Layer 2: `bench(starrocks): multi-CN TPC-H harness, build runbook, and DuckDB correctness oracle`. Layer 3: `bench(starrocks): reproducible pinned-vs-unpinned TPC-H kit`. Layer 4: `bench(starrocks): two-host CN bring-up over NVLink`.)

LAYER 1 DESCRIPTION OUTLINE

Motivation. `pin_table` / `unpin_table` and compressed pinning have been in the tree since #1474 and the compressed-pinning work, but there is no end-to-end procedure a user can follow to see what pinning buys them, and no worked example of the compression-plan directory. docs/README.md mentions the two tiers in four lines and stops. Separately, docs/super-sirius/README.md's index row for Streaming Sessions and its numbered reading order have drifted from the doc they describe: streaming-sessions.md gained a `### Partition fan-out` section (`enum class partition_mode { hash, broadcast }`, per-key cast types, broadcast replication semantics) in #1481, and Streaming Sessions was never added to the reading order, so Configuration and Optimizations are misnumbered.

What changed.
1. New `docs/pinned-tables-repro.md` — a step-by-step reproduction of the three hot-run modes (Pinned GPU, Pinned host, Pinned Host/GPU Compressed) over a single TPC-H Q1-shaped query on lineitem. Covers the prerequisite dataset generation, per-mode `CALL pin_table(...)` invocations, how compression-plan resolution actually works (one `<table_name>.<ext>` file per table in `pin_table_input_compression_plan_dir`; a table with no matching plan is pinned *uncompressed* and the call still succeeds — silent non-engagement is the failure mode, not an error), how to verify each mode really engaged, the optional knobs, an outcome-summary table, and a measured SF100 reference on an RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell showing 6.2x (GPU), 6.1x (GPU compressed), 5.1x (host compressed) and 2.1x (host) against the unpinned baseline with byte-identical results.
2. `docs/README.md` — four lines pointing the existing pin_table section at the new guide and naming `SET pin_table_compression = true`.
3. `docs/super-sirius/README.md` — index row now names partition fan-out; reading order gains Streaming Sessions at 11 and renumbers Configuration/Optimizations to 12/13.
4. `.claude/settings.json` — the pre-commit cleanliness hook prompt gains a STEP 0 that returns `allow` and stops without running any tools when the intercepted command is not a `git commit`. The `\"if\": \"Bash(git commit*)\"` matcher over-fires, so today an unrelated `ls` or `pkill` can pay a 180-second agent round trip.

How it was verified. All internal links resolved. The guide was executed end to end at SF100 on a single RTX PRO 6000: results byte-identical across all four modes, timings as tabulated. The note that `nvidia-smi` cannot show the pin footprint (Sirius pre-claims 95% of VRAM into its pool, so `memory.used` is near-constant) was confirmed the hard way and is called out in the doc.

Intentionally not handled. The StarRocks-compute-node pinning path — `ADMIN SET FRONTEND CONFIG (\"files_query_whole_file_ranges\" = \"true\")`, `ADMIN EXECUTE ON <node_id> 'pin_table ...'`, and per-CN file-subset serving — is a separate section of this guide held back until the FFI, scan-manager and FE changes it depends on have merged. Same for the `ADMIN EXECUTE` 600 s ceiling note.

Raw material for later layers: draft PRs #1686 (feat/pin-table-cn umbrella), #1674, #1672 and the byte-range stack #1636/#1637/#1638/#1639 carry usable prose for the pinning and scan-range motivation sections; #1644 (Stream fragment execution, 29 commits) and #1598 (Rust Fragment bindings) cover the multi-fragment ground that DEMO.md and TUNABLES.md sit on top of in layer 2. All are to be closed and replaced.

OPEN QUESTIONS:
- Do the maintainers want a top-level `bench/` at all beyond the existing `bench/sf1000-repro/`? Everything I recommend landing outside `docs/` sits under `experimental/starrocks/`, and the two survivors currently in `bench/` (common/RETARGETING.md + gen-tpch.sh, rtxpro6000-2gpu/tools/*, BUILD-SIRIUS-STARROCKS.md) are all proposed for relocation into `experimental/starrocks/`. If `bench/` is meant to hold per-box campaign folders, some of the excluded material could go there instead of being dropped — but then it needs a policy on stale results.
- test/tpch_performance/starrocks_performance_test.py (912 lines) and experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/pinned/ (6 files) are two independent drivers for the same pinned-vs-unpinned measurement. Which is the supported entry point? Landing both invites drift; landing only pinned/ loses the shared CLI shape with the existing performance_test.py.
- Should the trap knowledge currently living only in .claude/skills (CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES wins over --gpu-device; grep -c true over-counts Alive; QUERY_TIMEOUT=30 collapses hangs and refusals; bench.sh arg shifting; setup-engine-b.sh rewriting mem_limit every run) be inlined into the scripts' headers, or should the fixes simply land and the traps stop existing? I recommend fixing the two that are correctness-affecting (Alive count, CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES) and documenting the rest — but that turns layer 2 from a pure move into a change, which needs a reviewer with CN context.
- bench/rtxpro6000-2gpu/SIRIUS-TUNING-RUNBOOK.md (348 lines) is described by cn-tuning/SKILL.md as 'how the knobs work' (method, not values) and is the only tuning doc with a claim to portability. Is it worth a pass to strip the box-specific numbers and land it as experimental/starrocks/docs/TUNING.md, or is CN tuning too unsettled to document upstream yet? Note it contains a retired formula (STAGING ~= 96 GiB x SF/500 / N) that must be removed, not just annotated.
- The .claude/settings.json hook fix and the five .claude/skills are separable questions. The hook fix is clearly desirable. Are project-local skills something this repo wants to accumulate upstream at all? DEV already carries 17 under .claude/skills, so the precedent exists — but these five are qualitatively different (box facts and absolute paths rather than repo workflows). If the answer is yes-but-generalized, that is a follow-up PR after the runbooks land, not part of this stack.
- experimental/starrocks/scripts/cn-distribution.py is 724 lines — larger than most of the code slices in this campaign — and is pure operator tooling with no tests. Own PR, or fold into layer 2 and accept the size? It is also the only artifact that directly answers 'did the query actually fan out across all N GPUs', which is the question the whole Q1/Q6-many-GPU effort exists to answer, so it may deserve more prominence than a scripts/ directory.
- notes/OPEN.md is the only synthesis of live defects (PLAN-01 copy-out-on-arrival, PLAN-02 park ownership + cancel_plan_fragment stub, PLAN-04 scheduler stall, M1.4/M1.5 timeouts, #31 unhandled sink). If notes/ does not land, should these be filed as upstream issues so the PR descriptions in the other slices can say 'intentionally doesn't handle X, tracked in #NNN' as CONTRIBUTING.md item 3 asks?

## 2. CI, test registration, formatting, upstream conflicts, gh prerequisites
## CI JOBS
- Validate / validate (aggregator over `pr-title` + `pr-body`): amannn/action-semantic-pull-request enforces a Conventional-Commits PR TITLE (it becomes the squash commit); a second job strips HTML comments from the PR body. Runs on pull_request_target, so it fires for every PR including stacked/ ones. File: .github/workflows/validate.yml (unchanged DEV->SOT->UP).
- Check / lint (ubuntu-24.04, CONDA_OVERRIDE_CUDA=13): (a) `bash test/tpch_performance/test_sweep_threads.sh` harness smoke test, then (b) `pre-commit run --show-diff-on-failure --all-files` using a pixi GLOBAL `pre-commit` environment. This is the job that runs clang-format, black, cmake-format/cmake-lint, codespell, rumdl and the check-orphan-tests hook.
- Check / build (matrix). On a pull_request only two rows run: (x64, gcc, release, default, stable) and (x64, clang, debug, default, stable) -> `make release -j` / `make clang-debug -j` with CUDAARCHS=100 on self-hosted cpu-lg. On merge_group / push-to-dev four MORE rows run: (arm64, gcc, release), (arm64, clang, debug), (x64, gcc, release, vcpkg env, 240 min), and (arm64, gcc, release, nightly cudf, continue-on-error). A PR can be green and still break in the merge queue on arm64 / vcpkg.
- Check / build -> 'Rust bindings checks' step. Runs ONLY on the (x64, gcc, release, default, stable) row: `pixi run cargo fmt --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml --package sirius --package sirius-sys --check`; `pixi run cargo clippy --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml --package sirius --package sirius-sys --all-targets -- -D warnings`; `pixi run cargo test --no-run --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml --package sirius --package sirius-sys` (compile+link only, never executed — GPU bring-up is not available on the CPU runner).
- Check / check (aggregator; the branch-protection required check for lint+build).
- Test / build (matrix cuda 13 on PRs, cuda 12 additionally on merge_group/dev), self-hosted cpu-xl, sccache: `git submodule update --init --depth=1 duckdb substrait cucascade`, then `make -j$(nproc)` with CUDAARCHS=75; tars build/release/ into an artifact.
- Test / test-run, self-hosted gpu-2xt4, SIRIUS_LOG_LEVEL=trace, 60 min: (1) `timeout 45m ./build/release/extension/sirius/test/cpp/sirius_unittest --abort` — the FULL Catch2 suite, no tag filter; (2) `SIRIUS_EXP_LATE_MAT=1 timeout 20m ./build/release/.../sirius_unittest --abort "[late_mat],[deferred_query],[native_filter]"` — the only tag-filtered run in CI; (3) `./setup_test_datasets.sh` + `./build/release/duckdb -f scripts/tpch_to_parquet.sql`; (4) `./test/tpch_performance/benchmark_and_validate.sh 1` TPC-H SF1 snapshot (validation errors only hard-fail under merge_group, they are a warning on PRs).
- Test / test (aggregator).
- Experimental / starrocks — PATH-FILTERED: only triggers on `pull_request` touching `experimental/**` or `.github/workflows/experimental.yml` (plus merge_group). ubuntu-24.04, CONDA_OVERRIDE_CUDA=13, pixi manifest experimental/starrocks/pixi.toml env `cn`. Steps: `git submodule update --init --depth=1 --jobs 2 experimental/starrocks/starrocks experimental/starrocks/brpc`; `cargo fmt --package sirius-starrocks-cn --package starrocks-plan-translator --package starrocks-thrift -- --check`; `cargo clippy --all-targets --no-default-features -- -D warnings`; `cargo test --workspace --no-default-features`.
- Experimental / experimental (aggregator).
- Distribution / cuda-13, cuda-12, distribution — every job is `if: github.event_name != 'pull_request'`, so this workflow is effectively skipped on PRs and only runs in the merge queue / on dev via sirius-db/extension-ci-tools.
- NOTE: SOT commit 0bc03cca did NOT touch .github/ at all — `git diff origin/dev origin/feat/pin-table-cn -- .github/` is empty. DEV..UP touches only .github/pull_request_template.md (2/2). So every carved PR inherits exactly the DEV workflow set above; no workflow edits are needed EXCEPT the starrocks-patch problem described in rust_ci_constraints.

## RUST CI
TWO independent Rust workspaces, checked very differently.

(1) rust/ (sirius-sys + sirius), checked inside Check/build on ONE matrix row only (x64, gcc, release, default env, stable cudf): `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test --no-run`. Consequences for the ffi-fragment-rust slice (rust/crates/sirius/src/lib.rs +2488/-202, sirius-sys/src/lib.rs +159/-57): the new bindings must COMPILE, LINK against the libsirius built earlier in the same job (build.rs discovers build/release), be rustfmt-clean and clippy-clean at -D warnings INCLUDING `#[cfg(test)]` code (clippy runs --all-targets). None of the Rust tests actually execute in CI — a wrong-but-compiling FFI binding ships green, so the PR description must state how it was verified on a GPU box. Also: only the two named packages are checked, so adding a third crate under rust/ would silently escape fmt/clippy unless the workflow's package list is extended. Upstream (DEV..UP) touches nothing under rust/ — zero merge exposure for this slice.

(2) experimental/starrocks (sirius-starrocks-cn + starrocks-plan-translator + starrocks-thrift), checked in Experimental/starrocks with `--no-default-features` for BOTH clippy and test, and a package-scoped `cargo fmt --check` (`--all` is deliberately avoided because the `sirius` path dep would drag in the sibling rust/ workspace). SOT flips `default = ["sirius-engine"]` to `default = ["sirius-engine", "nixl-transport"]` and adds `nixl-sys = { version = "1.3", optional = true }`; since CI always passes --no-default-features, libnixl is never needed by CI and that part is safe.

BLOCKER for any CN slice that carries the nixl exchange RPCs: SOT's experimental/starrocks/build.rs adds `require_exchange_proto_patch()` (+50 lines) which runs UNCONDITIONALLY (build scripts do not see cargo features) and returns Err unless experimental/starrocks/starrocks/gensrc/proto/internal_service.proto contains `message PExchangeNixlMd`. That message only exists after `patches/nixl-exchange-proto.patch` is applied to the starrocks submodule, and the CI job only does `git submodule update --init` — it never applies patches (there is no `pixi run apply-starrocks-patches` step in experimental.yml, and the submodule gitlink is identical on DEV/SOT/UP: 14b7e3fa). So `cargo clippy --no-default-features` and `cargo test --workspace --no-default-features` will both fail at build-script time. Worse, even without the guard the build would fail anyway: experimental/starrocks/src/compute_node_service.rs imports `PExchangeNixlMd, PExchangeNixlMdResult, PStagingLeaseRequest, PStagingLeaseResult, PTransmitPackedParams, PTransmitPackedResult` and `use crate::nixl_transport::{...}` at the top of the file with NO `#[cfg(feature = "nixl-transport")]` gate. Any PR that lands those handlers MUST either (a) add a patch-apply step to .github/workflows/experimental.yml (and probably make `apply-starrocks-patches` a dependency of the CI path), or (b) feature-gate the RPC handlers + proto imports so the pure-Rust `--no-default-features` build still compiles. Pick this consciously and say so in the PR description — it is the single most likely way a CN PR turns the Experimental job red.

Other notes: pre-commit's global exclude contains `^experimental/.*`, so NOTHING under experimental/ is touched by clang-format/black/codespell/rumdl/trailing-whitespace — Rust style there is enforced solely by the `cargo fmt --check` step, and the `.patch` files are also excluded globally (`\.patch$`) precisely so formatters don't corrupt them. SOT's experimental/starrocks/pixi.toml diff adds tasks only (cluster2, fe-check, apply-starrocks-patches, and cn-build/cn-test/cn-run rewritten to `source scripts/cn-env.sh` with NIXL_NO_STUBS_FALLBACK=1) — no new conda dependencies, so the pixi lock stays valid and setup-pixi keeps working. cn-build / cn-test are the GPU+engine+libnixl tasks and are NOT used by CI; CI's pure-Rust equivalent is the pre-existing `cn-test-no-engine` task.

## CPP TEST REG
One Catch2 binary, one explicit source list, no globbing.

- Top-level /home/prestouser/aocsa/sirius/CMakeLists.txt: `set(TEST_SOURCES ...)` starts at line 673 (DEV) and runs to ~line 887; `add_executable(sirius_unittest ${TEST_SOURCES})` at line 912; links sirius_extension + duckdb_static. `SIRIUS_LEGACY_TEST_SOURCES` is appended if src/legacy exists, and a `SIRIUS_BUILD_S3_TESTS` block appends six S3 tests. Every new test/cpp/**/test_*.cpp must be added to TEST_SOURCES by hand.
- Enforcement: the `check-orphan-tests` pre-commit hook (scripts/check_orphan_tests.py, files regex `^(test/cpp/.*\.cpp|CMakeLists\.txt|test/cpp/orphan_test_allowlist\.txt)$`) fails when a test_*.cpp exists in the tree but is not referenced anywhere in CMakeLists.txt. The only escape is test/cpp/orphan_test_allowlist.txt, whose header says it "should only ever shrink". This runs in Check/lint, so an unregistered test file is a red CI check, not just a lost test.
  -> Concretely: SOT's test/cpp/exec/test_stream_lifecycle.cpp (343 lines) is NEW on SOT, is NOT in SOT's CMakeLists.txt, and is NOT in the allowlist. Shipping it would fail Check/lint. This corroborates the decision to drop the stream_lifecycle files from every PR.
- SOT's CMakeLists.txt delta is exactly 5 added lines, in 5 different regions: EXTENSION_SOURCES +src/exec/exchange_staging_arena.cpp (~line 291, exec block), +src/op/scan/parquet_byte_range.cpp (~337, scan block), +src/planner/substrait_scan_ranges.cpp (~399, planner block); TEST_SOURCES +test/cpp/exec/test_exchange_staging_arena.cpp (~688), +test/cpp/scan/test_parquet_byte_range.cpp (~867). Every other SOT-touched test file (test_streaming_fragment.cpp, test_gpu_merge_impl.cpp, test_can_serve_with_columns.cpp, test_stream_bind_catalog.cpp, test_build_probe_scheduling.cpp, test_cached_serving_hardening.cpp) already exists on DEV and is already registered, so those slices need no CMakeLists change. test/cpp/utils/pipeline_conversion_test_utils.hpp is a header and is not registered.
- Running: `make test` == `make test_release` == build the release preset then run `./build/release/extension/sirius/test/cpp/sirius_unittest` (whole suite). Debug/reldebug/ci-release variants exist. CI adds `--abort` and the one tag-filtered late-mat pass. Test logs land in build/release/extension/sirius/test/cpp/log/ and are uploaded as artifacts.
- Catch2 tags are used for filtering only; nothing registers by tag. Tags introduced/used by the SOT delta: `[staging_arena]`; `[parquet_byte_range]`, `[parquet_byte_range][scan]`; `[integration][streaming_fragment]`, `[integration][streaming_fragment][stream_cardinality]`, `[integration][streaming_fragment][empty_input_join]`, `[integration][streaming_fragment_control]`; `[operator][deterministic_agg]` plus `[operator][merge_*]`; `[scan][can_serve]`; `[stream_bind_catalog]`; `[hash_join][build_probe][unit]` and friends; `[cached_serving][scan_manager]`. None of these overlap the late-mat CI tag set (`[late_mat],[deferred_query],[native_filter]`), so they are only exercised by the full run.
- Upstream's CMakeLists delta (13/1) adds group_key_labels.cpp, sirius_physical_dense_count_join.cpp, cuvs_index_cache.cpp, dense_count_join_impl.cu, ivf_flat_index.cu and 6 new test files in the SAME lists but at different offsets; git 3-way merges it cleanly with SOT's 5 lines (verified with git merge-tree: CMakeLists.txt is "changed in both" but produces no conflict markers).

## FORMATTING
.pre-commit-config.yaml and .clang-format are byte-identical on DEV, SOT and UP — no PR needs to touch them.

Global pre-commit exclude: `\.csv$|\.patch$|^legacy/queries/.*|^experimental/.*|^src/include/third_party/.*`. Two consequences that matter for this carve-up: (1) the ENTIRE experimental/ tree — the Rust CN, its markdown, its Python, its configs — is exempt from every hook (cargo fmt in the Experimental workflow is the only style gate there); (2) `patches/*.patch` are exempt by design, because end-of-file-fixer / trailing-whitespace would strip the leading context space on blank lines and break `git apply`.

Hooks that will actually bite the carved PRs:
- clang-format (mirrors-clang-format v20.1.4, `-fallback-style=none -style=file -i`, types_or c++/c/cuda) over everything under src/ and test/cpp/. Run `pre-commit run -a` before pushing; CI runs it with --show-diff-on-failure.
- black 25.1.0 on Python — this covers the NEW test/tpch_performance/starrocks_performance_test.py (912 lines) and tpch_pin_columns.py (52 lines), which are NOT under the experimental/ exclusion.
- cmake-format + cmake-lint (line-width 220, --disabled-codes=C0307) on CMakeLists.txt — the 5 added source/test lines must keep the list formatting/sorting style.
- codespell v2.4.1 with --ignore-words=.codespell_words and --write-changes; long prose comments in the SOT diff are the usual tripwire.
- rumdl via `pixi run --locked rumdl check --fix` (local hook, system language) on all markdown outside experimental/. .rumdl.toml enables ONLY MD051 (link fragments must resolve) and MD057 (relative links must point at files that exist). This is the real hazard for the docs/bench/notes slices: docs/pinned-tables-repro.md, docs/README.md, docs/super-sirius/README.md, the 78 bench/ files and 51 notes/ files must not link to a file that lands in a LATER PR of the stack — MD057 will fail Check/lint on the earlier PR. Note the hook shells out to `pixi run --locked`, so the root pixi env/lock must resolve locally too.
- check-added-large-files (default 500 KB, with an exception only for test/cpp/integration/data/duckdb/*.duckdb). I checked every file added DEV..SOT: nothing exceeds 400 KB, so the bench/notes/benchmark kits are safe.
- texthooks fix-smartquotes, end-of-file-fixer, trailing-whitespace, mixed-line-ending, pretty-format-json --autofix (any JSON under bench/ or configs/ gets reformatted), check-yaml / check-toml / check-json, detect-private-key (relevant for the CN configs/ and conf/fe.conf), check-symlinks (its exclude list names specific .claude/ symlinks — new .claude/skills/ files should be real files, not links).
- check-orphan-tests (see cpp_test_registration).

Also in Check/lint before pre-commit: `bash test/tpch_performance/test_sweep_threads.sh` must keep passing — any PR that edits the test/tpch_performance harness scripts should run it locally.

(1) Include block: SOT adds `#include "op/aggregate/aggregate_op_util.hpp"`, upstream adds `#include "op/aggregate/group_key_labels.hpp"` on the same line after log/logging.hpp. (2) The comment block immediately above `constexpr cudf::size_type label_encode_min_rows`: SOT inserts its ~60-line bit-stable-float-sum rationale + `use_canonical_sorted_groupby` machinery in local_grouped_aggregate, while #1605 deletes 43 lines from that same function (it extracts cudf::encode into detail::make_group_key_labels in the new src/op/aggregate/group_key_labels.cpp) and rewrites the surrounding comments and the catch ladder (adds a `catch (const rmm::cuda_error&)`). - [likely-conflict] src/creator/task_creator.cpp <- OPEN DRAFT PR #1642 (Jedi18, fix/completion-quiescence-protocol) — NOT in upstream-pr/dev (slice: query-watchdog / completion (empty-stream completion for streaming fragments)) Highest-risk overlap in the whole delta, and it is a DESIGN collision, not just a textual one. #1642 rewrites manager_loop wholesale: it re-indents the entire `_bounded_pool->dispatch(...)` lambda, replaces the exact comment block above `std::sort(visited_pipelines...)` that SOT's insertion sits under, adds a completion-handler work-slot ledger (`handler->acquire_work()` in schedule() and schedule_lookahead(), request->work_slot carried through the pool hand-off), adds _shutdown_mutex/_completion_handler_mutex, and changes drain_pending_tasks(bool reactivate). SOT's block (exhausted head -> update_pipeline_status(false) -> complete_query_if_finished()) targets the SAME failure class #1642 calls 'completion quiescence' (a stream that ends without ever carrying a batch leaves nobody to signal completion). Decide early which one wins: if #1642 lands first, SOT's hunk almost certainly becomes redundant or must be re-expressed in terms of the work ledger. Verified by fetching https://github.com/sirius-db/sirius/pull/1642.diff (public, no auth needed; the GitHub REST API is rate-limited from this box but the .diff endpoint works).

Expect a real conflict.
- [likely-conflict] src/pipeline/task_scheduler.cpp <- OPEN DRAFT PR #1642 (not in upstream-pr/dev) (slice: query-watchdog / completion) No upstream-dev change at all (this file is absent from the DEV..UP diff), so it is conflict-free against upstream-pr/dev. But SOT's +29 lines implement complete_query_if_finished() (calls _completion_handler->mark_completed() without draining, deliberately, because the caller is the creator's manager thread) and fail_stalled_query() (calls _completion_handler->report_error(...)), and #1642 rebuilds the completion protocol around those same handler calls plus a work ledger and preserved-error handoff.
- [likely-conflict] src/sirius_engine.cpp <- OPEN DRAFT PR #1642 (not in upstream-pr/dev) (slice: query-watchdog / completion) Both sides edit the identical three lines in execute(): SOT replaces `future.get();` with `wait_for_query_future(future, *sirius_ctx);`, while #1642 keeps `future.get()` and inserts a `catch (const pipeline::premature_completion_error& e)` clause that calls drain_after_error() and take_preserved_error(). Same try/catch, same 5-line window.
- [none] (no overlap) rust/**, experimental/starrocks/src/**, experimental/starrocks/crates/**, src/exec/**, src/op/scan/**, src/planner/substrait_scan_ranges.cpp, src/pin_table.cpp, src/include/pin_table.hpp, src/sirius_ffi.{hpp,cpp}, src/op/merge/gpu_merge_impl.cpp, src/op/sirius_physical_ungrouped_aggregate.cpp, src/include/op/aggregate/aggregate_op_util.hpp, src/op/aggregate/aggregate_op_util.cpp, src/exec/streaming_fragment.cpp, docs/** <- none of the 12 upstream commits touch these (slice: staging-arena, ffi-fragment-rust, cardinality, byte-range core, pin-table core, all StarRocks-CN slices, docs) Verified by intersecting `git diff --name-only origin/dev origin/feat/pin-table-cn` with `git diff --name-only origin/dev upstream-pr/dev`: the intersection is exactly 13 files, all listed above. Everything else in the DEV..SOT delta — including the entire 169-file experimental/ tree, both rust/ crates, the FFI, the staging arena, substrait_scan_ranges, parquet_byte_range and the aggregate util — rebases onto upstream dev untouched. ## GH PREREQS
gh is NOT available on this box and nothing in the repo provides it.
- `command -v gh` -> not found. pixi IS installed (/home/prestouser/.pixi/bin/pixi); cargo and git are present; conda/mamba/micromamba are not.
- No pixi manifest or lock in the repo references gh / github-cli: grepping pixi.toml and pixi.lock (root) and experimental/starrocks/pixi.toml on DEV returns nothing (the only 'gh' hit is a comment about "GH workflows" next to the sccache pin). The materialized env /home/prestouser/aocsa/sirius/.pixi/envs/default has no bin/gh either.
- So gh must be installed out-of-band before any stacked work. Two viable routes given what is installed: `pixi global install gh` (conda-forge ships the `gh` package; this is the least invasive and matches how CI gets pre-commit — via a pixi GLOBAL environment, not a project dependency), or the upstream tarball from https://cli.github.com/. Do NOT add gh to the repo's pixi.toml — CI never needs it and it would churn pixi.lock. After install: `gh auth login`, then `gh extension install github/gh-stack` (CONTRIBUTING names gh-stack as the ONLY supported stacking tool; explicitly forbids mixing Graphite, git-spice or ghstack on the same branches).

CONTRIBUTING.md (upstream-pr/dev, 305 lines; 122 of them added by 84ea4ab5 #1664) requirements for this effort:
1. Branching. Self-contained PRs from a personal fork are the PRIMARY method. Stacked PRs are a SECONDARY method restricted to MAINTAINERS, must be pushed to the sirius-db/sirius repo itself, and every branch must be prefixed `stacked/` (e.g. stacked/scan-byte-ranges). Confirm aocsa has maintainer/push rights on sirius-db/sirius before committing to the stacked plan; if not, the carve-up must ship as independent self-contained PRs from the fork instead.
2. Remote naming — this worktree does NOT currently satisfy it. `git remote -v` shows only `origin -> git@github.com:aocsa/sirius.git`; there is no remote pointing at sirius-db/sirius at all (refs/remotes/upstream-pr/* exist but with no matching remote config). CONTRIBUTING requires, verbatim: `git remote rename origin $USER` then `git remote add origin "git@github.com:sirius-db/sirius.git"` — because gh stack's `--remote` flag is broken (gh-stack#381) and it only ever operates on `origin`. Doing this rewrites remote config for the shared clone, so coordinate before running it (this scout session made no changes).
3. Merging is BOTTOM-UP and manual. Do NOT use "Enqueue stack" (Web UI) or `gh stack merge` (CLI) — CONTRIBUTING says both were tested against this repo's merge queue (1 PR at a time, squash) and do not reliably merge the whole stack. Instead: open the bottom-most unmerged PR, confirm approval + required checks, click "Enqueue pull request" on that PR alone, wait for it to clear the queue (GitHub auto-retargets the next layer's base to dev), then `gh stack sync --prune` if the next layer shows conflicts, verify with `gh stack view` (merged layer shows a checkmark under a `--- merged ---` marker, no warning icons remain), and repeat. Conflicts during development are resolved with `gh stack rebase` then `gh stack submit`; always via the CLI, never the Web UI "Rebase stack" button.
4. PR reviewability checklist (the gate this whole carve-up exists to satisfy): (a) state the MOTIVATION, not the mechanics — `Closes #NNN` / `Refs #NNN` if an issue exists, otherwise the description itself must explain why; (b) scope to ONE reviewable sitting — split it or explicitly justify in the description why it cannot be split; (c) description must be SELF-SUFFICIENT: what changed, why, how it was verified, and anything non-obvious ("intentionally doesn't handle X, tracked in #NNN"); (d) OPEN AS A DRAFT until (a)-(c) are actually true, then flip to "Ready for review" — that flip is what triggers CODEOWNERS review requests and auto-assign.yml, so converting early pings a reviewer before there is anything to review. This directly governs closing #1686/#1674/#1672/#1644/#1598/#1636-#1639 and reopening as drafts.
5. Titles are Conventional Commits (`<type>(scope): <description>`), enforced by the Validate/pr-title check, and become the squash commit message. Scope optional; use it when the change is localized (fix(join):, ci(distribution):).
6. Reviewers are auto-routed by .github/CODEOWNERS to component teams (sirius-core, sirius-io, sirius-integrations, sirius-telemetry, sirius-cmake, sirius-build, sirius-docs, sirius-rust, sirius-python); any maintainer's approval suffices. Branches on the main repo are auto-deleted on merge.
7. Config changes must be documented inline where the config lives AND summarized in the PR description — relevant to SIRIUS_QUERY_WATCHDOG_SECS, SIRIUS_EXCHANGE_STAGING_BYTES, files_query_whole_file_ranges and the CN tunables.

## NOTES
Method: conflict exposure was measured with `git merge-tree origin/dev origin/feat/pin-table-cn upstream-pr/dev` (the read-only, non-writing form — no refs, objects or working-tree files were touched), cross-checked against per-file `git diff --stat` and hunk-offset comparison. Of 13 files changed on BOTH sides, exactly 4 produce real conflict markers today: gpu_aggregate_impl.cpp (2 markers), sirius_scan_manager.cpp, sirius_extension.cpp, sirius_physical_partition.cpp. The other 9 auto-merge.

Sequencing implication: the conflict-free mass is large. Nothing upstream touches rust/, experimental/starrocks/src|crates, src/exec/, src/op/scan/, substrait_scan_ranges, pin_table.cpp, sirius_ffi.*, gpu_merge_impl.cpp or docs/. If TPC-H Q1/Q6 across many GPUs is the priority, the slices that carry the least upstream drag are staging-arena, ffi-fragment-rust/cardinality and the byte-range core; the ones that need a real rebase are two-phase-agg-exchange (#1605) and anything touching the pinned-entry matcher (#1547/#1555).

Two hard CI gotchas worth deciding on before writing any PR:
1. experimental/starrocks/build.rs's require_exchange_proto_patch will fail the Experimental job, because CI checks out an UNPATCHED starrocks submodule and never runs apply-starrocks-patches — and compute_node_service.rs imports the patched proto types with no feature gate, so the `--no-default-features` build cannot compile either way. Either add a patch-apply step to .github/workflows/experimental.yml as part of the first CN PR, or cfg-gate the three RPC handlers. Note also that the Experimental workflow is PATH-FILTERED (`experimental/**`), so a PR that only touches src/ will never exercise it — and a CN PR that also edits the workflow will.
2. test/cpp/exec/test_stream_lifecycle.cpp is a NEW file on SOT that is in neither CMakeLists.txt nor test/cpp/orphan_test_allowlist.txt, so the check-orphan-tests pre-commit hook would turn Check/lint red if it were ever included. Independent confirmation that the stream_lifecycle trio must stay out of every PR.

Merge-queue-only risk: PR CI runs a reduced Check/build matrix (x64 gcc-release + x64 clang-debug only). arm64 gcc/clang, the vcpkg environment (240 min) and the cuda-12 Test rows only run in merge_group/on dev. New headers, new template code, or anything with narrowing/format warnings can pass PR CI and fail during bottom-up merging — which, with a stack, blocks every layer above it. Worth a local `make clang-debug` before submitting.

Also observed, not asked about but relevant to the carve-up: SOT's .gitmodules change adds `ignore = dirty` to the starrocks submodule with a comment explaining that patches/*.patch are applied into the working tree by design — that hunk belongs with whichever PR introduces patches/ + apply-starrocks-patches.sh, not spread across slices. And the `.pixi` stray path plus experimental/starrocks/rmm_log.txt must be dropped (upstream's .gitignore already ignores envs/ and .cache but not these).
