######## PR #1232 fix(starrocks): combine complete scan splits
present_on_sot=superseded  recommendation=update-pr-then-merge

SOT changes beyond PR:
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/scan_paths.rs:ScanFilePaths::resolve_ranges — instead of refusing partial coverage it normalizes each file's ranges and emits explicit [start,length) splits (whole-file collapse is just the `coalesced == [(0, file_size)]` branch), which is what actually lets N CNs each own a slice of lineitem.parquet; the PR still refuses that shape
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/scan_paths.rs:ScanFile (new pub(crate) struct with `range: Option<(u64,u64)>`) and node_translator.rs:local_files_rel — the split rides the wire as FileOrFiles.start/length, with `(0,0)` reserved as the whole-file encoding; the PR validates ByteRange and throws it away
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/scan_paths.rs:ScanFilePaths::add_ranges — refuses `TScanRangeParams.has_more == Some(true)` ("incremental scan-range delivery (has_more) is not supported") and skips `empty == Some(true)` placeholder ranges; the PR has neither, so a fragment whose ranges arrive incrementally is validated as if the prefix were the whole assignment
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/scan_paths.rs:ScanFilePaths::check_range — refuses an outer `compression_type` on a parquet range (a compressed container the reader would have to unwrap); absent from DEV and from the PR
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/scan_paths.rs:ScanFilePaths::resolve_ranges — explicit refusals for `end > file_size` ("byte range extends past the end of the parquet file"), negative start offset, and negative size other than -1; the PR's sweep accepts a range that overshoots EOF because it only tests `covered_until < file_size`
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/scan_paths.rs:ScanFilePaths::resolve_ranges — adjacent ranges coalesce before the whole-file test, and a node that ends up owning nothing is refused with "scan instance owns only empty byte ranges" rather than silently falling back to a named-table read
- src/include/planner/substrait_scan_ranges.hpp + src/planner/substrait_scan_ranges.cpp:scan_byte_ranges_state::{claim,assert_all_consumed} — the engine-side half that makes emitted ranges real: path canonicalization across `file:`/`file://`/bare spellings, single-shot per-file claim, and a loud error if any range reaches execution unclaimed ("executing anyway would read whole files and duplicate rows across splits"); plus src/op/scan/parquet_byte_range.cpp and the parquet_gpu_ingestible/scan-manager wiring
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/tests/translate.rs — split_broker_range_emits_a_ranged_local_file (pins the live 2-CN shape: 162140518-byte lineitem split at 81070259), open_ended_split_reads_to_the_end_of_the_file, split_past_the_end_of_the_file_is_refused, compressed_container_scan_range_is_refused, has_more_scan_ranges_are_refused; and test/cpp/scan/test_parquet_byte_range.cpp for the engine side
- experimental/starrocks/patches/files-query-whole-file-ranges.patch plus FE `Config.files_query_whole_file_ranges` — the FE-side escape hatch that stops FILES() queries from cutting splits at all, which is the only way the PR's whole-file-only rule holds on a real multi-instance cluster

Defects:
- [should-fix] experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/scan_paths.rs:ScanFilePaths::validate_complete_files (PR): The completeness rule still refuses the multi-CN TPC-H shape: whenever more than one scan instance is assigned to one parquet file, each instance holds a proper prefix/suffix, and translation fails with "byte-range splits do not tile the parquet file".
- [should-fix] experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/scan_paths.rs:ScanFilePaths::add_ranges (PR): `TScanRangeParams.has_more` is ignored, so with incremental scan-range delivery the tiling sweep is run over a prefix of the assignment; the refusal message then blames the FE's split layout for what is really an unimplemented delivery path (SOT refuses has_more explicitly).
- [should-fix] experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/scan_paths.rs:ScanFilePaths::resolve_ranges (SOT): A missing `file_size` on any range after the first is reported as "scan ranges disagree on the parquet file size", and an empty/negative file size is reported the same way — the two diagnostics nits the PR's fourth commit fixed and pinned with tests are absent on SOT.
- [should-fix] experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/scan_paths.rs:ScanFilePaths::resolve_ranges (SOT): The `scan_files.is_empty()` guard is per node rather than per path, so a file whose ranges are all empty is silently dropped from a node that also owns a second, non-empty file — no error, no rows from that file.
- [nit] experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/scan_paths.rs:ScanFilePaths::resolve_ranges (SOT): `by_node` is a HashMap, so when a fragment has several offending scan nodes the refusal names a different node from run to run; the PR deliberately used a BTreeMap (`CollectedRanges`) to make the error text deterministic and SOT dropped that.
- [blocking] experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/scan_paths.rs:ScanFilePaths::resolve_ranges + node_translator.rs:local_files_rel (SOT): The translator emits FileOrFiles.start/length unconditionally, so a CN running against an engine build without src/planner/substrait_scan_ranges.cpp silently degrades every split to a whole-file read and duplicates rows N times — the SOT slice cannot merge before the engine-side byte-range slice.
- [nit] experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/scan_paths.rs:ScanFilePaths::validate_complete_files (PR): A single range whose `size` overshoots EOF (start 0, size > file_size) passes the sweep because only `covered_until < file_size` is tested, so a malformed range is collapsed to a whole-file read instead of refused; SOT rejects it as "byte range extends past the end of the parquet file".
- [should-fix] https://github.com/sirius-db/sirius/pull/1232 (description): The description is two sentences plus a dependency link on #1111 — it does not state the motivation, the failure it prevents, or why completeness is the rule, so it does not meet CONTRIBUTING.md's self-sufficient-description bar even though the code comments do.

Tasks:
- Rewrite the PR #1232 description to state the motivation (DEV refuses every split, so a single-instance FILES() scan of a >min_bytes_per_broker_scanner parquet file fails outright), the rule (collapse only an exact tiling, refuse gaps/prefixes/overlaps), and an explicit note that partial splits are enabled later by the byte-range stack — then take it out of draft.
- Add `has_more == Some(true)` refusal and `empty == Some(true)` skip to ScanFilePaths::add_ranges in experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/scan_paths.rs, copying the SOT wording, with the has_more test from SOT's tests/translate.rs (has_more_scan_ranges_are_refused).
- Reword the `validate_complete_files` doc comment in the PR so it says byte ranges cannot be plumbed through *today* rather than as a permanent property, since origin/feat/pin-table-cn already plumbs them via FileOrFiles.start/length and src/planner/substrait_scan_ranges.cpp.
- Port the PR's two diagnostics fixes onto the SOT slice: in ScanFilePaths::resolve_ranges, check `ranges.iter().any(|r| r.file_size.is_none())` before the file-size agreement check, and give `file_size <= 0` its own "parquet scan range reports an empty or negative file size" branch.
- Make ScanFilePaths::resolve_ranges on origin/feat/pin-table-cn refuse per path rather than per node when a file's ranges are all empty, so a file owning zero bytes cannot be silently dropped from a node that also owns a non-empty file.
- Switch `by_node` (or the resolve_ranges iteration order) in experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/scan_paths.rs to a BTreeMap so a fragment with several offending scan nodes always reports the same node, as PR #1232's CollectedRanges does.
- Port PR #1232's tests that survive the SOT design into experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/tests/translate.rs: split_broker_ranges_in_descending_order_are_accepted, split_broker_ranges_disagreeing_on_file_size_are_unsupported, split_broker_range_missing_file_size_after_the_first_is_unsupported, and the reason-pinning version of empty_file_scan_range_is_unsupported.
- Add a translate.rs test for adjacent-range coalescing in ScanFilePaths::resolve_ranges (e.g. splits [(0,256),(256,256)] of a 1024-byte file yielding one item with start 0 and length 512) — SOT coalesces but nothing pins it.
- Sequence the DEV..SOT carve-up so the engine-side byte-range PR (src/planner/substrait_scan_ranges.{hpp,cpp}, src/op/scan/parquet_byte_range.cpp, src/op/scan/parquet_gpu_ingestible.cpp, test/cpp/scan/test_parquet_byte_range.cpp, CMakeLists.txt) merges strictly before the scan_paths.rs/node_translator.rs slice that emits FileOrFiles.start/length.
- Carve experimental/starrocks/patches/files-query-whole-file-ranges.patch and the FE Config.files_query_whole_file_ranges flag into their own PR and cross-link it from #1232, since that flag is what makes #1232's whole-file-only rule hold on a real multi-instance cluster.

Notes: Applicability: the PR's pre-image blobs (scan_paths.rs 76fa2afc09, tests/translate.rs 6836c3208d) are byte-identical on origin/dev AND upstream-pr/dev, and the API reports mergeable_state "clean" — it still applies to current upstream dev unchanged.

What the PR does: replaces DEV's per-range `whole_file` guard in check_range (which refused anything not provably covering the whole file) with fragment-wide collection into `CollectedRanges = BTreeMap<node, BTreeMap<path, Vec<ByteRange>>>` plus `validate_complete_files`, a strict tiling sweep (sort, `start != covered_until` refuses gaps AND overlaps, `covered_until < file_size` refuses prefixes). The paths themselves are deduped in add_ranges so a tiled file yields one local_files item. Four commits; the fourth is a diagnostics pass (missing-size checked before size-disagreement, empty file gets its own reason) with tests pinning the exact reason strings.

Relation to SOT: origin/feat/pin-table-cn's `add_ranges` is essentially line-identical to this PR's (same ByteRange struct, same `if !node_paths.contains(path)` dedup, same entry chain), but the ranges are stored on `self.byte_ranges` instead of an out-param, and `validate_complete_files` is replaced by `resolve_ranges`, which normalizes into `Vec<ScanFile>` and emits `FileOrFiles.start/length` via node_translator.rs:local_files_rel. So the PR's collection half is present verbatim on SOT; its decision half (refuse anything that is not a complete tiling) is deliberately reversed. The two behaviours the PR is titled for do survive on SOT: an exact tiling collapses to a single whole-file item (`coalesced == [(0, file_size)]`), and overlaps are refused ("overlapping byte ranges would read the same rows twice"). SOT even carries the PR's test `complete_split_broker_ranges_produce_one_local_file` under the same name. The PR's gap/prefix tests (split_broker_ranges_with_a_gap_are_unsupported, split_broker_ranges_covering_only_a_prefix_are_unsupported) assert refusals SOT intentionally no longer makes, so they must be deleted or inverted when the SOT slice lands.

Why merge it anyway rather than close it: it is self-contained in the Rust translator, needs nothing from the C++ side, is strictly fail-closed, and is a strict improvement over DEV for the case where one instance owns every split of a file (DEV refuses that today; with pipeline dop the FE routinely hands one instance several driver-sequence splits of one file, and the CN executes one merged plan per fragment instance so collapsing them is correct). Merging it also shrinks the eventual SOT slice to "swap validate_complete_files for resolve_ranges + ScanFile", because the collection scaffolding is already the same code.

Why it does not by itself unblock the campaign goal: for TPC-H Q1/Q6 across N CNs the FE splits lineitem.parquet across instances, each instance receives a proper sub-range, and this PR refuses it. The only two ways out are SOT's explicit-splits design (needs the engine-side byte-range work) or SOT's FE patch files-query-whole-file-ranges.patch, neither of which is in this PR. Q3 shapes add nothing new here — the constraint is per-file split assignment, not query shape.

Reviewer state: aocsa approved after the strict-tiling rework; dhruv9vats and wmalpica still pending; labels `! - P2`, `starrocks`; still draft.

######## PR #1233 fix(starrocks): materialize common project slots
present_on_sot=partially  recommendation=merge-as-is

SOT changes beyond PR:
- node_translator.rs:common_slots_consumed_above (line 499) + PlanContext.consumed_above (line 84) + translate_plan wiring (line 400): a pre-pass over the flat preorder plan that finds, per PROJECT_NODE, the non-materialized common slots an ANCESTOR (or the fragment's root output_exprs) references; the PR resolves common slots only inside the project that owns them, so the TPC-H q14 shape (sum(case when p_type like 'PROMO%' then <common 35> ...) above the project) still dies on a descriptor error with the PR alone
- node_translator.rs:translate_project_node tail (lines 2170-2205): emits the consumed common slots as extra trailing columns past the descriptor row, preferring the FE's own slot_map entry for the slot and falling back to the common binding, with a loud descriptor error when neither exists; returns CarriedSlot bindings so ancestors can resolve them
- expr_translator.rs:CarriedSlot + ExprContext::with_carried (lines 16-84) and expr_translator.rs:translate_slot_ref (lines 188-262): resolution order is exact (tuple,slot) override -> exact (tuple,slot) carried -> descriptor -> single-candidate carried by slot id; refuses when an override and a carried column both match the same key, and refuses an ambiguous multi-candidate by-slot-id fallback. The PR's version is only the exact-override branch
- node_translator.rs:refuse_carried_join_child (line 1880), called from translate_hash_join (1738-1739) and translate_nestloop_join (1933-1934): refuses a join child that physically carries common-expr columns, because slot_global_index computes the right side's offset from DESCRIPTOR widths and a wider child would shift every right-side column silently
- node_translator.rs:confine_carried (line 2338) called from lib.rs:320: drops the fragment root's carried columns back to the descriptor row BEFORE output names / the receiver's stream schema are derived, so carried columns never leave the fragment
- node_translator.rs:translate_aggregation (line 852): refuses a merge aggregation whose child carries common-expr columns, so the merge-state column remapping and the carried-column remapping can never silently combine
- node_translator.rs:TranslatedRel.carried_slots (line 53) with a written invariant (descriptor columns first, carried columns in the tail) and deliberate absence of Default, plus carried propagation through filter_rel, apply_conjuncts, sort (SortRel path), append_project and emit_columns
- tests/translate.rs: consumed_common_slot_is_carried_to_the_aggregate (5841), consumed_common_slot_without_slot_map_entry_falls_back_to_the_common_binding (5894), common_slot_consumed_only_inside_the_project_is_not_carried (5925), carried_common_slot_entering_a_join_is_refused (5960), common_slot_consumed_beyond_the_aggregate_fails_loudly (6030), root_filter_over_a_carried_common_slot_is_narrowed_at_the_root (6093), fragment_output_exprs_consume_a_common_slot (6140), sort_over_a_carried_common_slot_resolves_and_narrows (6164)
- expr_translator.rs unit tests: carried_by_slot_id_fallback_resolves_a_single_candidate (1213), ambiguous_carried_by_slot_id_fallback_is_refused (1230), duplicate_override_and_carried_key_is_refused (1258)

Defects:
- [should-fix] SOT experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_select / translate_hash_join / translate_nestloop_join (lines 359-366, 1727, 1922): SOT dropped the PR's reject_common_slots guard: a SELECT_NODE / HASH_JOIN_NODE / NESTLOOP_JOIN_NODE that carries its own common_slot_map is silently ignored instead of refused (the thrift IDL puts common_slot_map on exactly these three plus TProjectNode: PlanNodes.thrift lines 800, 842, 1291, 1296).
- [should-fix] SOT experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/descriptor_table.rs:slot_global_index (lines 314-324) reached from expr_translator.rs:translate_slot_ref: Because a non-PROJECT node's common slots are never materialized, a conjunct referencing one falls through to slot_global_index, whose cross-tuple by-slot-id fallback returns a single same-id column from a different tuple -- a wrong column read with no error, which is exactly what the PR's guard prevents.
- [nit] PR experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:reject_common_slots: The refusal triggers on any non-empty common_slot_map, including the case where every common slot id IS materialized in the node's row_tuples and therefore resolves correctly through the descriptor -- the very case SOT's common_slots_consumed_above (node_translator.rs:499) filters out; the guard should compare against desc.materialized_slot_ids(tuple) and refuse only the non-materialized ids.
- [should-fix] SOT experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/tests/translate.rs:project_common_slots_are_materialized_before_visible_expressions (line 1321): SOT carries the pre-hardening version of this test (it only asserts a Project exists under the visible one); the PR's third commit added the assertions that actually pin the lowering -- hidden.expressions.len()==1, emit_mapping(hidden)==[0,1,2], emit_mapping(visible)==[3] -- and those are absent on SOT.
- [should-fix] SOT tests/translate.rs (missing nested_common_slots_are_appended_in_slot_id_order and common_slots_outside_a_project_are_rejected, plus helpers arithmetic/as_project/struct_field): Nothing on SOT pins the one-project-per-common-slot chaining in ascending slot-id order, so a refactor that folded every common expression into a single ProjectRel would evaluate them all against the scan and pass SOT's suite while breaking a later hidden slot that names an earlier one.
- [nit] Both versions: expr_translator.rs:translate_slot_ref, slot_overrides lookup keyed by exact (tuple_id, slot_id); node_translator.rs:translate_project_node keying on output_tuples[0]: A ref to a common slot that carries a stale or secondary tuple id inside the same project misses the override map (no by-slot-id fallback exists for overrides, only for carried columns on SOT) and falls through to a descriptor error, and a project with several row_tuples only ever binds commons under its first tuple.
- [nit] PR experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_project_node (common-slot loop): The PR's two new expr_context_with_slots call sites do not chain .with_carried(&input.carried_slots), which is required the moment the carried-slot slice lands on top -- a project stacked on a carrying project would then fail to resolve the lower project's carried columns.

Tasks:
- Land PR #1233 unchanged after #1235 (upstream's remaining anti-join draft) merges -- #1111 and #1234 are already in origin/dev and the PR's three pre-image blobs (expr_translator.rs faad31bc, node_translator.rs d053afa9, tests/translate.rs 6836c320) are byte-identical on both origin/dev and upstream-pr/dev, so it applies cleanly with no rebase.

- Verify against a real GB200 CN run that no TPC-H Q1/Q6/Q3 fragment carries a common_slot_map on a SELECT_NODE, HASH_JOIN_NODE or NESTLOOP_JOIN_NODE (PlanNodes.thrift lines 800/842/1296) before the refusal ships, since the refusal turns such a plan into a hard translation failure.

Notes: State check: #1233 is an OPEN DRAFT (the GitHub page shows "Draft"; a WebFetch summary claiming "Merged" is wrong -- origin/dev's node_translator.rs blob d053afa9 is exactly the PR's pre-image, so nothing of it is in dev). 5 commits by mbrobbel; body is one line: "Materialize common project expressions before translating visible output expressions. Depends on #1111." aocsa approved 2026-08-24: "LGTM ... Merge this after #1234 and #1235." #1111 and #1234 are merged; #1235 (anti joins) is still draft.

Classification detail. The PR's three source-side pieces are on SOT essentially verbatim: ExprContext.slot_overrides / with_slot_overrides (expr_translator.rs:41,64), PlanContext::expr_context_with_slots (node_translator.rs), and the translate_project_node common-slot loop (node_translator.rs:2136-2152) -- SOT's loop differs only by the added .with_carried(...) and a comment about append_project never shifting existing columns. SOT then extends the same mechanism far beyond the PR (carried slots to ancestors, join refusal, root confinement). What SOT does NOT have is the PR's fourth and fifth commits: reject_common_slots on the three non-project nodes, the tightened emit-mapping assertions, and the nested-chaining test. So "partially present": the design is not superseded, it is the load-bearing base layer of SOT's design, minus one guard and two tests.

Nothing in the PR blocks Q1/Q6/Q3: on dev today a project with a common_slot_map fails loudly (its slot_map entries name slot ids that no tuple materializes), so the PR is strictly unblocking; the new refusals only fire on shapes that already fail. Conversely the PR alone is not sufficient for q14-style plans where the common slot is consumed above the project -- that needs the SOT slice in task 2.

No silent-data-loss path found in the PR itself. The one silent path found anywhere is on SOT: an unguarded non-PROJECT common_slot_map combined with descriptor_table.rs:slot_global_index's single-candidate cross-tuple by-slot-id fallback (lines 314-318). It requires a common slot id that collides with a materialized slot id in another tuple of the row, which query-global new-optimizer slot ids make unlikely -- hence PLAUSIBLE rather than confirmed, but it is precisely the hole the PR's guard closes, which is why the guard is worth porting back.

######## PR #1235 fix(starrocks): lower anti joins
present_on_sot=partially  recommendation=update-pr-then-merge

SOT changes beyond PR:
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_hash_join - SOT calls refuse_carried_join_child(node,&left) / (node,&right) before the join-op match, refusing a join child that physically carries common-expr columns (join conjuncts resolve via slot_global_index over DESCRIPTOR widths, so a carried child silently shifts every right-side column). Absent from the PR; belongs to a separate carve-out, not to #1235.
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:emit_columns - SOT's signature takes TranslatedRel (not a bare Rel) and sets carried_slots: Vec::new() because an explicit column list re-materializes the row; the PR calls emit_columns(filtered.rel, ...). Pure signature drift from the carried-slots work, no behaviour difference for anti joins.
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:filter_rel - SOT propagates carried_slots: input.carried_slots (a filter keeps the row layout); the PR's filter_rel has no such field. Same drift.
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_hash_join - SOT builds the JoinRel with carried_slots: Vec::new() and an explanatory comment ('Both children were refused above if they carried anything').
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/tests/translate.rs:unsupported_join_type_is_reported_before_missing_conjuncts - SOT re-points the test at TJoinOp::RIGHT_SEMI_JOIN; the PR re-points it at TJoinOp::CROSS_JOIN. RIGHT_SEMI is the better witness (a genuine hash-join op the FE can emit); CROSS_JOIN never reaches translate_hash_join in practice.

Defects:
- [blocking] origin/feat/pin-table-cn experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:1751 translate_hash_join (SOT only - PR #1235 commit 1b68623 is the fix): SOT lowers NULL_AWARE_LEFT_ANTI_JOIN to LeftMark + NOT(marker) unconditionally, with no guard for multi-key or correlated NOT IN, so those queries silently return too few rows.
- [should-fix] origin/feat/pin-table-cn experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/tests/translate.rs:4842 anti_hash_joins_are_lowered (SOT only): SOT's same-named test asserts only that translation succeeds and root(&plan).names.len() == 1, so it would pass unchanged if LEFT_ANTI were lowered as a plain LEFT join with the IS NULL filter dropped - i.e. it cannot detect the exact wrong-answer regression the lowering is at risk of.
- [should-fix] PR #1235 description (github.com/sirius-db/sirius/pull/1235): The body is one sentence plus a stale 'Depends on #1111' (merged as e4112409 on dev), which does not meet CONTRIBUTING.md PR reviewability: no motivation, no statement of why JOIN_TYPE_LEFT_ANTI is not used, no statement of the refusal's rationale.
- [should-fix] both PR and SOT - node_translator.rs:translate_hash_join, JoinOutput::{LeftAnti,RightAnti,NullAwareLeftAnti} arms: All three lowerings are asserted only at the Substrait level; no test executes one end to end, so nothing proves DuckDB's substrait consumer plus the Sirius GPU join actually return correct NOT IN / NOT EXISTS rows.
- [nit] both PR and SOT - node_translator.rs:filter_is_null / translate_hash_join first_equality: The anti-join filter tests IS NULL on only the first equality conjunct's opposite-side expression, which is correct only if that expression is strict (NULL-propagating) under NULL padding.
- [nit] both PR and SOT - node_translator.rs:translate_hash_join eq-conjunct opcode check: The loop rejects an eq conjunct only when eq.opcode is Some(x) with x != EQ, so a conjunct arriving with opcode None is lowered as plain 'equal'; for the new anti paths a null-safe key mistranslated this way turns matched NULL=NULL rows into anti-join output rather than merely producing a wrong join.
- [nit] PR #1235 - node_translator.rs:translate_hash_join, JoinOutput::LeftAnti / RightAnti arms: The unreachable 'has no equality key' branch returns TranslateError::malformed rather than UnsupportedPlanNode, so a hash join that somehow arrived with only other_join_conjuncts would be reported as a malformed plan instead of an unsupported one.
- [nit] PR #1235 - experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/tests/translate.rs (emit_mapping helper move): The PR relocates the emit_mapping helper from ~line 3532 up to ~line 2819 while SOT keeps it at ~line 5580, which guarantees a textual conflict and a duplicate-definition compile error when the SOT translator slice is rebased on top of a merged #1235.

Tasks:
- Rewrite the description of upstream PR sirius-db/sirius#1235 to be self-sufficient per CONTRIBUTING.md: state the motivation (StarRocks FE emits LEFT_ANTI/RIGHT_ANTI/NULL_AWARE_LEFT_ANTI for NOT EXISTS and NOT IN, which the CN currently refuses), state that substrait/src/from_substrait.cpp:TransformJoinOp has no JOIN_TYPE_LEFT_ANTI arm but does map JOIN_TYPE_LEFT_MARK to duckdb::JoinType::MARK (hence outer+IS NULL and mark+NOT), summarise the null-aware refusal and its rationale, and delete the stale 'Depends on #1111' line since #1111 merged as e4112409.
- Take PR #1235 as-is for the DEV..SOT carve-out of the anti-join lowering: do NOT re-derive it from origin/feat/pin-table-cn, because SOT's copy of translate_hash_join in experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs is the same code minus the null-aware refusal guard.
- Port PR #1235's third commit (1b68623) into origin/feat/pin-table-cn: add the guard in experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_hash_join that returns UnsupportedPlanNode with reason "null-aware left anti join with correlated or multi-column keys" when join.join_op is NULL_AWARE_LEFT_ANTI_JOIN and eq_join_conjuncts.len() != 1 or other_join_conjuncts is non-empty, so correlated and tuple NOT IN cannot silently return too few rows on SOT.
- Replace the weak anti_hash_joins_are_lowered test at experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/tests/translate.rs:4842 on origin/feat/pin-table-cn with PR #1235's version, which asserts the join r#type (Left/Right/LeftMark), the filter's extension-function name (is_null/is_null/not) and the emit mapping (vec![0]/vec![1]/vec![0]) for each of LEFT_ANTI_JOIN, RIGHT_ANTI_JOIN and NULL_AWARE_LEFT_ANTI_JOIN.
- When rebasing the SOT translator slice onto a merged #1235, reconcile the two mechanical divergences in experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs -- SOT's emit_columns and filter_rel take/return TranslatedRel and thread carried_slots while #1235's take a bare Rel -- and in tests/translate.rs delete the duplicate emit_mapping helper and the duplicate anti_hash_joins_are_lowered definition.
- Add an end-to-end correctness test that runs TPC-H Q16 (single-key NOT IN, exercises the LeftMark path), Q21 and Q22 (NOT EXISTS, exercises the LEFT_ANTI outer+IS NULL path) through the StarRocks CN and diffs the result against the StarRocks BE, since experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/tests/translate.rs only asserts the Substrait shape and nothing currently proves src/op/sirius_physical_hash_join.cpp:resolve_mark_join_result produces the right rows for these plans.
- Harden experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:filter_is_null so the anti-join null test does not depend on the first equality conjunct's opposite-side expression being strict -- either AND is_null over every equality key on that side, or refuse LEFT_ANTI/RIGHT_ANTI whose chosen key expression is not a plain field selection or a cast of one.
- Tighten the equality-conjunct opcode check in experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_hash_join so that eq.opcode == None is either refused or explicitly handled, because a null-safe (EQ_FOR_NULL) conjunct arriving without an opcode is currently lowered as plain 'equal' and, on the new LEFT_ANTI/RIGHT_ANTI paths, turns matched NULL=NULL rows into anti-join output.

Notes: Verdict: PR #1235 is strictly ahead of the source of truth and should be merged (after a description rewrite), not superseded.

What the PR contains, and where SOT stands on each of its three commits:
1. 93b5d61 "lower anti joins" - FULLY PRESENT on SOT. origin/feat/pin-table-cn node_translator.rs:1722-1870 has the identical JoinOutput enum {Both, Left, LeftAnti, RightAnti, NullAwareLeftAnti}, the identical TJoinOp arms (LEFT_ANTI -> JoinType::Left + LeftAnti, RIGHT_ANTI -> JoinType::Right + RightAnti, NULL_AWARE_LEFT_ANTI -> JoinType::LeftMark + NullAwareLeftAnti), the identical first_equality capture, the identical row_tuples/output_width match (NullAwareLeftAnti = left.output_width + 1 for the appended marker), and the identical filter_rel / filter_is_null helpers. The only differences are the carried_slots plumbing (emit_columns takes TranslatedRel on SOT vs Rel in the PR) and comment wording.
2. ff93806 "assert the anti-join lowerings, not just that they translate" - ABSENT on SOT. SOT has a test with the same name, anti_hash_joins_are_lowered at tests/translate.rs:4842, but its body only does assert_eq!(root(&translated.plan).names.len(), 1). The PR's version walks root -> Project(emit) -> Filter -> Join and asserts the join type, the filter's extension-function name and the emit mapping per join op. The PR's test is the one worth keeping.
3. 1b68623 "refuse null-aware anti joins that need per-row null-awareness" - ABSENT on SOT. Neither node_translator.rs nor tests/translate.rs on origin/feat/pin-table-cn contains the string "null-aware"; grep confirms no guard and no null_aware_anti_join_with_extra_predicates_is_rejected test. This is the one place where the PR fixes a live silent-wrong-results bug that SOT still has.

Design validation (I checked the actual consumers rather than taking the PR comments on faith):
- substrait/src/from_substrait.cpp:TransformJoinOp lines 542-569 handle INNER, LEFT, RIGHT, LEFT_SINGLE, LEFT_SEMI, RIGHT_SEMI, LEFT_MARK and OUTER, and throw NotImplementedException on everything else. So there is genuinely no JOIN_TYPE_LEFT_ANTI arm, and JOIN_TYPE_LEFT_MARK does map to duckdb::JoinType::MARK. The PR's choice of lowering forms is correct.
- duckdb/src/planner/binder/tableref/bind_joinref.cpp:340-348 removes the right bindings for a MARK JoinRef and adds a generic BOOLEAN __mark_index_column binding, and duckdb/src/planner/operator/logical_join.cpp:ResolveTypes appends exactly one BOOLEAN for MARK. Combined with from_substrait.cpp:205 emitting PositionalReferenceExpression(field + 1), field_selection(left.output_width) does land on the marker. The lowering's index arithmetic is right.
- src/op/sirius_physical_hash_join.cpp:is_join_type_supported accepts MARK, and resolve_mark_join_result (line 1758) takes both build_has_null and probe_keys, so the engine does implement the three-valued mark. The PR's premise for commit 3 is confirmed: _build_has_null is a single global flag set from table_has_any_null(build_keys) at lines 1921/2070/2235, i.e. exactly the global-NULL rewrite that makes LeftMark + NOT(marker) unsound once a second predicate can make a row definitely non-matching.

TPC-H impact: none of the priority shapes are affected either way. Q1 and Q6 have no joins at all and Q3 is inner joins only, so #1235 neither helps nor blocks the multi-CN Q1/Q6 push. It unblocks Q21 and Q22 (NOT EXISTS -> LEFT_ANTI, where correlated predicates in other_join_conjuncts are still allowed and still correct) and Q16 (single-key NOT IN -> the still-supported null-aware form). The new refusal does not reject any TPC-H shape.

Merge ordering: the PR's base blob for node_translator.rs is d053afa9fb, which is byte-identical to the blob at that path on upstream-pr/dev, so #1235 still applies cleanly to current upstream dev with no rebase. Merging it first is the cheapest order - the conflict cost then falls on the SOT carve-out, which has to drop its weaker duplicate test and adapt emit_columns/filter_rel to the carried_slots signatures anyway.

######## PR #1236 fix(starrocks): lower decimal operations to fp64
present_on_sot=fully  recommendation=update-pr-then-merge

SOT changes beyond PR:
- expr_translator.rs:aggregate_call — SOT takes a `merge: bool` and skips the FP64 argument cast on merge-phase measures ('a merge measure's argument is already the FP64 partial-state column; the decimal ret_type describes the original input'); the PR keys the cast on `is_decimal(&function.ret_type)` alone and would re-cast a partial-state column once two-phase lands
- expr_translator.rs:AggregateCall::raw_arguments — SOT keeps the pre-lowering arguments so a caller expanding one FE measure into several Sirius measures (two-phase avg: count the raw values, sum the cast ones) is not stuck with a cast that fits only one of them
- expr_translator.rs:cast_to_fp64 / expr_translator.rs:cast_to — SOT factors the throwing FP64 cast into shared helpers used by aggregate_call, node_translator::expand avg and avg_from_state; the PR open-codes `expression::Cast` twice
- expr_translator.rs:aggregate_call — SOT's refusal reads 'avg is only supported where it lowers to the GPU's FP64 avg (DOUBLE and DECIMAL inputs)'; the PR replaces the old message with 'temporal avg is not supported', which mislabels every non-temporal, non-double, non-decimal avg
- partial_state.rs:wire_columns — SOT models the two-phase wire column of a decimal `sum` (and of every `avg`) as FP64 precisely because of this lowering, so the exchange schema both fragments derive agrees with the emitted plan; the PR lowers the arithmetic with no counterpart model, so a decimal sum's FE-declared DECIMAL128(38,s) intermediate slot and the DOUBLE actually shipped disagree
- node_translator.rs:expand_measure (partial avg) — SOT casts avg's sum argument to FP64 for every input type, not just decimals, and counts the *uncast* argument, so an integer avg does not ship a BIGINT sum the receiver reads as DOUBLE
- node_translator.rs:merge_projection — SOT does cast merged measures back to the FE-declared output types (contradicting the PR doc's blanket 'never cast back'), which is what keeps a merged decimal sum/count acceptable at the next hop
- node_translator.rs (aggregation grouping-key check) — SOT compares only the Substrait type-kind discriminant so a lowered decimal key's width/nullability difference is not a false descriptor rejection
- tests/translate.rs:clone_expr_keeps_the_child_lowering — SOT pins that CLONE_EXPR returns its child uncast, preserving the arithmetic FP64 lowering instead of re-narrowing to the declared decimal type (CLONE_EXPR does not exist on upstream dev, so this cannot be ported into #1236)
- tests/translate.rs:two_phase_wire_types_agree_end_to_end and the merge-fragment test at ~line 2549, plus partial_state.rs tests decimal_sum_state_is_fp64_not_decimal / decimal_avg_state_is_the_same_two_columns — end-to-end coverage that a DECIMAL128(38,2) sum slot lowers to FP64 and is declared DOUBLE on the receiver's stream

Defects:
- [should-fix] origin/feat/pin-table-cn: experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/expr_translator.rs:translate_arithmetic and src/lib.rs (module docs): SOT carries the PR's code verbatim but dropped its rationale: the 'decimal arithmetic is evaluated in FP64 … result is approximate and is NOT cast back' comment, the lib.rs 'Decimal arithmetic is not exact' paragraph and the ARITHMETIC_EXPR table annotation are all absent on SOT, so a carve-up taken from SOT would land a silent-precision-loss trade with no stated motivation (fails CONTRIBUTING 'self-sufficient description').
- [should-fix] both #1236 and SOT: expr_translator.rs:translate_arithmetic vs node_translator.rs stream-input schema (type_mapper::duckdb_type_name over FE slot types): The lowering is unconditional but the *slot* mapping is not: decimal arithmetic whose FE-declared result precision is <= 18 (e.g. DECIMAL(15,2) + DECIMAL(15,2) -> DECIMAL(16,2)) yields an FP64 value while the receiver's stream column is still declared DECIMAL(16,2), so the value is either refused at the hop or reinterpreted; only precision > 18 results are self-consistent.
- [should-fix] both #1236 and SOT: type_mapper.rs:map_scalar_type (precision > 18 -> Fp64) reaching ReadRel base_schema via descriptor_table named_struct: The precision > 18 -> FP64 rule applies to scan and group-by slots too, so a parquet column physically DECIMAL(20..38,s) is declared FP64 to the reader and a GROUP BY on a wide decimal becomes float-keyed — silent precision loss beyond 2^53, untested in either tree (TPC-H happens to stay at DECIMAL(15,2)).
- [should-fix] #1236 only: expr_translator.rs:aggregate_call: The decimal sum/avg cast is decided from `function.ret_type` with no notion of aggregation phase; harmless on dev only because node_translator.rs rejects merge/update phases today, but it silently becomes a double-cast of an already-FP64 partial-state column the moment two-phase aggregation lands (SOT's `merge: bool` is exactly this fix).
- [should-fix] both #1236 and SOT: tests/translate.rs (no test) for expr_translator.rs:translate_decimal_literal FP64 arm and type_mapper.rs:map_scalar_type boundary: The new DECIMAL_LITERAL -> Fp64 literal arm and the precision 18/19 mapping boundary have no test in either tree; the only decimal-literal test uses precision 10 and still takes the Decimal arm, so a regression that emits a Decimal literal into an FP64 expression would pass CI.
- [nit] #1236 only: expr_translator.rs:aggregate_call refusal message: Replacing the old avg refusal with 'temporal avg is not supported' names the wrong cause for any non-double, non-decimal avg return type; SOT's wording ('only supported where it lowers to the GPU's FP64 avg (DOUBLE and DECIMAL inputs)') is the accurate one.
- [nit] #1236 and SOT: expr_translator.rs:translate_arithmetic builds `expression::Cast` inline: translate_arithmetic open-codes the throwing FP64 cast instead of using the cast_to_fp64 helper (SOT defines the helper and still open-codes it here), leaving two copies of the failure_behavior/nullability choice that can drift.

Line-by-line result: every code hunk of the PR is present on origin/feat/pin-table-cn verbatim — translate_decimal_literal's Decimal/Fp64/error match, translate_arithmetic's per-child throwing FP64 cast + FP64 output type, type_mapper::fp64_type (identical doc comment) and the precision <= 18 keeps DECIMAL / else FP64 rule, aggregate_call's decimal sum/avg argument cast, and both renamed tests (decimal_arithmetic_is_lowered_to_fp64, decimal_avg_is_lowered_to_fp64) byte-for-byte. SOT then extends it for two-phase aggregation. The only things the PR has that SOT lacks are documentation: the translate_arithmetic rationale comment and the lib.rs caveat paragraph/table annotation.

Q1/Q6/Q3 impact: on dev today, translate_arithmetic refuses every decimal ARITHMETIC_EXPR, which rejects Q1's l_extendedprice * (1 - l_discount) * (1 + l_tax) and Q6's revenue expression outright, and aggregate_call refuses decimal avg (Q1's avg_qty/avg_price/avg_disc). This PR is the change that unblocks those shapes, so it is on the critical path for the multi-GPU Q1/Q6 goal and should land rather than be re-carved. Nothing in the PR blocks a TPC-H shape; the residual risks are precision (approximate revenue, ~1e-14 relative) and the DECIMAL(p<=18)-declared-slot vs FP64-value mismatch at exchange boundaries, which only becomes reachable once the multi-fragment work lands.

Merge order suggestion: land #1236 first (it is the smallest, already-open, correctly-motivated slice), then base the two-phase/partial-state PR on it so the `merge: bool` guard and partial_state::wire_columns arrive as a reviewable delta rather than being buried in the multi-CN squash.

######## PR #1242 feat(starrocks): translate materialized exchanges
present_on_sot=superseded  recommendation=supersede-with-sot-slice

SOT changes beyond PR:
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_exchange - lowers EXCHANGE_NODE to a read of the engine stream view (stream_read_rel -> NamedTable ["sirius_stream_<node_id>"]) instead of a local_files parquet read; nothing is materialized, so the boundary is a pointer move rather than a GPU->file->GPU round trip (SOT test bound_exchange_feeds_aggregate_from_a_stream asserts no file appears anywhere in the plan)
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:stream_read_rel - new helper the PR has no counterpart for; the PR only factors out local_files_rel
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_exchange - merging exchanges are supported (SortRel via sort_fields over the stream read), where the PR rejects them with "merging exchanges are not supported by sequential execution"; SOT test merging_exchange_becomes_sort_over_stream_read pins that the input is still a stream, not a re-scan
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:merge_exchange_overrides + translate_exchange state-override block + src/partial_state.rs - rewrites the FE-declared intermediate slot types on an exchange feeding a merge aggregation to the modeled wire types, and inserts the extra columns a wider state (avg -> sum+count) occupies; the PR takes the descriptor types verbatim and has no notion of partial state (this is what makes TPC-H Q1's avg measures survive a two-phase plan)
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_exchange - records a StreamInputSchema (node_id, stream_view, columns with type_mapper::duckdb_type_name) on the context, surfaced as TranslatedPlan.stream_inputs in lib.rs, so the caller can declare the stream on the engine (a stream has no file to infer a schema from); the PR returns nothing for the caller to declare
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_exchange - the sender-names/row-width arity check lives after the partial-state expansion and compares against the post-expansion output_width; the PR's equivalent check sits in descriptor_table.rs:named_struct_for_tuples and compares against the FE-declared tuple width, which would wrongly reject every expanded (avg) exchange row
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/descriptor_table.rs:named_struct_for_tuples - SOT drops the PR's `names: Option<&[String]>` parameter entirely; names and types both come from the descriptor and the exchange rewrites both afterwards, which keeps the descriptor API single-purpose
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_exchange - sets carried_slots: Vec::new() with a stated reason (streams never carry common-expr columns), part of the SOT-only carried-slot machinery the PR predates
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/lib.rs:TranslatedPlan - adds output_partition_columns (sender-side hash-partition key indices) so a HASH_PARTITIONED exchange has a defined cross-sender parity contract; the PR ignores exchange.partition_type completely
- experimental/starrocks/src/compute_node_service.rs:execute_ready_fragment - builds ExchangeInput { node_id, stream_view: sirius_stream_view_name(node_id), names } from the parked sender streams and fails the query when senders disagree on output names; experimental/starrocks/src/wire_type_parity.rs and engine.rs (stream_inputs declaration) close the loop. PR #1242 adds a public API with no in-tree producer at all
- experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:apply_fetch - the PR's exchange-offset hunk (sort offset .or_else(exchange.offset)) is carried on SOT verbatim; it is the one part of the PR that survives unchanged

Defects:
- [blocking] PR experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_exchange (and lib.rs:ExchangeInput.paths): The PR's whole premise - an exchange reads local parquet files the wrapper materialized - is the design SOT abandoned: it forces a GPU->local-file->GPU round trip per fragment boundary and, because paths are node-local, cannot express a remote sender at all, so it does not scale to one StarRocks CN per GPU.
- [blocking] PR experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_exchange (merging-exchange rejection) and tests/translate.rs:merging_exchange_is_rejected: Rejecting every exchange that carries sort_info blocks the top fragment of TPC-H Q1 and Q3 (the FE puts the cross-fragment ORDER BY on a MERGING-EXCHANGE), so a multi-fragment Q1/Q3 cannot be translated at all; SOT lowers the same node to SortRel over the stream read.
- [should-fix] PR experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_exchange: exchange.partition_type is never inspected and nothing in the PR documents that a HASH_PARTITIONED or broadcast exchange must hand each receiver only its own share, so a wrapper that passes the full path list to every receiver silently multiplies rows instead of erroring.
- [should-fix] PR experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/descriptor_table.rs:named_struct_for_tuples: Threading a `names` override through the descriptor API couples the descriptor to one caller's rewrite and hard-codes the assumption that the sender's row width equals the FE-declared tuple width - an assumption that breaks the moment two-phase avg expands the row (SOT moved the check into translate_exchange for exactly this reason).
- [should-fix] SOT experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/lib.rs:9 and :246: Two doc strings still describe the abandoned design - the module doc says "EXCHANGE_NODE requires an explicit, materialized same-node input" and translate_fragment_with_exchange_inputs is documented as "Translates a fragment with fully materialized inputs" - while the code lowers to a stream view and the supported-surface table one screen below already says "engine stream view".
- [should-fix] SOT experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_exchange: SOT dropped the PR's empty-input guard without replacing it: an ExchangeInput whose stream_view is the empty string emits ReadRel/NamedTable { names: [""] } and fails only later inside the engine's binder, instead of a translate-time error naming the exchange node.
- [should-fix] SOT experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/tests/translate.rs (exchange tests around lines 4147-4390): Three guards the PR covers have no SOT test: the empty input_row_tuples rejection, the sender-names/row-width arity error at node_translator.rs:706, and apply_fetch's exchange-offset branch (the SOT merging test passes offset Some(0), so the branch that turns an exchange offset into a FetchRel with an explicit Count(-1) is never exercised).
- [nit] PR + SOT experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_exchange: Both versions pair sender names with descriptor types purely positionally, so a sender whose root output order differs from the receiver tuple's materialized-slot order binds columns under the wrong names with no error; SOT at least cross-checks senders against each other in compute_node_service.rs:execute_ready_fragment, the PR has nothing.

Tasks:
- Close draft PR #1242 with a comment pointing at the replacement stacked branch and stating why the materialized-parquet exchange was abandoned (GPU->file->GPU round trip, node-local paths cannot express a remote sender, and merging exchanges must translate for TPC-H Q1/Q3).
- Open a translator-only replacement PR carving the SOT exchange slice out of origin/feat/pin-table-cn: experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs (translate_exchange, stream_read_rel, the EXCHANGE_NODE dispatch arm, and apply_fetch's exchange-offset branch), src/descriptor_table.rs (named_struct_for_tuples multi-tuple, no names parameter), src/lib.rs (ExchangeInput{node_id,stream_view,names}, StreamInputSchema, StreamInputColumn, TranslatedPlan.stream_inputs, translate_fragment_with_exchange_inputs), and src/type_mapper.rs (duckdb_type_name), deliberately leaving merge_exchange_overrides/partial_state out so the PR stays one sitting.
- Fix the two stale doc strings on origin/feat/pin-table-cn before slicing: experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/lib.rs line 9 (module doc still says EXCHANGE_NODE needs a "materialized same-node input") and line 246 (translate_fragment_with_exchange_inputs is documented as taking "fully materialized inputs"), so they describe the engine-stream lowering the code actually does.
- Port PR #1242's three guard tests into experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/tests/translate.rs on the SOT branch, rewritten for the stream design: exchange_without_input_row_tuples_is_rejected, exchange_names_must_match_the_row_layout_arity (asserting TranslateError::Descriptor with the "but exchange input has N names" message from node_translator.rs:706), and exchange_offset_becomes_a_fetch (non-zero exchange.offset with limit -1 must yield CountMode::Count(-1) plus OffsetMode::Offset(n)).
- Add an empty-stream_view guard to experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_exchange returning TranslateError::malformed naming the exchange node, replacing the input.paths.is_empty() check PR #1242 had, plus a test asserting the error instead of a NamedTable named "".
- Document (and, if cheap, assert) the positional sender-name contract in experimental/starrocks/crates/starrocks-plan-translator/src/node_translator.rs:translate_exchange - sender column order must match the receiver tuple's materialized-slot order - and reference experimental/starrocks/src/compute_node_service.rs:execute_ready_fragment, which is the only place today that checks senders agree with each other.
- Plan the follow-up PR that lands the partial-state half of the SOT exchange work (node_translator.rs:merge_exchange_overrides, the state-override rewrite inside translate_exchange, src/partial_state.rs, and the merge_avg_exchange_names / merge-aggregation tests), since it is what makes TPC-H Q1's avg measures survive a two-phase multi-CN plan.
- Note in the replacement PR description that #1242's dependency #1111 is already merged on dev (07453497/e4112409) and that #1242's blobs match origin/dev exactly, so nothing about the PR is stale - it is being replaced on design grounds, not staleness.

Notes: Verification basis: PR diff fetched read-only (4 files, +396/-36: descriptor_table.rs 31/11, lib.rs 34/5, node_translator.rs 96/14, tests/translate.rs 235/6). The PR is a draft by mbrobbel against sirius-db dev, labels "! - P2"/starrocks, aocsa requested as reviewer, no review comments posted. Its three commits are the translator change plus two test-only commits (name override observable; five guards + exchange offset). It still applies cleanly: the blob hashes of all four touched files are byte-identical on origin/dev and upstream-pr/dev (node_translator d053afa9fb, descriptor_table 352ffed933, lib.rs 01ae26a010, tests 6836c3208d), and its stated dependency #1111 is merged.

Relationship to SOT: SOT kept the PR's entry points verbatim - PlanTranslator::translate_fragment_with_exchange_inputs, the ExchangeInput struct, the exchange_inputs HashMap<i32,&ExchangeInput> on PlanContext, the EXCHANGE_NODE dispatch arm, expect_children/missing-field/unbound-input guards, and the apply_fetch exchange-offset hunk - but replaced the payload: ExchangeInput.paths: Vec<String> became stream_view: String, and translate_exchange emits ReadRel/NamedTable ["sirius_stream_<node_id>"] via stream_read_rel instead of local_files. So the API survives (the doc comment at lib.rs:246 survives too, and is now wrong), the design does not. The PR's local_files_rel extraction also survives in spirit on SOT, but SOT's version takes crate::scan_paths::ScanFile and carries byte-range start/length, so it is not a clean cherry-pick.

Test mapping: PR exchange_node_is_rejected -> SOT tests/translate.rs:4147 (same shape, reason string now "exchange node requires a bound same-node input stream"); PR materialized_exchange_feeds_aggregate -> SOT bound_exchange_feeds_aggregate_from_a_stream:4176 (additionally asserts stream_inputs carries node 7, view sirius_stream_7, columns [("id","BIGINT"),("name","VARCHAR")]); PR merging_exchange_is_rejected -> inverted on SOT by merging_exchange_becomes_sort_over_stream_read:4348; PR exchange_with_no_materialized_files_is_rejected -> no SOT counterpart (guard deleted with paths); PR exchange_without_input_row_tuples_is_rejected, exchange_names_must_match_the_row_layout_arity, exchange_offset_becomes_a_fetch -> guards exist on SOT (node_translator.rs translate_exchange and apply_fetch) but no SOT test exercises them; SOT's fetch coverage (offset_without_limit_emits_an_unlimited_count, limit_and_offset_emit_both_modes) goes through sort nodes only.

TPC-H impact: the PR alone cannot run Q1 or Q3 across CNs (merging exchange rejected, and on dev translate_aggregation still refuses anything but need_finalize one-phase aggregation, so a two-phase Q1 never reaches the exchange either). Q6 has no exchange above a trivial UNPARTITIONED gather, so the PR shape would nominally work for it - at the cost of a parquet round trip per fragment.

## MILESTONE Q1/Q6
MINIMAL SET (seq numbers). Q6 on N CNs, one host, one CN per GPU: #1236 (1) + ffi-transaction-scope (7) + ffi-fragment-rust (8) + byte-range-rule/ingestible/substrait/cn-splits (9-12) + staging-arena-core/staging-ffi/ffi-packed-batch (14-16) + translator-exchange-stream (18) + translator-two-phase-agg (19) + cn-bringup (22) + cn-files-schema-ranges (23) + cn-fragment-run (24) + cn-exchange-dispatch (25) + cn-exchange-proto-patch (26) + cn-transport-tunables (27) + cn-prpc-client (28) + cn-nixl-transport (29) + bench-cluster-bringup/bench-tpch-harness (31-32). Q1 additionally requires: query-watchdog + zero-task-completion (5-6; with 4 groups and N>4 receivers most merge receivers see an EOS with zero batches — without 6 the query hangs forever), translator-wire-order (20), translator-avg-expansion (21), and cn-nixl-session-warmup (30; Q1's SHUFFLE is all-to-all, the reproduced cold-cluster first-contact deadlock). cardinality-engine (13) is in the chain as an enabler (it is what lets export_packed/PackedBatch/StagedBatch/PTransmitPackedParams ship with `rows` verbatim from SOT), not because Q1/Q6 need a declared cardinality. NOT required for the milestone: hash-join wedge (35), aggregate determinism (36-37), drain overlap (38), pin-table (39-42), carried slots (43), wire-type parity (44), docs (45-46), fabric/two-host (47-48), Arrow import (49). Byte-range splits are required because a single lineitem.parquet cannot fan out otherwise; the FE `files_query_whole_file_ranges` patch (pin path, seq 41) plus a many-files dataset is the only alternative and it comes with the pin slice.

END-TO-END VERIFICATION (all scripts exist on origin/feat/pin-table-cn and land in 31-32):
1. Build on a GPU box (GB200 is aarch64, so this also covers the merge-queue-only arm64 row): `pixi run apply-starrocks-patches && pixi run fe-check && pixi run cn-build` (cn-env.sh supplies NIXL_PREFIX/UCX/NIXL_NO_STUBS_FALLBACK=1). Also `make clang-debug` for the C++ side.
2. Dataset: `bench/common/gen-tpch.sh` at SF1 and SF10 into $TPCH_DATA. Run once with lineitem as ONE file (exercises byte-range splits: expect `FileOrFiles.start/length` in the dumped plan via SIRIUS_CN_DUMP_FRAGMENTS and no `assert_all_consumed` throw) and once with many files (exercises get_file_schema multi-range).
3. Launch: `NUM_CNS=<N> GPU_MEM=<...> STAGING=8GiB SIRIUS_QUERY_WATCHDOG_SECS=60 ./experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/cluster8.sh` (one CN per GPU). Check `nvidia-smi` shows exactly one sirius-starrocks-cn per GPU (the CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES trap); `mysql -P9030 -e 'SHOW COMPUTE NODES'` shows Alive=true for all N and the FE blacklist settles empty (run-abc.sh's guard logic; the CN logs `readiness` / no `blacklist` churn after ~3 s). Smoke on 2 CNs / 1 GPU first with `pixi run cluster2` and the DEMO.md Q6-shaped query: revenue 61567694.9502 (+/-1e-14 rel), count(*)=6001215.
4. Plan shape: `EXPLAIN` q01 must show PLAN FRAGMENT 2 `AGGREGATE (update serialize) STREAMING` -> `EXCHANGE HASH_PARTITIONED: l_returnflag, l_linestatus` -> `AGGREGATE (merge finalize)` + SORT -> `MERGING-EXCHANGE` WITHOUT `SET new_planner_agg_stage = 1`; q06 must show partial -> `EXCHANGE UNPARTITIONED` -> merge.
5. Measure: `MIN_BACKENDS=<N> TPCH_DATA=... QUERY_TIMEOUT=120 ./experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/bench.sh out.csv 3 q01 q06`, then `tools/oracle.py` (DuckDB CPU over the same parquet) and `tools/compare.py` -> 0 mismatches (q01: 4 rows, exact non-numerics, relative-tolerance numerics; q06: 1 row). `analyze.py` exits 0. Repeat with `--cold-restart` so run 0 is a true cold first contact (this is the warmup PR's proof: cold q01 must complete, not time out at 60 s with errorCode=62).
6. Logs, per CN: `nixl bandwidth canary peer=... gbps=` on first contact (same-host cuda_ipc expected ~85-90 GB/s on A100, 145+ on RTX PRO 6000, 300+ on GB200; anything near 0.4 means the pool-memory degradation and the tier must have refused); `transmitted batches via nixl ... bytes=64`-scale payloads (one partial row per CN for q06; ~4 groups x state columns for q01); NO `cross-node exchange ... needs the nixl transport tier`, NO `input stream row count unknown` for exchange inputs, NO watchdog `fail_stalled_query`. Then `experimental/starrocks/scripts/cn-distribution.py` (seq 33) over the per-CN telemetry: every CN shows non-zero scan rows for q01/q06 — this is the actual proof that the query fanned out across N GPUs rather than landing on a subset.
7. Unit/integration tests that gate each layer: `sirius_unittest "[staging_arena]"` (13 ARENA cases), `"[parquet_byte_range]"` (6), `"[streaming_fragment]"` (FRAG-1..5, the new empty-stream case, FRAG-10), `"[stream_bind_catalog]"` (CAT-10), the FFI transaction case; on a GPU box `cargo test -p sirius` (fan-out + packed_hop_matches_relay_hop + zero_row_export + fragment_over_an_empty_input_stream_terminates + byte_range_splits_read_every_row_exactly_once) and `cargo test -p sirius-starrocks-cn --features sirius-engine,nixl-transport` incl. `--ignored nixl_cross_agent_write_between_arena_leases` and engine_executes_local_files_and_sequential_exchange; in CI `cargo test --workspace --no-default-features` (translator exchange/two-phase/wire-order/avg tests, local_exchange, result_store, compute_node_service dispatch + transmit_packed tests, prpc_client, tunable). Optional evidence: seq 34's merge_shape_finalizes_avg(_with_sort) and merge_fragment_over_an_empty_partition.
8. Milestone is DONE when q01 and q06 pass steps 4-6 on N>=4 CNs at SF10 with 0 oracle mismatches, cold and warm, and cn-distribution.py shows all N CNs scanned.
## RISKS
- OPEN #1642 (Jedi18, completion-quiescence work ledger) rewrites task_creator::manager_loop, task_scheduler.{hpp,cpp} and the same `future.get()` try/catch in sirius_engine.cpp that seq 5 and 6 edit — a DESIGN collision, not textual. If #1642 lands first, seq 6's non-drain mark_completed() must be re-expressed in ledger terms; comment on #1642 before opening seq 5/6. Seq 6 is Q1-critical, so this is the highest-risk item on the critical path.
- Experimental CI turns red the moment a CN PR imports the patched proto types: build.rs's guard runs unconditionally and compute_node_service.rs imports PExchangeNixlMd etc. with no feature gate. Seq 26 MUST carry the .github/workflows/experimental.yml patch-apply step, and no handler may land before seq 26. Also the workflow is path-filtered to experimental/**, so src/-only PRs never exercise it.
- Row duplication is silent, not an error, if the translator emits FileOrFiles.start/length into an engine that ignores them (every split reads the whole file). Enforce seq 11 before seq 12 in the stack; do not let seq 12 be cherry-picked. Same class: an unset SIRIUS_EXCHANGE_STAGING_BYTES makes every remote exchange fail loudly (good), but an exported CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES collapses all N CNs onto one GPU while still answering queries (bad) — seq 31 must unset it in cluster8.sh.
- Nothing GPU runs in CI: rust/ tests are `cargo test --no-run`, the CN's engine.rs/agent_tier/warmup compile in NO CI job, and sirius_unittest runs on gpu-2xt4 only for C++. Every PR body must name the GPU box and paste the run. The merge-queue-only matrix (arm64 gcc/clang, vcpkg 240 min, cuda-12) can fail a layer after PR CI is green and block the whole stack above it — build on the GB200 (aarch64) before submitting.
- NIXL acceptance risks that only surface at review time: nixl-sys 1.3 links a non-redistributable NVIDIA library (license/provenance), `default = ["sirius-engine","nixl-transport"]` makes a plain `cargo build` need libnixl, and `ignore = dirty` on the starrocks submodule is an unusual ask (vendoring a Sirius-owned proto is the alternative — decide before seq 26). ArenaRegion::device_id() is hardcoded 0 (correct only under one-CN-per-GPU; assert it).
- Option B exists and is not in SOT: the Q1/Q6 exchange payload is ~64 bytes per CN (one partial row), so a brpc-attachment host-bounce transport (~200 lines: export to lease -> D2H into the transmit_packed attachment -> H2D into the peer's lease -> push_packed) would make Q1/Q6 work without nixl-sys, UCX, the canary or session warmup, and would make the --no-default-features CN functional multi-CN. It loses the GPU-to-GPU path heavier queries need (q14: 948 MB/destination). Keep NIXL as the plan (it is written and hardware-tested), but if seq 29 stalls in review this is the shortest fallback to the milestone.
- Q1's SHUFFLE gives most receivers zero batches; three separate mechanisms must all be right: zero-task completion (seq 6), push_packed/close_input EOS-only paths (seq 16), and the receiver-first rendezvous marking a sender set complete with zero frames (seq 25). Only merge_fragment_over_an_empty_partition (seq 34, GPU-only) tests the composed path — run it on hardware before flipping the milestone.
- Q6's revenue is computed in FP64 after #1236 (~1e-14 relative drift, order-dependent across N CNs); compare.py's relative tolerance handles it, but aggregate determinism (seq 36) is intentionally NOT in the chain because canonicalize_row_order's cost on every float-SUM aggregate is unmeasured and it disables the STRING dict-encode path. Benchmark before landing; never claim it fixes q15.
- Upstream conflicts confirmed by merge-tree: gpu_aggregate_impl.cpp vs #1605 (two markers; seq 36), sirius_scan_manager.cpp vs #1547/#1555 (seq 39's subset block at the reworded comment), sirius_extension.cpp vs #1553/#1205 (seq 40's insertion anchor), sirius_physical_partition.cpp vs #1606 (seq 35: partition_sizing_input gains combined_total_bytes). All post-milestone; the critical chain touches none of the 13 both-sides files except CMakeLists.txt (auto-merges) and the adjacent sirius_engine.cpp/task_creator.cpp hunks.
- gh is not installed on this box and the worktree has no remote pointing at sirius-db/sirius (only origin -> aocsa/sirius). Before any stacked work: `pixi global install gh`, `gh auth login`, `gh extension install github/gh-stack`, CONTRIBUTING's `git remote rename origin $USER && git remote add origin git@github.com:sirius-db/sirius.git` (coordinate — shared clone), and CONFIRM aocsa has push rights on sirius-db/sirius; stacked/ branches are maintainers-only, otherwise everything ships as independent fork PRs. Merge bottom-up manually (Enqueue one PR at a time; never `gh stack merge` / Enqueue stack); titles are Conventional Commits (Validate check); open every PR as a DRAFT until the description is self-sufficient.
- Formatting tripwires specific to this carve: rumdl MD057 fails a docs PR that links to a file landing in a LATER PR (bench-cluster-bringup vs bench-tpch-harness cross-links; RETARGETING.md's six dead links); check-orphan-tests fails if test_stream_lifecycle.cpp is ever included (it is registered nowhere — corroborates dropping it); pre-commit skips experimental/** entirely, so `cargo fmt --check` is the only style gate there; black covers test/tpch_performance/*.py.
- Several SOT tests are hang-class and use under_watchdog/watchdog_guard, which call std::process::exit(101) / rely on SIRIUS_QUERY_WATCHDOG_SECS: a single regression takes down the whole `cargo test` binary. Seq 5 must land before any of them, and the threshold values (20-30 s) may be too low on a slow CI GPU — make them env-configurable.
- Perf cliffs that are correct-but-slow and therefore invisible: cudaMallocAsync memory over cuda_ipc (~220x, caught only by the canary floor), export_packed over-leasing 8 MiB per in-flight batch (arena sizing), and a parked Fragment pinned for the process lifetime when a receiver never arrives (no GC in seq 24; only drop_parked on failure paths).
- mbrobbel coordination: the four drafts touch node_translator.rs/expr_translator.rs/tests/translate.rs which seq 17-21 rewrite heavily (~4700 test lines). If they do not merge within the window, seq 18+ must be rebased to include #1236's hunks (Q1/Q6 do not translate without it) — offer to push the requested updates to his branches rather than wait.
## NARRATIVE
## What Q1 and Q6 actually need (answers to the two framing questions)

I checked the FE plans SOT captured (`experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/tpch/plans/q01.explain.txt`, `q06.explain.txt`) and the SOT authors' own analysis (`notes/2026-08-07-sf1-l4/TPCH-PLAN-ANALYSIS.md:96-110, 206-218`):

- **Q6** is 2 fragments: partial ungrouped SUM on every scan instance, then ONE `EXCHANGE UNPARTITIONED` gather into a single merge instance. Payload: one partial-state row per CN (`bytes=64` in DEMO.md's log). It needs: decimal→FP64 lowering (#1236), byte-range splits (a single lineitem file cannot fan out otherwise), EXCHANGE_NODE→stream read, the partial/merge phase classifier with `count→sum` substitution, and a cross-process hop. It does **not** need hash keys, the empty-partition completion fix, avg expansion, wire-order fixes, cardinality, or session warmup (N→1 gather: the receiver's own sender is a local self-exchange, so no two transport threads block on each other).
- **Q1** is 3 fragments: partial grouped agg → `EXCHANGE HASH_PARTITIONED(l_returnflag, l_linestatus)` → merge-finalize + SORT → `MERGING-EXCHANGE` → root. So Q1 needs everything Q6 needs PLUS: `declare_output_hash_key` + `output_partition_columns` (the SHUFFLE), N receivers of which at least N−4 receive EOS with zero batches (only 4 groups exist) → the zero-task completion fix is mandatory, not optional; the merging exchange lowered to a SortRel; three `avg` measures → sum+count expansion and the finalizing division; GROUP BY/ORDER BY keys whose FE order ≠ tuple slot order → the wire-order fix; and an all-to-all first-contact pattern → the NIXL session warmup (the 100%-reproducible cold-cluster deadlock).

**Does Q6 need the arena + NIXL on one host?** With SOT's design, yes: one CN per GPU means one process per GPU, `ExchangeIdentity` is host+port, so every non-self destination is `Remote`, and the only cross-process data plane SOT has is packed cudf bytes written into a peer's staging lease over nixl/UCX cuda_ipc. There is no host-bounce fallback in SOT. I note in the risks that the Q1/Q6 payload is tiny (~64 B per CN), so a ~200-line brpc-attachment transport would technically suffice and would shorten the chain by ~2.5k lines and the libnixl dependency (Option B) — but it is new code, not source of truth, so the plan keeps NIXL and flags Option B as the fallback if seq 29 stalls.

**Does Q1 need the hash-partitioned exchange or a single receiver?** Hash-partitioned. The FE will not gather a grouped two-phase aggregate to one instance; `new_planner_agg_stage=1` is the current workaround and it is exactly what pins the whole query to one GPU. Retiring that setting is the point of seq 19.

## Ordering logic (Perspective A)

1. **mbrobbel first (seq 1-4).** #1236 is on the critical path (neither Q1 nor Q6 translates without decimal→FP64) and SOT carries it verbatim, so merging it is strictly cheaper than re-carving. #1235/#1233/#1232 are not critical but are approved/ready, apply clean to upstream dev, and all touch the translator files that seq 17-21 rewrite — landing them first turns four future conflicts into zero. Each gets the small updates the reviews found (#1236: rebase + 2 tests + avg message + cast helper; #1235: description; #1233: as-is; #1232: has_more/empty refusal + description). #1242 is closed as superseded by seq 18 (materialized-parquet exchanges cannot express a remote sender and reject the merging exchange Q1's top fragment uses); its three guard tests are ported.

2. **Engine-side, conflict-free, parallel (seq 5-16).** Nothing upstream touches rust/, src/exec/, src/op/scan/, sirius_ffi.*, substrait_scan_ranges or the new arena files, so these can be opened simultaneously. The liveness pair (watchdog → zero-task completion) goes first because every hang-class test that follows needs `watchdog_guard`, and zero-task completion is Q1-critical. The FFI stack (transaction fix → Rust bindings) is the bottom of every CN slice. Byte-range L1-L4 is its own stack; L4 (Rust translator) deliberately lives in the C++ stack so it can never merge before the engine half. The arena stack comes last of this group because `ffi-packed-batch` needs four other PRs merged first.

3. **Translator (seq 17-21).** Split by concern so each is one sitting: slot-id fallback (80) → exchange→stream (1300, both queries) → two-phase phases (1400, Q6 done here) → wire order (700, Q1) → avg expansion (700, Q1). The atomic replacement of both legacy guards stays inside seq 19.

4. **CN (seq 22-30).** The two skeptics independently proved that `engine_settings.rs`/`EngineSettings`, `brpc.rs`, `main.rs` and `GPU_ENGINE_TEST_LOCK` are hard dependencies every map deferred as "operability". Confirmed against `cluster8.sh` (it passes `--gpu-device/--gpu-memory-limit/--host-memory-limit`, none of which exist on DEV) and the blacklisting fact (2 of 4 CNs dropped without the readiness listener). So `cn-bringup` is layer 1 of the CN stack, followed by the FILES() multi-range schema fix, then the runtime split 5a/5b (nixl-free, Remote = loud error), then the transport in the order proto-patch (with the CI step) → tunables → prpc client → nixl core (blocking drain loop) → warmup. The drain-overlap perf layer is post-milestone.

5. **Harness (seq 31-32), then the milestone.** cluster8.sh with the CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES fix, bench.sh with the Alive-column fix and the DuckDB oracle wired in (bench.sh has no correctness gate today: a 1-row answer scores as a fast WIN). run-abc.sh stays out of tree (box-entangled). cn-distribution.py (seq 33) is the fan-out proof.

6. **Post-milestone in priority order:** hash-join wedge (22/22 TPC-H), aggregate determinism (benchmark first), drain overlap, pin-table stack (the Q1/Q6 speedup, replaces #1686), carried slots, wire-type parity, docs, fabric/two-host, Arrow import (new work; settle `from_arrow` vs `from_arrow_host` first and delete the ABI shim).

## How the skeptics' blocking findings are folded

- ffi-fragment-rust: the hang-class test moves to seq 16 (after seq 6 lands); its C++ twin ships WITH the fix in seq 6 using the DEV fragment_fixture; `let mut ctx`→`let ctx`; push_packed doc links rewritten; the C++ regression test gets its 3 include dirs (contradicting the map's "leave CMakeLists alone" — no upstream overlap).
- staging-arena: the rows/cardinality leak is resolved by ORDER, not by stripping — cardinality-engine (C++) lands before the packed PR, so SOT's tests compile verbatim; the byte-range test helper is present because seq 11 landed; write_users_parquet/stream_read_plan/rows()/result_to_arrow come from seq 8 (re-cut from SOT, not from the stale #1598 branch).
- nixl: GPU_ENGINE_TEST_LOCK and EngineSettings come from cn-bringup; `as_halves` is mapped into seq 29; `rows` is already there; prpc_client's tests adapt to the 2-arg `with_executor` at their position; the workflow step is in seq 26; DEMO.md's `cluster2` references are satisfied because seq 29 carries the pixi task.
- two-phase L5: engine_settings/brpc/main/file_schema/build.rs are all explicitly placed (seq 22, 23, 25, 26); local_exchange ships whole; the watchdog_guard dependency of FRAG-10 is satisfied by seq 5/6 ordering.
- hash-join: throw placed in `get_next_task_input_data_for_build_probe`; direct unit test of the zero-byte strategy; two operators.md sentences; strays dropped.
- pin-table: RefCell/&self already landed (seq 8), so L2's bodies compile and L3's engine_thread borrow pattern works.
- byte-range: has_byte_ranges test added; line-level carve of the fused can_serve_with_columns hunk.
- Arrow: reframed as new work, off the critical path; shim deleted.

## Sizes and reviewability

Critical chain ≈ 28 PRs / ~21k changed lines (of the ~25k code lines in DEV..SOT). Three PRs exceed ~1500 lines and each says why in its body: cn-exchange-dispatch (ServiceCore shared by dispatch + failure propagation), cn-nixl-transport (one wire contract; the 360-line Cargo.lock is generated), bench-tpch-harness (780 lines of it are the 22 SQL files). Everything else is ≤ 1500.

Install gh via `pixi global install gh` (not in pixi.toml), `gh extension install github/gh-stack`, rename remotes per CONTRIBUTING (the worktree currently has NO sirius-db remote), confirm maintainer push rights (stacked/ is maintainers-only; else ship as fork PRs), close #1686/#1674/#1672/#1644/#1598/#1636-#1639/#1242 with pointers, open every new PR as a draft, merge strictly bottom-up one Enqueue at a time, `gh stack sync --prune` between merges.
