## MILESTONE Q1/Q6
MINIMAL SET (22 PRs) after which TPC-H Q1 and Q6 run across N StarRocks CNs, one per GPU, over byte-range splits and a nixl exchange, with no `SET new_planner_agg_stage = 1`:

Engine C++ (all standalone or bottom-of-stack, wave 0-1): F1 ffi-embedder-bringup-fixes; S1 scan-byte-range-rule; S2 scan-byte-range-ingestible; X exec-exchange-staging-arena; K exec-stream-cardinality (only because F5 folds declare_input_cardinality in — if K stalls, F5 ships without the two Fragment methods and Q1/Q6 are unaffected); L1 pipeline-zero-task-liveness (REQUIRED for Q1 whenever N > number of (returnflag, linestatus) groups: the CNs that receive zero hash keys close their merge input stream empty and the query hangs forever without it).
FFI stack: F2 ffi-fragment-rust; F3 ffi-fragment-fanout-tests (needed only as the base of F4 in the stack — its tests are not on the path); F4 ffi-substrait-byte-ranges; F5 ffi-exchange-staging.
Translator: #1236 (decimal→FP64: Q6's sum(l_extendedprice*(1-l_discount)) and all four Q1 sums plus three avgs do not translate without it); T1 translator-exchange-stream-read; T2 translator-two-phase-agg (Q6 stops here); T3 translator-avg-expansion-wire-order (Q1 needs avg expansion and the GROUP BY/ORDER BY tuple order); T5 translator-byte-range-splits (HOLD until F4 merged).
CN: C3 cn-exchange-proto-patch (with the experimental.yml step); C4 cn-transport-tunables; C1 cn-cluster-bringup (--gpu-device/--gpu-memory-limit/readiness — without it 8 CNs cannot be launched or stay Alive); C2p result store; C2a park/relay; C2b exchange dispatch; C5 prpc client; C6a nixl agent tier; C6b nixl exchange; C7 nixl session warmup (Q1's hash-partitioned merge is an all-to-all first contact; the cold-cluster deadlock reproduced 100% on 4 CNs without it — Q6's single gather may survive without C7 but do not ship the milestone on that).

NOT on the path (and deliberately excluded from the milestone so they cannot delay it): A agg-deterministic-sums (correctness stability across arrival orders, not runnability — but land it before quoting numbers), L2 join wedge, S3/F6/C9 pinned path (an alternative route to the same Q1/Q6 result via files_query_whole_file_ranges; pursue after), C8 drain overlap, T4 carried slots, #1235/#1233/#1232, F7/F8/C10, all docs/bench PRs, arrow work.

VERIFY (state this verbatim in C6b's and C7's descriptions, since CI runs none of it): (1) On a multi-GPU box, `NUM_CNS=<gpus> experimental/starrocks/benchmarks/cluster8.sh` from the SOT branch (D2a not yet merged — run the launcher out-of-tree), confirm `SHOW COMPUTE NODES` shows N Alive with `awk -F'\t' '$9=="true"'`, confirm `nvidia-smi` shows one CN process per GPU (the CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES trap). (2) Do NOT set new_planner_agg_stage; run tpch/queries/q01.sql and q06.sql with __TPCH_DATA__ substituted, against a multi-file lineitem so the FE cuts byte-range splits. (3) Correctness: `oracle.py` (DuckDB read_parquet of the same SQL) + `compare.py` must report 0 mismatches for both; additionally assert Q6 `count(*) = 6001215` (SF1) or the SF's row count as the exactly-once check, and Q1's four groups with `count_order` summing to the lineitem row count. (4) Fan-out proof: each CN log shows `transmitted batches via nixl` (Q6: bytes=64 per CN — one partial-state row) and a `nixl bandwidth canary ... gbps=` line well above the 2.0 floor on first contact; `scripts/cn-distribution.py` over the per-CN telemetry shows non-zero scan bytes on every CN (not 50/0/0/50). (5) Liveness: run Q1 with N > 4 CNs and confirm completion (this is the L1 proof; before L1 it hangs until the FE's query_timeout). (6) Cold start: restart the cluster and run Q1 first — must complete in seconds, not fail at ~121 s with errorCode=62 (C7 proof). (7) Record box, driver, date, SF and paste the log excerpts into the PR bodies; the Rust GPU tests were compile-checked only by CI.

- gh is not installed on this box and the worktree has no remote pointing at sirius-db/sirius (`origin` is aocsa/sirius). CONTRIBUTING requires `git remote rename origin $USER && git remote add origin git@github.com:sirius-db/sirius.git` because gh stack only operates on `origin` — this rewrites remote config in a shared clone; coordinate before running it. Install via `pixi global install gh` (do not add to the repo's pixi.toml) + `gh extension install github/gh-stack`. Also CONFIRM aocsa has push rights on sirius-db/sirius; stacked/ branches are maintainer-only, otherwise every multi-layer stack degrades to independent fork PRs and the file-ownership scheme is what keeps that survivable.
- Upstream #1642 (Jedi18, completion quiescence work ledger, OPEN) rewrites manager_loop, task_scheduler.{hpp,cpp} and the same 5-line try/catch in sirius_engine::execute that L1 changes. It is a design collision, not textual. If #1642 lands first, L1's zero-task hunk may be redundant or must be re-expressed via the ledger, and the watchdog must coexist with take_preserved_error. Mitigation: open L1 as a draft in wave 0, comment on #1642 immediately, keep FRAG-6..9 and the F7 Rust tests as the acceptance criteria that survive either design.
- A (agg-deterministic-sums) is the only DEV..SOT change with real conflict markers against upstream (#1605 in gpu_aggregate_impl.cpp, two hunks in the same function) AND it carries an unmeasured full sort+gather on every grouped aggregate that has an FP64 SUM — which, after #1236, is essentially every TPC-H aggregate on the StarRocks path. Also: Experimental is path-filtered on experimental/**, so a PR touching only src/ never exercises it, and one that edits the workflow does.
- The FFI stack is 8-9 layers deep and rust/crates/sirius/src/lib.rs is touched by 7 of them. Any post-review change to F2 (e.g. renaming into_arrow→result_to_arrow after F3 is written) rebases everything above. Decide the name, the &self/RefCell shape and the doc text in F2 BEFORE opening F3; if a reviewer demands changes to F2 late, batch them into one `gh stack rebase`.
- Cross-stack gates are documented, not enforced: T5 must not be enqueued before F4 (silent N-times row duplication), C6b before F5, C6a before C3, F5 before X and K, F4 before S2, C9 before F6+S3, C10 before T2+T3. gh stack cannot express these; put a 'DO NOT MERGE before #NNN' line at the top of each gated PR body and check it off manually at enqueue time.
- Merge-queue-only CI rows (arm64 gcc/clang, vcpkg env 240 min, cuda-12 test) do not run on PRs. A narrowing/format warning that passes x64 PR CI can fail in the queue and block every layer above it in the stack. Run `make clang-debug` locally before submitting C++ layers, especially X (cuda driver API, uint64 arithmetic) and S1/F4.

Every such PR must name the GPU box, driver and pass output in its body, and the three verdict-found compile breaks (GPU_ENGINE_TEST_LOCK, EngineSettings, as_halves) are exactly the class CI would not catch again.

## NARRATIVE
## Principle: partition by file ownership, then order inside each partition by dependency

Perspective B treats the DEV..SOT delta (~73k lines, 90 code files) as a set of shared-file hot spots and asks: which PRs must touch each hot spot, and can every touch of one file be confined to ONE gh stack? Inside a stack, a file being edited by several layers is cheap — `gh stack rebase` moves the whole column, the same author owns every layer, and reviewers see each hunk in isolation. ACROSS stacks, the second PR to touch a file rebases on a moved base, its review comments detach, and if the two stacks belong to different reviewers the file's story is told twice. So the plan makes seven ownership assignments and then never violates them:

| shared file(s) | owning stack | who else may touch it | how the collision is avoided |
|---|---|---|---|
| src/sirius_ffi.{hpp,cpp}, rust/crates/sirius-sys/src/lib.rs, rust/crates/sirius/src/lib.rs | **ffi** (F1..F8) | nobody | K appends `estimated_rows` AFTER `built` so the brace-init at sirius_ffi.cpp:320 keeps compiling; all Fragment FFI methods (cardinality, staging, packed, pin) land in F5/F6; all Rust GPU tests for other stacks' C++ fixes land in F7/F8 |
| sirius_scan_manager.{hpp,cpp}, parquet_gpu_ingestible.{hpp,cpp}, pin_table.* | **scan** (S1..S3) | nobody | byte-range L2 and pin L1 are stacked in SOT order so the fused `can_serve_with_columns` hunk applies verbatim; only this stack negotiates #1547/#1555 |
| task_creator.cpp, task_scheduler.{hpp,cpp}, sirius_engine.cpp, hash_join.*, sirius_physical_partition.cpp | **liveness** (L1, L2) | nobody | zero-task completion + watchdog are ONE PR so #1642 is negotiated once; L2 negotiates #1606 |
| starrocks-plan-translator/** | **translator** (T1..T4) + T5 standalone | mbrobbel's 3 drafts merge FIRST as the base | T5 (byte-range emission) edits only scan_paths.rs, scan_rel/local_files_rel and the scan-group tests — disjoint from T1..T4 — so it is a held standalone rather than a mid-stack layer |
| experimental/starrocks/src/** | **cn** (C1..C10) + C0/C3/C4 standalone | nobody | compute_node_service.rs is rewritten in C2b/C6b/C8/C9 only; engine.rs in C1/C2a/C6b/C9 only; the runtime and nixl work are each split along FILE boundaries (C2a/C2b, C6a/C6b), which costs zero extra touches |
| CMakeLists.txt | many | upstream (+13) | every insertion is in a different list block; verified auto-merge with `git merge-tree`; F1 adds 3 include-dir lines in yet another region |
| docs/super-sirius/README.md, docs/README.md | **D1** | nobody | F2's streaming-sessions row and the pin link both ship in D1 (zero code dependency), so no code PR renumbers the doc index |

Result: 5 real stacks (scan 3, liveness 2, ffi 8-9, translator 4, cn 11), one docs stack (3), and ~11 single-layer PRs that open in parallel on day one. Total ≈ 46 PRs plus 5 mbrobbel dispositions.

## Wave structure (what opens when)

**Wave 0 — open immediately, all against dev, all reviewable in parallel:** D1 docs; F1 embedder fixes; S1 byte-range rule; X staging arena core; K stream cardinality (engine only); L1 zero-task liveness + watchdog; A deterministic sums (after a rebase onto upstream-pr/dev for #1605); #1236 → #1235 → #1233 → #1232 (mbrobbel, description updates); C3 proto patch + workflow step; C4 tunables; C0 FILES() multi-range schema; C1 cluster bring-up. Fourteen PRs, none of which touches another's files; 12 of them have zero upstream overlap.

**Wave 1:** S2 (on S1), F2 (on F1), L2 (on L1, rebased on #1606), T1 (after the three mbrobbel merges), C2p → C2a (on C1; C2a needs F2 merged for relay_from).

**Wave 2:** S3 (on S2, rebased on #1547), F3 (on F2), T2 (on T1), C2b (on C2a).

**Wave 3:** F4 (on F3; gate S2), T3 (on T2), C5 (on C2b; gate C4), F5 (on F4; gate X and K).

**Wave 4:** T5 standalone (HOLD until F4 merged), F6 (on F5, rebased on #1553/#1205), C6a (on C5; gate C3), T4 (on T3).

**Wave 5:** C6b (on C6a; gate F5), F7 (on F6; gate L1, L2, A), F8 (on F7).

**Wave 6:** C7 → C8 → C9 (gate F6, S3) → C10 (gate T2, T3).

**Wave 7:** D2a (gate C7) → D2b → D2c; D3 (gate C9, D2b); D4 optional. AR1/AR2 whenever the Doris design question is answered.

Merging is bottom-up and manual per CONTRIBUTING: enqueue the bottom layer alone, wait, `gh stack sync --prune`, repeat. Because the stacks own disjoint files, five bottom layers can sit in the queue in the same day without conflicting.

## Where "critical path first" would create churn, and the compromise

1. **Staging FFI before byte-range FFI.** Critical path wants F5 (nixl prerequisite) as early as possible and would put it directly on F2. But SOT's `local_files_plan` delegates to `local_files_plan_ranged`, and the arena's zero-row test manufactures its empty batch with a range inside one row group. Landing F5 first means either rewriting that test around a filter (unclear whether Sirius emits a zero-row batch after full pushdown) or landing `local_files_plan_ranged` twice. Compromise: F4 before F5. S1/S2 are ~600 lines of pure new code and clear the queue during F2/F3 review, so the gate costs no wall-clock time.

2. **One giant multi-CN PR.** Critical path would open "runtime + nixl" as one ~5000-line PR to get Q1/Q6 running in one review. Perspective B splits along file boundaries (C2p / C2a / C2b / C6a / C6b) — the split is free in touch count because result_store.rs, {fragment_executor, engine, local_exchange}.rs and {compute_node_service, brpc, main}.rs are disjoint sets, and each piece has its own CI-visible tests. The only cost is C2a keeping an `execute()` shim for one layer.

3. **Skipping cluster bring-up.** engine.rs on SOT calls `SiriusEngine::start(EngineSettings)` and its kept tests use `test_settings()`; the verdict proved C2a does not compile without engine_settings.rs. Critical path would defer the "operability" PR; B makes C1 the stack bottom. It is also what makes 8 CNs launchable at all.

4. **Skipping cardinality.** Not needed for Q1/Q6, so critical path would defer all of 55e63371. But then export_packed ships 3-arg and PTransmitPackedParams `rows = 10` is dead, and a later PR changes a two-PR-old signature. Compromise: K is a 300-line standalone in wave 0 (engine half, no FFI, field appended after `built`), and F5 is born with the `rows` out-param plus the two Fragment methods. If K stalls, F5 still ships `rows` (no engine dep) and the two methods slide to F7.

5. **Hang-class test in the wrong PR.** Critical path puts `fragment_over_an_empty_input_stream_terminates` in F3 as proof of the Rust bindings. Without L1 it hangs and `under_watchdog` exits the whole `cargo test` binary. B defers it and every other Rust proof of a C++ fix (empty-build join, int64 overflow, merge-shape oracle) to F7/F8. Cost: L1/L2/A land with C++-only coverage for a few weeks; state it in their bodies.

6. **FRAG-10 placement.** Both K and L2 append to test_streaming_fragment.cpp and both need `<optional>`; L2 also introduces `watchdog_guard`, which FRAG-10 on SOT uses. Critical path would fold K into the liveness stack. B keeps K standalone (300 lines, zero deps, merges in days), drops the one watchdog line from FRAG-10, and accepts that L2 rebases once over K's EOF append.

7. **#1232 then T5.** Critical path merges the approved #1232 now and rewrites `check_range` again in T5. B notes this is the one place merge-then-supersede is real churn (~60 lines); the compromise is to merge anyway because `add_ranges` is byte-identical to SOT and the approval is already earned — or close it if T5 is genuinely close.

8. **Arrow-in-out "depends on nothing, land it first".** Its map proposed landing push_arrow first because it is dependency-free. That would make it a fifth independent toucher of sirius_ffi.{hpp,cpp} in the busiest region (between relay_from and close_input, exactly where push_packed lands). B parks the helper (new files only) as a standalone and the FFI method at the top of stack F, after the design question is answered.

9. **Transaction fix inside F2.** Folding F1 into F2 saves one PR but makes the bindings PR the only proof of a change to an already-merged path, and F4's lower_substrait hunk would textually depend on an unmerged F2. B keeps F1 at ~130 lines with its own Catch2 case (3 include-dir lines on sirius_unittest) so it merges in days and both F2 and F4 rebase onto a merged shape.

## mbrobbel's drafts

They are not aocsa's to close-and-replace; three of them are strictly ahead of SOT and one is the critical-path decimal lowering. Order: **#1236** first (Q1/Q6 do not translate without it; blobs identical to upstream dev; ask for the two missing tests and SOT's avg wording), **#1235** (SOT lacks its null-aware refusal — a live wrong-results hole on SOT), **#1233** (the base layer of SOT's carried-slot design; T4 back-ports its `reject_common_slots` guard narrowed to non-materialized ids), **#1232** (merge with has_more/empty refusal and doc reword, or close if T5 is imminent). **#1242** is closed: its API survives in T1 verbatim (`translate_fragment_with_exchange_inputs`, `ExchangeInput`) but its materialized-parquet payload was replaced by the stream view, and it rejects the merging exchanges Q1/Q3 need. Port its three guard tests into T1. Stack T opens only after the first three merge so `TranslatedRel.carried_slots` already exists and no constructor is touched twice.

#1686 (umbrella → motivation prose for S3/C9), #1674/#1672 (demo multi-CN → C2b/C6b), #1644 (stream fragment execution, 29 commits → C2a/C2b and the L1 hunk it already carries verbatim), #1598 (Rust bindings → F2; restore its doc comments and 3 tests; do NOT take its uuid lockfile downgrade), #1636/#1637/#1638/#1639 (byte-range stack → S1/S2/F4/T5; content byte-identical, only the base moved). Close each with a link to its replacement when the replacement is opened as a draft.

## CI facts that shaped the split

- `check-orphan-tests` fails Check/lint on any unregistered test_*.cpp — test_stream_lifecycle.cpp is unregistered on SOT, corroborating the global exclusion. Every new test file in S1, X lands with its CMake line in the same PR.
- `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` on rust/ means F2 must flip `let mut ctx` → `let ctx`; Rust tests are compile-checked only, so every GPU-touching PR body names the box and pastes output.
- Experimental is `--no-default-features` and never applies patches: C3 carries the workflow step or every CN PR is red; engine.rs/agent_tier/warmup.rs/wire_type_parity.rs are compiled by nothing in CI.
- pre-commit excludes experimental/** and *.patch; rumdl MD057 (relative links must exist) governs D1/D2/D3 ordering; black covers the two test/tpch_performance Python files.
- PR CI runs a reduced build matrix; run `make clang-debug` locally on C++ layers before submitting.

## Sizes

Wave-0 standalones: 130-930 lines each. Stack layers: 240-1400 except F5 (950), C2b (~1550), D2b (~1900), C1 (~1230), T1/T3 (~1300), C6a/C6b (~1150/1250). The four >1500 candidates each have a pre-planned zero-touch-cost split named in the risks.

## Exclusions (never in any PR)

src/exec/stream_lifecycle.{hpp,cpp}, test/cpp/exec/test_stream_lifecycle.cpp; .pixi; experimental/starrocks/rmm_log.txt; test/cpp/utils/pipeline_conversion_test_utils.hpp (+1 <optional>, noise); benchmarks/pinned/generated/*; tpch/plans/*, tpch/results/*; notes/** (52 files); .claude/skills/* (5 box-specific skills); configs/gb200-*; bench/a100x8, bench/gb200-4gpu, bench/sf500-gb200, bench/rtxpro6000-2gpu/{results,STATUS,TPCH-STATUS,SF500-*,README}; nixl_bench.rs/nixl_echo.rs/two_node_harness.rs/nixl-echo-2node.sh (2291 lines that never compile in CI — propose separately later, if at all); run-abc.sh, io-mode-demo.py, 2NODE-ENGINE-B-*, 8GPU-NVLINK-RUNBOOK.md, host-facts-*.txt.
